The first six-fixture run covers Seed-san, the constraint sample, the UV animation sample, both binary-expression mask samples, and Alicia VRM0. Alpha-mask mismatches are small and under the new tolerance `64`: Seed-san wgpu/Bevy `25/32`, constraint `11/11`, UV animation `0/0`, expression mask samples `12/12`, and Alicia `32/32`. Selected `rgb-visible` PSNR is still low on broader avatars because it includes silhouette/runtime/material deltas: Seed-san wgpu `20.4130 dB`, Bevy `20.3065 dB`; constraint wgpu `25.6531 dB`, Bevy `25.6476 dB`; UV animation wgpu `27.4437 dB`, Bevy `27.4271 dB`; mask samples around `29.84 dB`; Alicia wgpu `20.3469 dB`, Bevy `20.3434 dB`. The recipe uses `--render-fail-under 20` only as a regression floor, not as the final compatibility threshold.
- Tightened exact MToon light/color accumulation by making `--mtoon-light-accumulation three-vrm` ignore glTF occlusion textures for MToon surfaces in both wgpu and Bevy capture shaders. This matches three-vrm's WebGL `MToonMaterial` path, where `aoMap`/`aoMapIntensity` are not ShaderMaterial properties, while leaving PBR fallback materials and the PSNR-tuned capture mode unchanged. The generated MToon light fixture remains a high-signal color audit: selected `rgb-interior1px` is wgpu `58.9968 dB` with max selected channel delta `1`, and Bevy `51.8032 dB` with max selected channel delta `2`.
- Matched the concrete outline expansion formula more closely to three-vrm. The wgpu and Bevy capture paths now expand outline vertices from local/object normals scaled by the inverse-transpose normal-matrix length instead of directly pushing along normalized world normals, and the wgpu vertex shader applies three-vrm's outline `clip.z += 1e-6 * w` nudge. The focused guards remain numerically stable rather than improved: generated screen outline stays wgpu `Infinity` / Bevy `53.2689 dB`, and the real tangentless normal/outline sweep stays Seed-san wgpu `32.8751 dB` / Bevy `32.5273 dB`, constraint wgpu `35.9456 dB` / Bevy `35.9394 dB`, all with the same alpha counts. This removes transform-scale and wgpu clip-depth as likely causes for the remaining real outline residual.
- Added renderer-facing glTF morph-target extraction and default weight retention. `vrm-io` now exposes mesh weights, node weights, and per-primitive morph target position/normal/tangent deltas, and both wgpu and Bevy capture paths apply the active node-over-mesh weights before skinning, normal/tangent transforms, and outline expansion. The generated mesh extraction test now covers morph targets and node/mesh weight extraction. Re-running the real tangentless normal/outline sweep is numerically unchanged (Seed-san wgpu `32.8751 dB` / Bevy `32.5273 dB`, constraint wgpu `35.9456 dB` / Bevy `35.9394 dB`), which indicates this static frame's remaining residual is not missing default morph weights; visual diff remains concentrated around hair/body outline edges.
- Added expression-driven morph render parity. The three-vrm browser reference, wgpu capture, Bevy capture, and local Rust CI runner now accept expression weights (`--expression happy=1.0` / `--render-expression happy=1.0`). Rust resolves VRM expression morph binds into active node morph weights before skinning and outline expansion, matching three-vrm's `expressionManager.setValue()` path. A new source-like generated fixture at `.external-fixtures/generated/morph-expression.vrm.gltf` is exercised by `just render-parity-morph-expression-generated`; it reports selected `rgb-interior1px` PSNR `Infinity` for both wgpu and Bevy with max selected channel delta `0`, while tracking a tiny fill-rule alpha edge mismatch of `3` pixels (tolerance `8`).
- Broadened the generated expression render-parity fixture beyond morph writeback. The same `happy=1.0` path now covers VRM expression material `color` binds, material texture-transform binds over an embedded bufferView PNG, and exact three-vrm binary expression semantics (`weight < 1.0` resolves to `0`). The concrete wgpu and Bevy capture paths apply those effects before MToon shading, texture UV transforms, and outline-color expansion. `just render-parity-morph-expression-generated` now reports selected `rgb-interior1px` PSNR wgpu `58.2703 dB` with max selected channel delta `1`, and Bevy `50.8123 dB` with max selected channel delta `2`; the known edge-only alpha mismatch remains `3` pixels (tolerance `8`).
- Added a swatch-level MToon light/color parity guard. `tools/render-parity/compare-swatch-colors.rs` is a clap-based Rust script that labels connected opaque swatches in RGBA JSON artifacts, drops one-pixel edges, and reports per-swatch PSNR, mean channel delta, max channel delta, and pass/fail. `just render-parity-mtoon-light-generated` now writes `.external-fixtures/render-parity-mtoon-light-generated/reports/mtoon-light_vrm.{wgpu,bevy}.swatches.json` after the aggregate PSNR reports. The current generated light run proves no individual MToon term is hiding behind aggregate PSNR: wgpu passes every swatch with max channel delta `0-1` and per-swatch PSNR `>= 52.9020 dB` where finite; Bevy passes every swatch with max channel delta `0-2`, with the lowest swatch `ambient-ao-ignored` at `47.8009 dB`.
- Matched skinned outline expansion order more closely to three-vrm's vertex shader. The wgpu and Bevy capture paths now keep the existing local/object offset path for unskinned primitives, but skinned outline vertices are offset after the blended skin transform along the skinned normal direction. Re-running `just render-parity-real-normal-maps` stayed numerically unchanged (Seed-san wgpu `32.8751 dB` / Bevy `32.5273 dB`, constraint wgpu `35.9456 dB` / Bevy `35.9394 dB`), and `just render-parity-screen-outline-generated` still passes with wgpu `Infinity` / Bevy `53.2689 dB`. This makes pre/post-skin outline offset ordering unlikely to be the remaining real-fixture outline blocker.
- Added non-white directional-light coverage to the exact MToon light/color guard after a Spark read-only review called out direct/rim accumulation as a remaining risk. `tools/render-parity/three-vrm-browser-capture.mjs`, `tools/ci/local-ci.rs`, `examples/wgpu_render_capture.rs`, and `examples/bevy_render_capture.rs` now forward directional RGB into the reference and both Rust captures, and the wgpu/Bevy MToon shaders multiply direct diffuse plus rim-light mix by that light color. New `just render-parity-mtoon-light-colored-generated` disables ambient and renders the generated swatch fixture with directional color `(1.0, 0.55, 0.25)`: selected `rgb-interior1px` PSNR is wgpu `58.8377 dB` with max selected channel delta `1`, and Bevy `52.0812 dB` with max selected channel delta `2`; per-swatch guards also pass at wgpu `<= 1` / Bevy `<= 2`. The existing white-light guard is unchanged at wgpu `58.9968 dB` / Bevy `51.8032 dB`.
- Added a source-like generated post-correction fixture and a transparent-aware interior PSNR metric. `tools/render-parity/generate-post-correction-fixture.rs` writes `.external-fixtures/generated/mtoon-post-correction.vrm.gltf` with linear-to-sRGB, overbright emissive clamp, emissive-strength clamp, and transparent overbright MToon swatches. `compare-psnr.mjs` and `tools/ci/local-ci.rs` now accept `rgb-visible-interior1px`, which includes opaque and partial-alpha interiors while dropping one-pixel silhouette edges. `just render-parity-mtoon-post-correction-generated` disables direct/ambient light on both sides and passes with selected PSNR wgpu `52.8818 dB` / Bevy `54.1650 dB`, max selected channel delta `1`, and only `144` alpha mismatches beyond one LSB from the known edge fill-rule difference.
- Started the next Sans I/O cleanup pass for render parity. `vrm-io` now exposes `GltfNormalMapPlan` and `GltfNormalMapMode`, a renderer-neutral decision point for authored tangents, generated-tangent fallback, derivative-normal fallback, and material/vertex normal-scale delivery. The wgpu and Bevy capture examples now consume that plan instead of carrying separate tangentless normal-map branching, with unit coverage for authored, generated, derivative, and disabled paths.
- Continued the render-prep Sans I/O cleanup by adding `GltfMaterialRenderExtraPlan` and `GltfMtoonLightAccumulation` to `vrm-io`. The shared plan now owns V0-compat, PBR fallback, three-vrm light-accumulator, derivative-normal, unlit, metallic, roughness, occlusion, and direct-light-scale uniform packing before wgpu/Bevy-specific material creation. Focused generated MToon light and normal guards stayed stable: light fixture wgpu `59.7573 dB` / Bevy `51.1653 dB`, normal fixture wgpu `47.8977 dB` / Bevy `46.7852 dB`.
- Added `GltfPrimitiveData::transformed_vertices` so morph/skin/world-transformed position, normal, tangent, UV, and color data are prepared once in `vrm-io` before renderer-specific buffer creation. wgpu base draws plus Bevy base and outline meshes now consume the shared transformed vertex list. The generated normal and morph-expression parity guards stayed stable: normal wgpu `47.8977 dB` / Bevy `46.7852 dB`, expression wgpu `58.2703 dB` / Bevy `50.8123 dB`.
- Added `GltfOutlineVertexSettings` and `GltfPrimitiveData::outline_vertices`, moving outline width texture sampling, UV transform application, origin selection, and expanded outline vertex positions into `vrm-io`. wgpu and Bevy outline capture paths now consume the shared outline vertex list. The generated screen-outline guard remains stable: wgpu `Infinity` / Bevy `53.2689 dB` on `rgb-opaque`, with the known `188` edge alpha mismatches under the `256` tolerance.
- Rechecked the real Seed-san Bevy `rgb-visible` floor against the current three-vrm reference after the outline helper. `just render-parity-vrm1-samples` still fails on Seed-san Bevy at `33.0100 dB`, and `just render-parity-outline-off` fails at `33.2973 dB`; a detached `7a20788` worktree produced the same `33.0100 dB` Bevy value, so this is a pre-existing current-reference parity blocker rather than the outline helper regression. The next parity slice should raise Bevy Seed-san material/color parity or revise the diagnostic metric only with an explicit rationale.
- Attempted to delegate the 2026-06-01 coverage refresh to `gpt-5.3-codex-spark`, but the Codex thread was still at its agent limit. The main agent ran the documented `cargo llvm-cov` JSON/update flow locally and reviewed the docs diff before keeping it.
- Tightened render-parity review-manifest validation. `validate-review-manifest.rs` now confirms that manifest reference/capture artifacts match the `expected`/`actual` paths embedded in both direct-imqraw numeric reports and RGBA diagnostic reports, and that manifest summary strings mirror the numeric report's selected PSNR, selected max-channel delta, alpha mismatch count, and alpha max delta. A focused 64px Seed-san render-parity smoke passed with the stricter validator.
- Added a renderer-side normal-map scale diagnostic to the wgpu/Bevy capture examples and local render-parity runner. The normal-map parity default remains `1.0`, but `--render-normal-map-scale` now lets focused sweeps test Rust-side normal strength without changing the three-vrm reference. Seed-san 128px checks at `0.75` and `1.25` both worsened PSNR, while disabling normal maps on both sides raised the 256px focused run to wgpu `36.1664 dB` / Bevy `35.4455 dB`; the next normal-map parity work should target tangent-frame/sampling behavior rather than a scalar strength adjustment.
- Added `view-derivative` as a second tangentless normal-map diagnostic. `GltfNormalMapMode` now distinguishes world-space derivative and view-space derivative modes, render-extra uniforms carry the view-derivative flag, and the wgpu/Bevy capture shaders can compute a view-space derivative tangent frame before transforming the perturbed normal back into their world-space lighting path. The focused 64px Seed-san smoke compiled on both renderers and preserved exact alpha parity, but stayed below the default generated-tangent path at wgpu `29.5312 dB` / Bevy `29.5376 dB`, so it remains diagnostic rather than the parity default.
- Fixed a Bevy capture regression in generated-tangent normal-map coverage. Bevy previously discarded generated tangents for a whole primitive when any referenced vertex failed tangent generation, which made Seed-san's normal-map-on Bevy output almost identical to normal-map-off. The Bevy mesh path now fills missing generated tangents with `vrm-io::fallback_tangent`, restoring the `just render-parity-real-normal-maps` gate: Seed-san wgpu `34.6181 dB` / Bevy `34.0835 dB`, constraint sample wgpu `36.2443 dB` / Bevy `36.2352 dB`, all with exact alpha parity.
- Added `tools/render-parity/inspect-imqraw-deltas.rs` plus `just imqraw-deltas` / `just render-parity-imqraw-seed-normal-deltas` for direct `.imqraw` hotspot inspection without PNG conversion. The current Seed-san real-normal-map reports show no alpha deltas, but max RGB delta `255` inside visible/interior pixels: wgpu `8151` changed pixels with `6791` interior-nonblack, and Bevy `10340` changed pixels with `8855` interior-nonblack. This narrows the next Seed-san blocker to model-body material/geometry/pose residuals rather than alpha or transparent handling.
- Added a Seed-san-oriented opaque `flat` diagnostic render mode to the three-vrm browser reference and the wgpu/Bevy capture examples, plus `--render-diagnostic-mode` in the local runner and `just render-parity-seed-flat-diagnostic`. The diagnostic keeps geometry, runtime transforms, opaque alpha/cull/depth, outline, and render ordering intact while replacing material/shader RGB with flat white. The 2026-06-10 Seed-san run reports `rgb-nonblack-interior1px = Infinity` for both wgpu and Bevy and `0` `interiorNonblackChangedPixels` in direct imqraw hotspot reports; the remaining flat-mode deltas are only `229` wgpu / `228` Bevy RGB silhouette/raster-edge pixels. This splits the Seed-san blocker into material/shader color parity for the model body and a smaller edge/raster classification task.
- Extended the direct-imqraw hotspot mapper with renderer-facing material pipeline policy, sRGB-decoded diagnostic UV comparison, perspective-correct CPU UV interpolation, cull-policy visibility, draw-order indices, visible-only nearest matches, frontmost visible candidates, and nearest-candidate sample offsets. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-base-uv-hotspots` on the current Seed-san base-UV diagnostic shows Rust's actual UV color matches the mapper's frontmost visible candidate, while the three-vrm expected UV usually maps to a nearby visible candidate one pixel away in mixed directions (`1,0`, `0,-1`, `0,1`, `-1,0`). This reclassifies the active Seed-san base-UV blocker as local triangle-boundary / UV-seam raster selection between the three-vrm WebGL path and Rust CPU-prepared geometry, rather than transparent blending, outline expansion, color-space decoding, or texture-transform state.
- Measured the same Seed-san base-UV diagnostic with `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior2px`; wgpu reports `39.1371 dB` and Bevy `38.9656 dB`, still with max selected-channel delta `166`. The modest improvement after dropping a two-pixel boundary band supports treating the residual as internal UV seam / triangle-boundary selection rather than only outer silhouette fill.
- Added a reusable `summary` block to hotspot reports. The current Seed-san top-32 base-UV summary reports wgpu actual/frontmost triangle matches `31/32`, Bevy actual/frontmost triangle matches `27/32`, and three-vrm expected/frontmost triangle matches only `4/32` for both. Mean frontmost UV distance is `0.0017` wgpu / `0.0039` Bevy for Rust actuals versus `0.3739` for three-vrm expected colors, making future GPU-skinning or raster convention experiments easier to evaluate.
- Added `--sample-center-x/y` to the hotspot mapper and scanned Seed-san base-UV top-32 hotspots over `0.25/0.5/0.75`. The default `0.5,0.5` center best explains Rust actuals, while `0.75,0.50` raises three-vrm expected/frontmost triangle matches to `16/32` for both wgpu and Bevy reports and `0.75,0.75` lowers expected mean UV distance to `0.13`. This points to a measurable subpixel raster-alignment component, but not a complete global offset because the shifted centers worsen actual/frontmost agreement. Next render-side work should add diagnostic screen jitter to the Rust captures and evaluate real PSNR movement.
- Added render-side screen jitter diagnostics to the wgpu/Bevy capture examples and local render-parity runner, then fixed the wgpu projection offset math after pessimistic review. The focused Seed-san base-UV baseline remains wgpu `38.6925 dB` / Bevy `38.5360 dB` on `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px`; jittering Rust captures by `+0.25` pixel on X worsens to wgpu `27.0500 dB` / Bevy `26.9913 dB`, and `-0.25` worsens to wgpu `26.9579 dB` / Bevy `26.9061 dB`. This rejects a global projection/camera offset as the fix for the current UV-seam residual and keeps the next target on local surface-selection or CPU/GPU-prep parity.
- Added screen-space triangle-edge distance diagnostics to `map-render-hotspots.rs`. Re-running the Seed-san base-UV hotspot maps shows every top-32 frontmost candidate is within `0.25px` of a triangle edge for both wgpu and Bevy, with mean edge distance `0.0341px`; this makes boundary ownership the strongest explanation for the current top hotspot set, while broader interior pixels and lower-amplitude deltas remain separate follow-up checks.
- Added a license-safe generated UV-boundary control fixture and `just render-parity-uv-boundary-generated`. It writes `.external-fixtures/generated/uv-boundary.vrm.gltf`, renders `base-uv` with outlines disabled, and passes `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px` at wgpu `66.8378 dB` (max delta `1`) and Bevy `53.0706 dB` (max delta `2`). The hotspot maps match expected/actual colors to the frontmost triangle for all top-32 deltas, so a simple planar UV discontinuity does not reproduce the Seed-san residual; the remaining blocker is more specific to real-model local surface ownership.
- Added `just render-parity-seed-base-uv-hotspots-wide` and mapped the Seed-san base-UV top-256 delta band. The wider pass keeps the boundary signal but weakens the overbroad top-32 claim: wgpu has `173/256` frontmost candidates within `0.25px`, `217/256` within `0.5px`, and `253/256` within `1px`; Bevy has `149/256`, `204/256`, and `246/256`. Mean edge distance is wgpu `0.2257px` and Bevy `0.2924px`, so lower-amplitude deltas remain mostly near-boundary rather than large interior drift.
- Extended `map-render-hotspots.rs` with nearest-edge identity and `frontmost_nearest_edge_counts`. The Seed-san base-UV top-32 pass now shows identical leading edge buckets for wgpu and Bevy, centered on node `145` / mesh `4` / primitive `3` / material `1` triangles `160`, `164`, and `171`, plus node `144` / mesh `3` / material `5` triangle `1548`. The top-256 band spreads across more mesh `3`/`4` base-pass edges, narrowing the next useful investigation to adjacent-triangle/depth-edge ownership rather than global projection or shader color.
- Added edge-adjacency classification to the hotspot mapper. Seed-san top-32 rejects a simple adjacent-triangle ownership explanation: wgpu actual/expected same-triangle matches are `31/4` and edge-neighbor matches are `0/0`; Bevy is `27/4` with `1/0` edge-neighbor. Top-256 has some adjacency (`10/17` wgpu, `39/25` Bevy for actual/expected), but not enough to explain the worst residuals alone, so the next diagnostics should focus on UV/color quantization, interpolation/sample-position, or ambiguity in matching base-UV colors to repeated UV regions.
- Added CPU frontmost base-UV sRGB quantization distances to the hotspot mapper. Seed-san top-32 shows Rust actual is essentially identical to CPU frontmost UV color (wgpu mean/max `0.0313/1`, Bevy `0.7134/1.4142`) while three-vrm expected is far away (`80.0337` mean, `188.0877` max). Top-256 keeps the same shape at wgpu actual/expected `0.6093/11.1972` and Bevy `1.8690/10.9681`. This shifts the next investigation toward three.js `vMapUv` generation for the affected materials rather than Rust-side projection or UV transform self-consistency.
- Added three-vrm diagnostic material metadata to the browser capture artifact and a `node --check` guard for that script in local CI. Re-running Seed-san base-UV shows all 29 mapped diagnostic materials use identity map transforms (`offset=0,0`, `repeat=1,1`, `rotation=0`, `center=0,0`, `channel=0`, `flipY=false`, identity matrix), with two unmapped entries. This rules out ordinary texture-transform mismatch for the current base-UV residual and leaves UV attribute/varying or raster sample behavior as the sharper next target.
- Extended the same browser capture metadata with diagnostic mesh attribute summaries. Seed-san reports 21 diagnostic meshes with populated `uv` attributes and no populated `uv1`/`uv2`, weakening a UV-channel mismatch explanation and leaving shader varying semantics or raster sample-position behavior as the sharper remaining base-UV diagnosis.
- Added `--hotspot-deltas` / `--hotspot-top` browser-side projection diagnostics to `three-vrm-browser-capture.mjs` plus `just render-parity-seed-base-uv-three-hotspots`. The Seed-san top-32 browser projection shows the three.js CPU-frontmost candidate is not the rendered pixel (`0/32` exact, mean expected RGB distance `76.72`), while the nearest same-pixel candidate is much closer (`5/32` exact, `20/32` within RGB distance `16`, mean `26.81`). This shifts the base-UV blocker toward edge/fill rule, subpixel sample-position, or rasterization ownership differences after ruling out material transform, UV channel, and Rust scene projection self-consistency.
- Added `--hotspot-sample-center-x/y` to the three-vrm hotspot projection and parameterized `just render-parity-seed-base-uv-three-hotspots`. A 3x3 sample-center sweep over `0.25/0.5/0.75` found `0.75,0.75` best for hotspot projection (`nearestMean 11.77`, `26/32` within RGB distance `16`, versus `26.81` and `20/32` at `0.5,0.5`). Rendering Rust with global `+0.25,+0.25` screen jitter worsens full base-UV PSNR from wgpu/Bevy `38.14/38.12 dB` to `27.01/27.45 dB`, so this remains a local triangle fill/raster ownership issue rather than a camera offset fix.
- Confirmed the public `imq` HEAD is still `0fdc5263`, so VRM-specific render-parity domains remain local for now. Strengthened both local comparators by reporting per-metric `mae` and adding `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior3px` as a sharper diagnostic for separating persistent shared-edge/raster ownership from actual material color drift.
- Ran Seed-san base-UV through the new `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior3px` metric. Reusing the same raw captures, wgpu improves from `38.6925 dB / MAE 0.1307` at 1px to `40.4952 dB / MAE 0.1040` at 3px, and Bevy improves from `38.5360 dB / MAE 0.4307` to `40.2751 dB / MAE 0.4050`. This supports treating most of the base-UV residual as shared-edge ownership, while the remaining max channel delta `166` marks a few localized samples for follow-up.
- Extended `inspect-imqraw-deltas.rs` with `shared-nonblack-interior3px`, added `just render-parity-seed-base-uv-hotspots-interior3`, and wired comparator/inspector `--help` checks into local CI. The Seed-san 3px hotspot map keeps the same classification: top-32 wgpu/Bevy actual-frontmost mean RGB distance is `0.0754` / `0.9047`, reference-frontmost mean RGB distance is about `49`, and `30/32` hotspots are within `0.25px` of the nearest frontmost edge.
- Added `just render-parity-samples-shared-body3` for the six real shaded fixtures using `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior3px >= 28.5 dB`. The run passes with exact alpha parity; the current floor is the constraint sample at wgpu `28.6412 dB` / Bevy `28.6300 dB`, followed by UV animation wgpu `30.1907 dB` / Bevy `30.1538 dB`, Seed-san wgpu `31.9233 dB` / Bevy `31.2229 dB`, Alicia wgpu `33.1953 dB` / Bevy `33.1425 dB`, and expression mask samples above `50 dB`.
- Added `just render-parity-constraint-shared-body3-diagnostics` and measured the current floor branch. Disabling normal maps is flat (`28.6428/28.6321 dB` wgpu/Bevy), while disabling outlines improves the constraint sample to `31.0592/31.0407 dB`. The top-64 shared-body hotspots are the same between wgpu and Bevy and concentrate on material `12`, `6`, `7`, `9`, and `3`, so the next constraint-sample parity work should focus on real outline expansion/color ownership rather than tangentless normal maps.
- Fixed renderer-neutral skinning in `vrm-io`: `skin_vertex` now returns the weighted joint-matrix position and normal instead of the original local vertex when skin matrices are active. Added a non-identity weighted skinning regression test. Re-running the static constraint shared-body3 capture is unchanged (`28.6412/28.6300 dB` wgpu/Bevy), which confirms the current floor is still outline/material ownership in the rest-pose fixture, while posed/VRMA/skinned render paths now use the corrected vertex data.
- Extended `tools/render-parity/map-render-hotspots.rs` with base/outline pass ownership summary fields and added `just render-parity-constraint-shared-body3-pass-summary`. With expanded outline geometry, the constraint top-64 hotspots report frontmost visible candidates as `57` base / `7` outline, nearest actual-color candidates as `58` base / `6` outline, and nearest expected-color candidates as `61` base / `3` outline for both wgpu and Bevy. Pass matches are `51/64` actual-vs-frontmost and `56/64` expected-vs-frontmost, with `62-63/64` hotspots within `0.25px` of the nearest edge. A trial outline-normal inversion branch slightly worsened the constraint score (`28.6388/28.6281 dB`), so it was not retained.
- Extended the same hotspot summary with frontmost-to-nearest surface transition counts. The constraint top-64 run is dominated by base-material seam transitions rather than outline: `material_6 -> material_12` (`13` actual / `12` expected), `material_12 -> material_4` (`6` / `6`), `material_12 -> material_7` (`4` / `6`), `material_9 -> material_12` (`4` / `5`), and `material_12 -> material_3` (`4` / `5`). The only high-count outline transition is `outline material_0 -> base material_0` at `3` pixels, so the next constraint pass should classify fill-rule/material seam ownership around those base-material boundaries.
- Added a license-safe generated material seam fixture and `just render-parity-material-seam-generated`. `tools/render-parity/generate-material-seam-fixture.rs` writes `.external-fixtures/generated/material-seam.vrm.gltf` with adjacent opaque MToon primitives and a high-contrast diagonal material boundary. The focused `base-factor`/outline-disabled run passes with exact alpha parity and selected `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px` PSNR wgpu `42.1555 dB` / Bevy `42.1555 dB`; direct `.imqraw` deltas show only `7` changed shared-nonblack interior pixels, and hotspot maps classify all top deltas as base-pass seam pixels within `0.25px` of the diagonal `material_2 -> material_3` edge. This gives the constraint-sample base-material transition blocker a small reproducible guard before changing real-model material/fill-rule behavior.
- Added a license-safe generated base-texture UV-boundary fixture and `just render-parity-texture-boundary-generated`. `tools/render-parity/generate-texture-boundary-fixture.rs` writes `.external-fixtures/generated/texture-boundary.vrm.gltf` with the same planar UV-discontinuity topology as the UV-boundary control plus an embedded PNG base-color texture. The focused `base-color`/outline-disabled guard passes with exact alpha parity, selected `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px` PSNR wgpu `52.9232 dB` / Bevy `49.5132 dB`, and max selected-channel deltas `7` / `6`; top-32 geometry ownership agrees with the frontmost base-pass triangle/material. This narrows the Seed-san base-texture blocker away from generic embedded PNG, sampler, or simple generated UV-discontinuity handling and back toward real-model local primitive/texture lookup behavior.
- Added a license-safe generated per-material base texture selection fixture and `just render-parity-texture-selection-generated`. `tools/render-parity/generate-texture-selection-fixture.rs` writes `.external-fixtures/generated/texture-selection.vrm.gltf` with four opaque MToon primitives, four materials, and four distinct embedded PNG base textures. The focused `base-color`/outline-disabled guard passes with exact alpha parity, selected `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px` PSNR wgpu `58.0021 dB` / Bevy `52.5998 dB`, and max selected-channel deltas `1` / `2`; top-32 hotspot maps match the frontmost base-pass triangle/material for expected and actual pixels. This makes broad material texture binding-table mistakes unlikely as the Seed-san base-texture blocker.

- Added a concrete Vulkan-shaped MToon materialization example for non-Bevy engine adapters. `examples/ash_mtoon_pipeline_materialization.rs` starts from the public `mtoon_renderer_material_plans` API and maps base/outline MToon passes into descriptor-set layout bindings, combined image sampler writes, push constants, rasterization/depth/blend pipeline keys, shader module names, material uniform blocks, and sorted draw queues without taking an `ash` dependency. The local CI smoke list now runs this example so the ash/Vulkan adapter guidance is checked by the normal gate.
- Extended owner-id diagnostics with per-transition pixel bounds/sample pixels and per-triangle screen projection metadata from all three renderers. `tools/render-parity/compare-owner-id-images.rs` now reports `bounds` plus up to eight `sample_pixels` for each top owner transition, while three-vrm, wgpu, and Bevy owner records include `screenBounds` and average NDC `depth`. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-owner-id-diagnostic D:/git/three-vrm` shows the leading Seed-san transition is still `arm_plastic (Outline)` / `robo_arm_2` triangle `406` to Rust base-pass `material_6` on `robo_arm` triangle `406`, but their screen bounds overlap to float precision (`x=186.98..210.80`, `y=57.80..65.74`) while depth differs (`0.94899` three-vrm outline versus `0.97450` Rust base). This narrows the current owner blocker to pass/depth ownership rather than mesh projection, texture binding, or missing owner metadata.
- Added owner-label render policy and facing diagnostics. Owner records now carry draw index, render order, material side/cull mode, alpha/depth/blend policy, normalized `webglDepth`, depth range, screen signed area, and a screen-area front-facing flag. The same Seed-san top owner transition now shows `webglDepth` matches after normalizing Rust's 0..1 NDC depth to WebGL's -1..1 range (`0.9489916` vs `0.9489918`), so the prior depth-looking delta was only a projection convention mismatch. The triangle is back-facing under the three.js screen-area convention (`screenSignedArea ~= -185.816`, `frontFacing=false`); three-vrm renders the BackSide outline owner, while Rust still reports the base owner. The next parity slice should therefore target cull/facing/pass ownership rather than depth math.
- Added a capture-level front-face diagnostic knob for the concrete wgpu and Bevy renderers plus local CI summary/review-manifest metadata. The default remains `ccw`, preserving the normal shaded render path; `just render-parity-seed-owner-id-front-face-cw-diagnostic D:/git/three-vrm` is now available to isolate cull/facing ownership in owner-ID mode. On Seed-san, the CW diagnostic changes the top pass transitions from the default `outline -> base` dominance to mostly same-pass outline ownership: wgpu `outline -> outline = 11769`, `base -> base = 707`, `base -> outline = 148`, `outline -> base = 23`; Bevy `outline -> outline = 11773`, `base -> base = 707`, `base -> outline = 148`, `outline -> base = 19`. This improves owner-ID PSNR only modestly to wgpu `16.9160 dB` / Bevy `16.9249 dB`. A shaded Seed-san trial with CW failed badly at wgpu `14.8325 dB`, while the restored default CCW real normal-map gate passes at Seed-san wgpu `34.6538 dB` / Bevy `34.1163 dB` and constraint wgpu `36.2518 dB` / Bevy `36.2349 dB`, so CW is retained as a diagnostic lever rather than a renderer default.
- Extended `compare-owner-id-images.rs` with `top_render_policy_transitions`, grouping mismatched shared-owner pixels by expected pass/material side/front-facing/depth-write and actual pass/cull/front-face/front-facing/depth-write. Recomputing the Seed-san owner reports makes the cull/facing signal explicit: the default CCW wgpu report is dominated by `outline/back/frontFacing=false -> base/cull=back/frontFace=ccw/frontFacing=false` (`11770` pixels), while the CW diagnostic is dominated by `outline/back/frontFacing=false -> outline/cull=front/frontFace=cw/frontFacing=false` (`11765` wgpu pixels, `10882 + 887` Bevy pixels split by Bevy's front-facing metadata). This gives the next pass a direct aggregate target for winding/facing policy without reading individual owner pairs.
- Added Rust-side `gpuFrontFacing` and `visibleByCullPolicy` owner metadata to separate the old three.js-style screen-area label from the concrete capture pipeline's cull estimate. Re-running the owner diagnostics shows the default wgpu dominant bucket is `outline/back/frontFacing=false -> base/back/ccw/frontFacing=false/gpuFrontFacing=true/visibleByCullPolicy=true` (`11770` pixels), while the CW bucket is `outline/back/frontFacing=false -> outline/front/cw/frontFacing=false/gpuFrontFacing=false/visibleByCullPolicy=true` (`11765` pixels). That confirms the top wgpu owner switch is a facing-policy/winding convention issue, not a contradiction where a culled triangle somehow won. Bevy follows the same pass/cull/front-face shape but still has smaller buckets where the metadata cull estimate says not visible (`2265` default CCW pixels and `887` CW pixels), so the next Bevy-specific diagnostic should verify whether those are projection/fill-estimate artifacts or a Bevy mesh/material specialization difference.
- Switched the Bevy owner metadata projection to Bevy's actual `PerspectiveProjection::get_clip_from_view()` path, including reverse-Z infinite perspective, and labeled its depth range as `bevy-reverse-zero-to-one-ndc`. Re-running both owner diagnostics leaves the Bevy cull-visibility split unchanged (`2265` default CCW pixels and `887` CW pixels in the not-visible bucket), so that split is not caused by the previous finite-projection depth approximation. The useful conclusion is that wgpu remains the cleaner facing-policy probe, while the Bevy residual should be treated as a Bevy owner metadata/fill/decode diagnostic until a smaller fixture reproduces it.
- Extended `compare-owner-id-images.rs` with actual-side cull-visibility aggregate fields so Bevy's residual no longer has to be read out of the top render-policy buckets by hand. Recomputing the existing Seed-san owner artifacts shows wgpu has `0` actual-not-visible owner pixels in both default CCW and CW diagnostics. Bevy reports `2436` default CCW actual-not-visible shared owner pixels and `1014` CW pixels; the same counts appear in the mismatched-shared slice, and the wgpu-vs-Bevy reports see `2439` / `1016`. This keeps the main wgpu conclusion unchanged and quantifies the Bevy-specific split as owner metadata/fill/decode work rather than a pipeline cull violation in the cleaner wgpu probe.
- Fixed the three-vrm browser `owner-id` diagnostic so overlapping base/outline material groups no longer share one vertex-color stream. The old path converted the whole geometry to non-indexed form once, then recolored shared vertices for each overlapping material group; Seed-san's outline owner IDs were therefore being drawn during the base material pass. The capture now builds a diagnostic geometry by duplicating all attributes and morph attributes per material group, preserving group-local owner colors. Re-running Seed-san flips the interpretation: default CCW now reports wgpu pass transitions `base -> base = 11911`, `outline -> outline = 18`, `outline -> base = 4`, while the CW diagnostic worsens to `base -> outline = 11913`. The prior "CW fixes owner pass" conclusion was a browser-reference diagnostic artifact, not a Rust winding fix. Bevy still has the separate actual-not-visible metadata bucket (`2436` default / `1014` CW), so keep that as Bevy-specific metadata/fill/decode work.
- Added Bevy-focused owner metadata recovery diagnostics to `compare-owner-id-images.rs`. The report now counts actual owner IDs whose projected `screenBounds` do not contain the pixel, then checks whether a nearby decoded ID (`red/green/blue +/- 1` style deltas) does contain it. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-owner-id-diagnostic D:/git/three-vrm` keeps wgpu clean (`metadataMiss=0`, `actualNotVisible=0`) while Bevy reports `4498` wgpu-vs-Bevy metadata misses, all `4498` recovered by near IDs; Bevy-vs-three-vrm similarly reports `4492/4492` recovered. This reframes the Bevy-only owner bucket as diagnostic ID decode/fill ownership rather than a cull or projection violation, and leaves the main parity target on the three-vrm-vs-Rust pass/material/fill ownership deltas.
- Added owner geometry-class aggregation to `compare-owner-id-images.rs`. Reports now include scalar counts for mismatched pixels that are still the same projected triangle or same/adjacent projected triangle, plus `top_owner_geometry_classes` grouped by pass, normalized mesh name, material relation, triangle relation, and projected bounds/depth relation. Recomputing the Seed-san owner reports shows the wgpu-vs-three-vrm mismatch is mostly explained by owner numbering and renderer-specific material labels rather than surface disagreement: `11649/11933` mismatched shared pixels are the same pass, normalized mesh, same triangle, and depth-close projection, and `11742/11933` are same or adjacent projected triangles. The remaining truly suspicious wgpu bucket is now down to the small non-overlap/different-pass tail instead of the full owner-ID PSNR delta.
- Added `unexplained_owner_tail_mismatched_shared_nonzero` and `top_unexplained_expected_to_actual_details` to the owner comparator. The unexplained tail excludes same/adjacent projected-triangle owner mismatches and Bevy near-ID recovery artifacts, so it exposes only the small set still worth hand-inspecting. Recomputing Seed-san reports leaves the clean wgpu-vs-three-vrm tail at `191/11933` mismatched shared pixels. The largest remaining wgpu tail transition is only `4` pixels (`base` triangle `1510` to Rust `base` triangle `1548`), and the next few buckets are `2-3` pixels each, mostly scanline-thin base-pass surface ownership at y=141 or near the bottom edge. This moves the owner diagnostic from a broad blocker into a focused tail for future raster/fill-rule checks while broader shaded PSNR remains active.
- Added draw/render-order relation classes to `compare-owner-id-images.rs` and normalized owner metadata with `renderPhaseOrder`. three-vrm, wgpu, and Bevy owner records now expose the 0/19+offset MToon phase order beside their raw renderer `renderOrder`, and reports include `top_render_phase_order_transitions`, `top_draw_order_relation_classes`, and `top_draw_order_transitions` for broad pass/order buckets plus exact draw-index/render-order values. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-owner-id-diagnostic D:/git/three-vrm` shows the clean wgpu-vs-three-vrm shared mismatch is dominated by `base -> base`: `11123` pixels have matching normalized MToon phase order. The prior `788` one-phase bucket was a misleading non-MToon/PBR comparison (`MeshStandardMaterial` render order `0` against Rust's PBR fallback queue `2000`), so browser, wgpu, and Bevy owner captures now emit `renderPhaseOrder = null` for non-MToon materials. The remaining non-MToon bucket is therefore `unknown -> unknown`, while the small real outline MToon bucket is `19 -> 20` as expected for Rust's base-plus-one outline phase. Future order work should use `render_phase_order_relation` for MToon-only queue parity and raw `render_order_relation` only as renderer-local context.
- Added `LoadedVrm::material_display_name` and preserved glTF material names in `GltfMaterialData`, then switched the concrete wgpu/Bevy owner metadata and CPU hotspot mapper to use that IO-level helper. This removes the diagnostic-only `material_5`/`material_14` ambiguity and lets Rust reports line up with three-vrm names such as `huku_bake`, `arm_mat`, `body_bake`, and `backpack_nm`. Added `just render-parity-seed-base-color-outline-off-diagnostic`; it runs the Seed-san `base-color` diagnostic with outlines disabled, compares direct `.imqraw` artifacts, and maps shared-body hotspots. The outline-off slice is essentially unchanged from the normal base-color blocker: wgpu `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px = 32.4602 dB`, Bevy `32.4177 dB`, exact alpha parity, top-64 hotspot frontmost pass `base` only, and `58/64` hotspots within `0.25px` of a projected edge. This narrows the Seed-san base-texture residual away from outline expansion and toward real-model local surface/material ownership around dense UV/material boundaries.
- Added outline-disabled owner diagnostics for the focused Seed-san base-color blocker. `just render-parity-seed-owner-id-outline-off-diagnostic` compares three-vrm, wgpu, and Bevy owner IDs with outlines disabled, and `just render-parity-seed-base-color-outline-off-owner-hotspots` projects the same top hotspot set through the browser owner diagnostic. A Rust screen-jitter sweep over `-0.25/0/0.25` confirmed the default `0,0` screen position is best; offsets worsen wgpu/Bevy PSNR, so there is no single global pixel-center shift to apply. The outline-off hotspot projection reports `27/64` rendered owners at the frontmost center sample, `49/64` recovered by subpixel sampling, and `44/64` recovered in the one-pixel neighbor search. The refreshed wgpu-vs-three-vrm outline-off owner report has `11933` mismatched shared owner pixels, but `11631` are the same projected triangle, and the unexplained tail is `209` pixels. `top_unexplained_material_transitions` now groups that tail by pass/mesh/material: the largest buckets are same-material, different-triangle local ownership around `huku_bake` (`wear_4 -> wear`, `99` pixels), `hair` (`hair_1 -> hair`, `20`), outline/base `huku_bake` (`19`), `body_nm` (`14`), `body_bake` (`9`), `arm_mat` (`8`), and `arm_plastic` (`8`). This keeps the next parity target on real-model local primitive/material ownership rather than broad UV transform, sampler, mip, outline, or global raster offset fixes.
- Added a license-safe split-ownership fixture for the Seed-san-style same-material split-mesh blocker. `tools/render-parity/generate-split-ownership-fixture.rs` writes `.external-fixtures/generated/split-ownership.vrm.gltf` with two overlapping MToon meshes named `wear_4` and `wear`, one material named `huku_bake`, and UV islands sampling opposite sides of a high-contrast embedded PNG. `just render-parity-split-ownership-generated` now gates the focused base-color/outline-off path at selected `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px >= 49 dB`, max selected-channel delta `<= 8`, and exact alpha; the current run reports wgpu `51.5385 dB` / Bevy `49.9613 dB`, both with max delta `7`. The first run exposed a Bevy-only equal-depth overlap regression (`24.5771 dB`, max delta `179`) because Bevy's opaque binned phase did not preserve three.js/wgpu source-order tie-breaks. The Bevy capture now separates shader alpha semantics from render phase selection: diagnostic OPAQUE MToon keeps OPAQUE shader behavior and owner metadata, but in diagnostic render modes it is queued through ordered `Transparent3d` with depth write, `GreaterEqual` reverse-Z compare, and a small material/source-order distance offset. Real `BLEND` shaded materials keep the normal Bevy transparent sort without this diagnostic offset. Re-running `just render-parity-split-ownership-owner-generated` leaves wgpu and Bevy identical (`bevy-vs-wgpu mismatched_shared_nonzero = 0`) and both have only the same `3` pixel unexplained owner tail against three-vrm. Regression checks after the change passed `just render-parity-transparent-generated` (wgpu `54.3997 dB`, Bevy `56.8605 dB`, max delta `1`, exact alpha) and `just render-parity-samples-shared-body3` with the existing six-fixture floor unchanged: Seed-san `31.9233/31.2229`, constraint `28.6412/28.6300`, UV animation `30.1907/30.1538`, expression masks `51.6179/50.1671`, and Alicia `33.1953/33.1425` for wgpu/Bevy.
- Added `tools/render-parity/summarize-render-hotspots.rs`, a clap-based Rust script that compresses `map-render-hotspots.rs` reports into compact JSON/Markdown review artifacts. The Seed-san outline-off recipe and split-ownership recipe now write `*.hotspots.summary.{json,md}` beside the full hotspot JSON. On the current Seed-san outline-off summaries, both wgpu and Bevy keep `64/64` frontmost visible candidates, `58/64` hotspots within `0.25px` of an edge, and only `6/64` exact frontmost triangle matches, while Rust actual colors are closer to the CPU frontmost base texture than the three-vrm expected colors for `43/64` hotspots. This makes the remaining base-texture blocker easier to review visually and keeps the next action focused on real-model edge/fill ownership rather than renderer-wide sampler or texture-binding changes.
- Added low-gradient and gradient-complement direct `.imqraw` comparison domains, `rgb-shared-nonblack-flat32-interior1px` and `rgb-shared-nonblack-gradient-interior1px`, to the Rust comparator, legacy JSON comparator, delta inspector, local CI runner, and `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-outline-off-diagnostic`. The flat32 domain keeps shared non-black one-pixel interiors only when both expected and actual 3x3 RGB neighborhoods stay within 32 channel values of the center pixel, so it is a diagnostic for stable texture/material interiors rather than an easier replacement for the normal render gate; the gradient domain is the shared-body interior complement for local texture/material/fill edges. The first Seed-san base-color/outline-off run reports exact alpha parity and raises the focused score from the ordinary shared-body `32.4602` / `32.4177 dB` to flat32-interior wgpu `49.7607 dB` and Bevy `47.5483 dB`; worst remaining flat32 pixels have max channel delta `29`. The gradient complement is sharply worse at wgpu `26.3277 dB` over `2638` pixels and Bevy `26.3322 dB` over `2642` pixels, with max channel deltas `219` / `218`. This confirms that much of the current Seed-san base-texture residual is concentrated near local texture/material/triangle gradients, while non-edge interior texture color is already close.
- Extended the flat32 diagnostic recipe to map gradient-complement deltas through `map-render-hotspots.rs` and summarize them with `summarize-render-hotspots.rs`. The compact summaries now include same-triangle, edge-neighbor, and base-UV distance counts. The gradient hotspot summaries match the focused outline-off blocker: `64/64` frontmost candidates are visible, `58/64` are within `0.25px` of a frontmost edge, every top hotspot is base pass, Rust actual is closer than three-vrm expected to the CPU frontmost base texture for `44/64` hotspots, same-triangle matches are only `6/64`, edge-neighbor matches are `0/64`, and mean base-UV distance from the frontmost candidate is large (`0.6707` / `0.5730` actual/expected on wgpu). This gives the next parity pass a sharper target: real-model high-gradient UV/material ownership around named base materials such as `huku_bake`, `body_bake`, `arm_mat`, `backpack_nm`, and `body_nm`, rather than a simple adjacent-edge fill-rule issue.
- Added `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-gradient-owner-hotspots`, which projects the same gradient top-64 hotspot set through three-vrm's rendered `owner-id` diagnostic. The current owner projection renders non-zero owners for `64/64` gradient hotspots, but only `27/64` match the CPU center-sample frontmost candidate; subpixel recovery reaches `49/64` and all recovered subpixel owners are depth-rank `1`, while one-pixel neighbor recovery reaches `44/64` with mixed offsets. The leading rendered-owner material is `huku_bake` (`24/64`). This reinforces that the remaining base-color blocker is local subpixel/UV/material ownership on real high-gradient regions, not a renderer-wide sampler or texture-binding issue.
- Fixed the three-vrm owner-hotspot diagnostic lookup to map projected candidates against the duplicated owner-id diagnostic geometry as well as the original source triangle numbers. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-gradient-owner-hotspots D:/git/three-vrm` produced the same aggregate counts (`27/64` center frontmost, `49/64` best subpixel), so the current gradient ownership conclusion is not an artifact of stale source-triangle lookup.
- Added `--force-nearest-textures` to the three-vrm browser reference, wgpu capture, Bevy capture, and local render-parity runner, plus `just render-parity-seed-base-color-nearest-diagnostic`. The Seed-san base-color outline-off nearest/no-mip run gives shared-body wgpu `33.5501 dB` / Bevy `33.4815 dB` and gradient-complement wgpu `27.1258 dB` / Bevy `27.1208 dB`, only a small improvement over the normal filtered gradient result. This keeps the remaining blocker focused on local ownership/raster/UV behavior rather than a broad mip/filter policy mismatch.
- Re-tested a global Rust-side screen jitter at `--render-screen-jitter-x 0.25` on the Seed-san base-color outline-off slice. The run worsened shared-body PSNR to wgpu `22.7068 dB` / Bevy `22.3348 dB`, and the wgpu gradient complement dropped to `16.7432 dB`, so the remaining gradient blocker is not a simple global sample-center offset.
- Added `tools/render-parity/generate-subpixel-ownership-fixture.rs` and `just render-parity-subpixel-ownership-generated`, a source-like same-material `huku_bake` fixture with near-subpixel seams and high-contrast base texture islands. The current run passes exact alpha parity and the new local guard at selected shared-body PSNR wgpu `43.8282 dB` / Bevy `43.4195 dB` with max selected-channel deltas `161` / `160`; the gradient slice is wgpu `34.1148 dB` / Bevy `34.1066 dB`. Hotspot summaries keep `32/32` top deltas on the same base material and `28/32` on the same frontmost triangle, so this generated fixture is useful coverage but still does not reproduce the deeper real Seed-san gradient floor.
- Extended `map-render-hotspots.rs` and `summarize-render-hotspots.rs` with CPU frontmost base-texture local RGB gradient diagnostics. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-outline-off-diagnostic D:/git/three-vrm` keeps the selected flat32 score at wgpu `49.7607 dB` / Bevy `47.5483 dB` and shows the gradient-domain top-64 hotspots have low frontmost texture gradients despite large rendered deltas: wgpu mean/max `4.7577` / `25.4951`, Bevy `4.6618` / `25.4951`, and `0/64` hotspots at `>=32`. This narrows the Seed-san base-texture blocker further away from same-surface high-frequency texture sampling and toward real local UV/material/triangle ownership where three-vrm chooses a nearby surface that is not the CPU center frontmost candidate.
- Added `tools/render-parity/generate-transparent-mask-texture-fixture.rs` and `just render-parity-transparent-mask-texture`, a source-like transparent MToon fixture focused on `baseColorTexture` alpha for `MASK` cutoff, baseColorFactor alpha multiplied with texture alpha, and a `BLEND` partial-alpha control. The recipe renders on a transparent background and gates direct `.imqraw` artifacts at selected `rgb-visible >= 51 dB`, max selected-channel delta `<= 3`, and alpha max delta `<= 1`. The current run has exact alpha bucket parity across three-vrm, wgpu, and Bevy (`transparent=40720`, `opaque=13552`, `partial=11264`), no alpha mismatches beyond 1 LSB, selected PSNR wgpu `55.3599 dB` and Bevy `51.2036 dB`. This closes a generated-fixture gap between alpha-mode factor-only cutoff tests and broader BLEND texture-alpha tests.
- Added `examples/wgpu_mtoon_pipeline_materialization.rs`, a dependency-free but wgpu-shaped concrete materialization example parallel to the existing ash/Vulkan version. It maps `MtoonRendererMaterialPlan` into render-pipeline keys, primitive/depth/blend state, WGSL module/entry names, bind-group layout entries, texture/sampler binding pairs, sample-type choices, uniform payloads, and material bind-group recipes. The default local Rust gate now runs it through `cargo run --example wgpu_mtoon_pipeline_materialization`, so concrete non-Bevy wgpu material/pipeline planning stays covered alongside the ash and custom-engine adapter examples.
- Added renderer coordinate policy types to `vrm-adapter`: `ClipDepthMapping` with `ZeroToOneDepth`, `ReverseZeroToOneDepth`, and `NegativeOneToOneDepth`, plus `RendererFrontFace` for y-down screen-area front-face checks. The wgpu and Bevy capture examples now use generic `owner_triangle_projection::<DepthPolicy>` calls instead of hard-coded WebGL-depth formulas, so reverse-Z Bevy diagnostics and zero-to-one wgpu diagnostics can share one comparison convention. `compare-owner-id-images.rs` also now reports `unexplained_projection_gap_summary` for the owner tail. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-owner-id-outline-off-diagnostic D:/git/three-vrm` shows the clean wgpu outline-off tail remains `209` pixels, all with overlapping screen bounds and small mean WebGL-depth delta `0.000238`; after the Bevy reverse-Z metadata fix, Bevy's mean depth delta drops from about `1.88` to `0.00969`. The remaining Seed-san blocker is therefore still real local triangle/material ownership, while the coordinate-system behavior is now explicit and reusable.
- Lifted the owner-ID screen projection math itself into `vrm-adapter` as `project_triangle_to_screen::<DepthPolicy>`, `ScreenProjectionSize`, `ScreenProjectionBounds`, and `ScreenTriangleProjection`. The wgpu and Bevy capture examples now only provide renderer-specific vertices, projection matrices, dimensions, and front-face policy; bounds, y-down signed area, normalized depth, WebGL-reference depth, and GPU-facing flags come from the shared Sans I/O helper. Re-running the Seed-san outline-off owner diagnostic is numerically unchanged (`11933` wgpu mismatched shared owner pixels, `11631` same projected triangle, `209` unexplained tail), so this is a behavior-preserving refactor that makes future wgpu/Bevy/ash coordinate policy changes less ad hoc.
- Added type-level coordinate-space mapping to `vrm-adapter` with `CoordinateSpaceMapping`, `VrmCoordinateSpace` / `GltfCoordinateSpace`, and `FlipZCoordinateSpace` / `LeftHandedZForwardCoordinateSpace`. The mapping covers positions, directions, rotations, `Transform`s, and explicitly affine transform matrices, and the new `project_vrm_triangle_to_screen::<CoordinateSpace, DepthPolicy>` helper composes coordinate-space policy with clip-depth policy while keeping renderer IO out of the math. This makes engine axis conventions configurable through generics instead of hidden global switches.
- Added `CoordinateSpaceTarget` and `coordinate_space_target::<CoordinateSpace, _>(&mut target)` to make coordinate-system policy usable at the full adapter boundary, not only in projection diagnostics. The wrapper delegates non-spatial engine traits and converts local/world transforms, engine-provided affine world matrices, constraint rest rotations, and lookAt rotations between VRM space and engine space. Focused `vrm-adapter` tests cover direct affine matrix basis conversion, reverse-Z projection policy, child traversal forwarding, and a left-handed Z-forward boundary with `FlipZCoordinateSpace`, keeping `vrm-core` / `vrm-runtime` Sans I/O and VRM-native while engine integrations choose axis conventions through generics.
- Marked the coordinate-space policy hot path with `#[inline(always)]`: concrete `CoordinateSpaceMapping` conversions, default transform/affine-matrix helpers, clip-depth conversion helpers, projection helpers, and `CoordinateSpaceTarget` trait delegation now all carry inline hints. The abstraction still uses static dispatch through marker types, so there is no trait-object call in the coordinate wrapper path; the inline hints make the intended low-overhead shape explicit for optimized downstream builds without treating LLVM inlining or zero instruction overhead as a formal guarantee.
- Re-ran the coordinate-policy and capture-example audit with three `gpt-5.4-mini` subagents focused on low-overhead abstraction. Follow-up changes renamed the explicit matrix helpers to `from_vrm_affine_matrix` / `to_vrm_affine_matrix` while preserving deprecated compatibility aliases, added `SceneGraph::visit_children` traversal with Headless/Bevy/coordinate-wrapper overrides for hot paths, moved spring child lookup onto the visitor path, fused wgpu primitive pipeline-index lookup back into GPU primitive construction, reused one Bevy material plan across base/outline/material setup, and removed repeated node-key allocation plus steady-state `_Color` string churn from the custom-engine staging sync.
- Ran three additional pessimistic audit rounds with `gpt-5.4-mini` subagents to check for regressions from that low-overhead work. Round 1 caught two public API hazards, so `CoordinateSpaceMapping` now keeps deprecated `from_vrm_matrix` / `to_vrm_matrix` compatibility aliases and `SceneGraph::visit_children` keeps trait-object compatibility through `Self: Sized`; focused tests cover both. Later rounds found no render/material regression and verified the local gate, coverage snapshot, and low-overhead wording are synchronized with the implementation.
- Extended `compare-owner-id-images.rs` to read per-owner triangle `indices` metadata and classify `shared-edge-indices` / `shared-vertex-indices` separately from raw triangle-number adjacency. Recomputing the Seed-san owner reports without rerendering shrinks the clean default wgpu unexplained owner tail from `191/11933` to `67/11933`, and the outline-off tail from `209` to `85`; the newly explained outline-off bucket includes `198` same-pass/same-normalized-mesh/same-material shared-edge pixels. Bevy remains dominated by projection/depth metadata differences (`same_projected_or_adjacent_triangle_mismatched_shared_nonzero = 0`), so the next Bevy-specific owner blocker is still depth/projection convention rather than topology classification.
- Added near-depth owner diagnostics without weakening the existing close-depth owner classification. `compare-owner-id-images.rs` now reports `same_projected_or_adjacent_triangle_near_depth_mismatched_shared_nonzero` and `within_webgl_depth_001` / `within_webgl_depth_02` in the projection-gap summary. Recomputing Seed-san outline-off reports shows wgpu stays close-depth clean (`11848` same/adjacent/shared-edge projected mismatches, `85` unexplained tail, `79/85` tail pixels within `0.001` WebGL depth), while Bevy has `7303/12140` mismatches that are same/adjacent/shared-edge projected triangles within `0.02` depth and `7637/7637` tail pixels within `0.02`. That isolates Bevy's current owner diagnostic blocker as a small systematic depth convention offset, not topology or screen-bounds disagreement.
- Added finite WebGL-reference depth metadata to the Bevy owner capture while preserving Bevy's actual infinite reverse-Z depth metadata. `bevy_render_capture.rs` now writes `referenceWebglDepth` / `referenceDepthRange` beside `webglDepth` / `depthRange`, and the owner comparator prefers the reference depth when present. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-owner-id-outline-off-diagnostic D:/git/three-vrm` moves Bevy-vs-three-vrm strict same/adjacent/shared-edge projected owner mismatches from `0` to `7292`, with `7303` still within the near-depth bucket, and shrinks the Bevy unexplained owner tail from `7637` to `539`. The remaining Bevy tail is now mostly the same local screen-bounds/topology residue as wgpu instead of a projection convention artifact.
- Added a separate "touching triangle" owner residual class for diagnostics that includes same projected triangle, indexed shared edges, indexed shared vertices, and raw adjacent triangle numbers while keeping the stricter same/adjacent/shared-edge counter intact. Recomputing the Seed-san outline-off reports without rerendering gives wgpu-vs-three-vrm `same_touch = 11853/11933` and `tail_after_touch = 80`, Bevy-vs-three-vrm `same_touch = 7977/12140` and `tail_after_touch = 149`, and Bevy-vs-wgpu `same_touch = 2228/6332` and `tail_after_touch = 99`. This is not a final parity acceptance rule; it is a narrower residual lens for local vertex-touching ownership around dense material/UV boundaries.
- Integrated the attached `vrm-rs-osc-crate` package as `crates/vrm-osc`, a dependency-free OSC 1.0 codec crate for future VMC-style motion streaming. The workspace now has an optional root `osc` feature and facade re-export at `vrm_rs::osc`; tests cover all supported OSC argument variants, nested arrays/bundles, UDP roundtrip, TCP length-prefixed encode/decode, partial stream waiting, OSC time conversion around the 1900/1970 epoch boundary, and the root feature re-export. `docs/osc.md` records the codec scope and keeps VMC semantics as a future layer above the generic OSC packet model.
- Added `examples/headless_vrma_animation.rs`, a renderer-neutral animation sample that loads a VRM avatar plus a separate VRMA clip, samples `VrmAnimation` frames, applies them through `HumanoidPoseRig` and `HeadlessSceneState`, and prints hips/head/lookAt/expression state without committing binary fixtures. `just vrma-animation` runs it against local external fixtures, and the normal local CI smoke now checks `cargo run --example headless_vrma_animation -- --help`.
- Added a practical Bevy scene bridge behind `vrm-adapter-bevy/render` and `viewer` features. `spawn_vrm_scene` now turns `LoadedVrm` into Bevy `Mesh3d` / `StandardMaterial` / `Image` assets, `BevyVrmInstance` owns the headless runtime scene and optional `BevyVrmAnimationClip`, and `BevyVrmScenePlugin` samples VRMA clips then CPU-bakes skinning and morph targets into Bevy meshes each update. `examples/bevy_vrma_viewer.rs` consumes that crate API instead of inlining loader/render logic, `just bevy-vrma-viewer` runs it via `cargo run --release`, and the local CI smoke checks the release-built viewer `--help` path without opening a window.
- Added reusable Bevy viewer camera controls behind the `viewer` feature. `VrmOrbitCamera` and `BevyVrmOrbitCameraPlugin` provide left-drag orbit, right/middle-drag pan, wheel zoom, `F` focus, and `R` reset, with configurable radius limits and a pure state API covered by unit tests. `examples/bevy_vrma_viewer.rs` now spawns this component instead of a fixed camera.
- Removed the repository-specific absolute path defaults from `Justfile` and replaced the broken Windows implicit `sh` assumption with a minimal `windows-shell` setting that invokes `pwsh` by command name rather than absolute path. Recipes now use repo-relative `.external-fixtures/...` paths and portable command bodies, so the common `just ci`, `just render-parity*`, and viewer commands are no longer tied to a local absolute `three-vrm` checkout.
- Added `crates/vrm-adapter-wgpu`, a Bevy-independent wgpu/winit adapter crate with `WgpuVrmViewerOptions`, `run_vrma_viewer`, and a crate-local `vrma_viewer` example. It loads a VRM avatar and optional VRMA clip, samples animation through the shared Sans I/O adapter/runtime path, CPU-bakes skinning and morph targets into wgpu buffers, uploads glTF base-color textures, and provides orbit/pan/zoom/focus/reset camera controls. `just wgpu-vrma-viewer` runs it in release mode through `cargo run --release -p vrm-adapter-wgpu --example vrma_viewer`, and the local Rust CI smoke checks the same `--help` path.
- Added `crates/vrm-adapter-ash`, an ash/Vulkan-shaped adapter crate that keeps unsafe Vulkan setup in downstream apps while providing renderer-ready plans. `AshVrmFramePlanner` evaluates a VRM plus optional VRMA clip through the shared Sans I/O runtime, emits CPU-baked vertices/indices, RGBA texture uploads with `ash::vk` format/extent metadata, material records, and MToon pipeline plans using `ash::vk` topology/cull/depth/blend/descriptor/sampler enums. `just ash-vrma-frame-plan` runs the release-built crate-local example, and the local fixture smoke on Seed-san plus `idle_loop.vrma` produced `21` primitives, `17` materials, `15` texture uploads, and `17` MToon pipeline plans.
- Added an ash renderer-edge implementation example. `ash_renderer_frame_from_plan` now converts `AshVrmFramePlan` into Vulkan-shaped vertex/index buffer uploads, sampled image resource plans, resolved descriptor-set bindings, and sorted draw-call plans. `crates/vrm-adapter-ash/examples/renderer_integration.rs` demonstrates a mock ash renderer allocating buffer/image/pipeline/descriptor handles and recording draws from those plans without crossing into unsafe Vulkan device calls; `just ash-renderer-integration` and the local CI release help smoke cover the entrypoint.
- Added a real ash/Vulkan device materialization example for renderer integration validation. `crates/vrm-adapter-ash/examples/unsafe_device_renderer.rs` keeps `unsafe` limited to an example binary and creates an ash entry, Vulkan instance, physical/logical device, command pool, vertex/index buffers, sampled images, image views, descriptor set layouts, descriptor pool/sets, pipeline layouts, offscreen color/depth attachments, render pass, framebuffer, shader modules, graphics pipelines, a recorded primary command buffer, and an optional queue-submit/readback path from the existing renderer-frame plan. It intentionally still stops before swapchain/presentation. `just ash-unsafe-device-renderer` runs the release-built fixture smoke, and the current local Seed-san + `idle_loop.vrma` run materialized `42` buffers, `15` images, `17` descriptor sets, `17` graphics pipelines, `1` recorded command buffer, and `21` draw plans on a local Vulkan physical device.
- Added the shared MToon GPU ABI/resource-plan slice. `vrm-adapter` now exposes `MtoonGpuUniform`, `MtoonGpuMaterial`, texture binding plans, binding-number helpers, `MTOON_GPU_UNIFORM_SIZE`, and `MTOON_REFERENCE_WGSL`; the facade re-exports the same entry points. The wgpu adapter now has `wgpu_mtoon_resource_plans` for backend-owned pipeline/material setup, the ash adapter carries `MtoonGpuUniform` in `AshMtoonPipelinePlan` and emits per-pipeline uniform uploads in `AshRendererFrame`, and Bevy-facing material plans carry the same shared uniform for custom Bevy material assets. Focused adapter/wgpu/ash tests and the wgpu, ash, and Bevy materialization examples pass.
- Started manual integration of `D:\sanze\Downloads\vrm-rs-extension-kit.zip`. The archive's `tools/apply.py --dry-run` exposed useful audited deltas, but the zip does not contain the referenced `overlay/` sources for its proposed new crates/modules, so the repo now tracks the kit as a requirement backlog rather than applying the script blindly. The first implemented slice covers expression conformance: `vrm-core::Expression` owns VRM-compatible output weight normalization, `OverrideMode::Block` and `Blend` now differ, runtime expression updates share the binary/category multiplier semantics, `ExpressionManager::set_value_checked` reports unknown names, renderer-facing expression effects use the same binary threshold, non-finite weights collapse to `0`, and malformed VRM1 expression JSON now fails strict sans-IO conversion instead of being silently dropped. Focused `vrm-core`, `vrm-runtime`, and `vrm-io` expression tests pass.
- Added the structured diagnostics foundation for the extension-kit load-policy slice. New crate `vrm-diagnostics` provides typed severity, strict/lenient policy, JSON-path locations, displayable reports, and serde roundtrips. `vrm-sans-io::ValidatedAssetBuilder` now has `with_diagnostic_policy` and `build_with_diagnostics`; default strict mode preserves fail-fast malformed VRM1 expression behavior as `BuildError::Diagnostics`, while lenient mode records the diagnostic and skips only the malformed expression entry. The root facade re-exports the diagnostics crate behind the `diagnostics` feature, and the coverage updater now includes `vrm-diagnostics` in generated tables.
- Added the first lossless source-preservation slice for future writer/metadata editing work. `vrm-io::LoadedVrm` now carries `GltfSource`, preserving the original input bytes, raw JSON bytes, parsed root JSON value, root unknown extension/extras accessors, and raw GLB chunks with typed JSON/BIN/unknown chunk classification. Generated tests cover `.gltf` root unknown extension/extras retention and `.glb` JSON/BIN chunk retention without adding binary fixtures to the repository.
- Added the first writer/metadata-editing slice on top of `GltfSource`. `VrmMetadataPatch` edits VRM1 `VRMC_vrm.meta` and VRM0 `VRM.meta` fields without disturbing unknown root data, `GltfSource::to_bytes_with_json` rewrites `.gltf` JSON or `.glb` JSON chunks while preserving BIN/unknown chunks, and `save_with_json_atomic` / `save_original_atomic` write through a same-directory temporary file before renaming. Tests cover VRM1 metadata edits with atomic save/reload, GLB metadata edits with BIN retention, and VRM0 legacy title/author/license mapping.
- Hardened the preserved-source writer path. `GltfWriteOptions` now selects compact or pretty JSON for `.gltf` and `.glb`, `GltfSource` has direct metadata-patch byte/save helpers, GLB parsing rejects mismatched declared lengths and unaligned chunk lengths before accepting a source snapshot, and generated tests verify pretty writer roundtrip, unknown GLB chunk preservation, atomic metadata-patch failure behavior, and root facade re-exports.
- Added the codec/resource registry foundation requested by the extension kit. `vrm-io::resource` now has `ResourceLimits`, `ResourceReader`, `FileResourceReader`, `resolve_resource_uri`, `CodecRegistry`, mesh codec provider traits for Draco/Meshopt, and texture provider traits for KTX2/Basis-style payloads. Data URIs are decoded without extra dependencies, relative file paths reject absolute/prefix/parent traversal, file/data/decode byte limits are enforced, and missing providers return explicit `MissingMeshCodec` / `MissingTextureCodec` errors instead of silently producing empty mesh or texture data. The facade re-exports these APIs, and focused `vrm-io` tests cover data URI, percent decoding, path safety, file size limits, missing codecs, provider dispatch, and decompression-bomb style decoded-size limits.
- Deepened the codec registry texture-provider API for real renderer integration. `CompressedTexturePayload` now records source color space, `TextureDecodeOptions` carries accepted output formats and GPU-compression requirements, `TextureFormatCapabilities` can derive renderer-facing BC7/ETC2/ASTC/RGBA preferences, texture providers can override option-aware decode before committing to a transcode target, and the registry rejects unsupported decoded texture formats after input/output size checks. Focused tests cover desktop/mobile GPU format selection and unsupported-format errors without bundling codec libraries.
- Closed the VRM0 LookAt curve part of the extension-kit known-limitations list. `vrm-core::RangeMap` now carries a `RangeMapCurve` ADT with VRM0 Unity-style two-key Hermite evaluation, `vrm-sans-io` preserves `FirstPersonDegreeMap.curve` instead of reducing VRM0 LookAt maps to linear ranges, and `vrm-runtime` expression LookAt weight calculation delegates to the shared curve-aware evaluator. Focused core, sans-IO, and runtime tests cover the default linear VRM0 curve, a non-linear Hermite curve, preserved conversion, and expression weight output.
- Closed the VMC transport/rate-limit part of the extension-kit known-limitations list without taking socket ownership. `vrm-vmc` now exposes `VmcTransportGate`, `VmcTransportPolicy`, `VmcPacketContext`, sender ids, accepted-packet reports, and transport-specific errors. The gate collects and validates full packets before sink mutation, supports sender allow lists, per-sender minimum packet intervals, packet message-count limits, relative-time rewind and large-step rejection, lenient parse policy forwarding, and all-or-nothing runtime sink application over the existing transaction path. Focused VMC tests cover missing/unauthorized senders, rate limiting, independent sender state, relative-time jump/rewind rejection before sink writes, and lenient parse plus transaction behavior.
- Closed the FullMeta part of the extension-kit known-limitations list without expanding normalized runtime `Meta`. `vrm-io::GltfSource::vrm_full_metadata` now returns `VrmFullMetadata`, an enum carrying typed VRM1 or VRM0 protocol metadata plus the raw metadata JSON from the preserved source. This keeps author/license/permission fields, VRM1 meta extensions/extras, and unknown raw metadata available to applications while avoiding a breaking core model change. Focused IO tests cover VRM1 typed/raw metadata, VRM0 legacy metadata, missing/malformed metadata errors, and root facade re-export.
- Advanced the ash adapter from resource materialization toward a real drawable path. `AshRendererFrame` now includes `AshGraphicsPipelinePlan` entries with Vulkan topology/cull/depth/blend keys, vertex input layout, color format, depth format, and descriptor-set linkage. The mock ash renderer consumes these concrete pipeline plans, and the unsafe ash example now creates an offscreen render pass/framebuffer, embedded minimal shader modules, real `vk::Pipeline` objects, records sorted indexed draws into a primary command buffer, transitions the color target to transfer source, copies it to a host-visible readback buffer, and can submit/wait/read the frame. It can now also write the readback as render-parity `.rgba.json` and direct `.imqraw` artifacts, and `just ash-render-parity-readback` verifies the raw bundle against the JSON bytes. The default local CI now runs a Vulkan-free `--artifact-self-test` that writes and reads back tiny RGBA/imqraw artifacts, while the environment-dependent recipe exercises the real device path. Focused ash tests, `cargo clippy -p vrm-adapter-ash --all-targets -- -D warnings`, release-built unsafe-device help/dry-run smokes, and local 32x32 readback runs passed; the current smoke read back `4096` bytes, `38` nonzero pixels, and checksum `73d3778676cc1d8d`.
- Connected the ash readback artifact path to the render-parity runner as an explicit supplemental capture. `tools/ci/local-ci.rs -- --render-parity --render-ash-readback` now creates `ash/<fixture>.frame000.{rgba.json,imqraw,png}`, verifies the imqraw bytes against the JSON artifact, and records the ash capture in `review-manifest.json` under `supplementalCaptures` without treating it as a wgpu/Bevy PSNR gate. `just render-parity-with-ash-readback` is the convenience entrypoint for this bridge.
- Tightened the ash renderer resource path toward practical MToon rendering. `AshVrmFramePlanner` now uploads every texture referenced by the generated MToon descriptor bindings, not only base-color textures, while preserving base texture lookup for material records. The unsafe ash example now creates host uniform buffers for `MtoonGpuUniform`, texture staging buffers, a fallback white texture, Vulkan samplers, writes uniform and combined-image-sampler descriptor sets, and records texture image layout transitions plus buffer-to-image copies before the offscreen draw pass. The shader is still the minimal color-only smoke shader, so this is not yet visual MToon parity, but the real Vulkan descriptor/resource path is now wired for the next shader step. Focused ash tests, `cargo clippy -p vrm-adapter-ash --all-targets -- -D warnings`, release dry-run/artifact smokes, a local 32x32 Seed-san readback, and imqraw-vs-RGBA verification passed; the current readback produced `4096` bytes, `38` nonzero pixels, and checksum `e55d557357ed0a4d`.
- Added a shader handoff point to the unsafe ash renderer. `--vertex-spv` and `--fragment-spv` now let downstream experiments replace the built-in color-only smoke shader with precompiled SPIR-V while reusing the same MToon uniform buffers, texture images, samplers, descriptor sets, pipeline keys, draw ordering, and readback artifact path. The loader validates SPIR-V word alignment and magic before creating shader modules, and readback artifacts now record whether the frame used `built-in-color-smoke` or `external-spirv`. This avoids committing shader binaries while making the next MToon-compatible Ash shader iteration testable against the existing raw image pipeline.
- Added the first source-controlled ash shader handoff fixture without vendoring binaries. The initial `mtoon_smoke.{vert,frag}.glsl` source used the shared uniform ABI and fixed texture slot bindings, while `AshMtoonPipelinePlan` descriptor sets exposed stable binding numbers for every MToon texture slot with fallback textures for missing inputs. The initial compile/readback path wrote local SPIR-V under `target/`, ran through the unsafe Ash external-SPIR-V path, and verified the `.imqraw` bytes against the `.rgba.json` artifact. The local 32x32 Seed-san external-SPIR-V smoke read back `4096` bytes, `20` nonzero pixels, checksum `023adf91a4abcf4c`, and `shaderSource = external-spirv`.
- Advanced the source-controlled ash shader handoff from color-only smoke to a first MToon base shader. `AshVrmVertex` now carries normal and tangent attributes at locations `3` and `4` while preserving existing position/UV/color locations for older shaders. `crates/vrm-adapter-ash/shaders/mtoon_base.{vert,frag}.glsl` consumes the shared `MtoonGpuUniform`, fixed MToon texture slots, UV animation mask, shade/shading-shift/normal/matcap/rim/outline textures, alpha mode, and outline-pass flag. `tools/ash/compile-ash-mtoon-base-shaders.rs` and `just ash-mtoon-base-readback` replace the source shader entrypoint; the old `ash-mtoon-smoke-*` recipes remain aliases. Focused tests, clippy, GLSL compile, and local Ash external-SPIR-V readback passed; the 32x32 Seed-san base-shader readback produced `4096` bytes, `20` nonzero pixels, checksum `82bd92ebdb4d9cfb`, and an imqraw/RGBA match. This is still supplemental rather than parity-gated because Ash lacks the full camera/light/material-extra uniform surface used by the wgpu and Bevy render captures.
- Added the first Ash scene/camera/light uniform surface. `AshSceneUniform` mirrors the wgpu capture's view-projection, view, world-from-view, light direction/color, camera position, and MToon lighting accumulator shape; `ash_renderer_frame_from_plan` now resolves material uniform binding `0` and scene uniform binding `9` to distinct uniform uploads. The unsafe Ash example writes the binding-specific uniform buffers instead of assuming every uniform descriptor points at the material pipeline buffer. The source-controlled GLSL now projects with `scene.view_projection` and uses the scene light/camera/MToon lighting values for direct, ambient, and rim accumulation. `just ash-mtoon-base-readback` passed after this change; the 32x32 Seed-san external-SPIR-V readback produced `4096` bytes, `128` nonzero pixels, checksum `e2e189a8b47cfb7f`, and an imqraw/RGBA match.
