- Added the Ash material-extra and UV-transform uniform surface. `AshMtoonPipelinePlan` now carries packed `AshMaterialUvUniform` and `AshMaterialExtraUniform` payloads derived from the same `LoadedVrm::material_uv_transforms` and `LoadedVrm::material_shading_plan` APIs used by the wgpu/Bevy render captures. `ash_renderer_frame_from_plan` emits material, UV, render-extra, and scene uniform uploads with explicit `AshUniformScope` variants and descriptor bindings `0`, `10`, `11`, and `9`, respectively. The source-controlled GLSL now samples base, shade, shading-shift, normal, matcap, rim, and UV-animation-mask textures through the packed transform data and applies the render-extra direct-light scale. Focused Ash tests, clippy, GLSL compile, and `just ash-mtoon-base-readback` passed; the 32x32 Seed-san external-SPIR-V readback remained `4096` bytes, `128` nonzero pixels, checksum `e2e189a8b47cfb7f`, with an imqraw/RGBA match.
- Moved the Ash source-controlled MToon shader closer to the wgpu/Bevy capture accumulator. `mtoon_base.frag.glsl` now applies linear-to-sRGB output correction, view-space matcap UV generation, back-face normal/TBN flipping through `gl_FrontFacing`, unlit and PBR fallback branches from `AshMaterialExtraUniform`, v0-compatible direct-light clamping, rim plus matcap composition, and render-extra direct-light scaling. The shader-contract test now checks for these branches, GLSL compilation passed, and `just ash-mtoon-base-readback` produced `4096` bytes, `128` nonzero pixels, checksum `dbb8bb1c2294b29e`, with an imqraw/RGBA match. Ash is still supplemental because it still lacks inclusion in the full PSNR review gate.
- Added the Ash glTF emissive/occlusion descriptor-slot slice. `AshMtoonPipelinePlan` descriptor sets now use `LoadedVrm::material_texture_slots` instead of only MToon texture refs, preserving the source-controlled outline-width binding while adding glTF emissive and occlusion combined-image-sampler bindings. The Ash MToon shader now samples emissive texture color through `material_uv.emissive_transform` and applies occlusion texture strength to PBR and MToon ambient in the same shape as the wgpu/Bevy capture shaders. Focused Ash tests, clippy, GLSL compile, and `just ash-mtoon-base-readback` passed; the 32x32 Seed-san external-SPIR-V readback stayed `4096` bytes, `128` nonzero pixels, checksum `dbb8bb1c2294b29e`, with an imqraw/RGBA match.
- Added the Ash outline geometry/policy routing slice. `AshVrmPrimitive` now records its MToon pass, `AshVrmFramePlanner` emits separate base and outline primitives when a material has an active outline, and the outline vertices are expanded through `GltfPrimitiveData::outline_vertices` with the same outline-width texture and screen/world scale helper used by the wgpu/Bevy capture paths. `ash_renderer_frame_from_plan` now resolves draw calls by `(material, pass)` instead of always choosing the base pipeline, and a focused test locks outline primitives to the outline pipeline/descriptor set. `just ash-mtoon-base-readback` now records `31` draw plans instead of `21`; the 32x32 Seed-san readback produced `4096` bytes, `134` nonzero pixels, checksum `b3c96babf358e0fa`, with an imqraw/RGBA match.
- Promoted Ash readback artifacts into the render-parity review language. `tools/ci/local-ci.rs --render-parity --render-ash-readback` now compiles the source-controlled Ash MToon GLSL handoff to local SPIR-V, passes those shaders into the release-built Vulkan readback example, writes Ash RGBA/imqraw/PNG artifacts, verifies raw bytes, generates Ash direct-imqraw and RGBA diagnostic reports, writes an Ash diff heatmap, includes Ash in `summary.md` and `visual-review.html`, and records it as a non-gating `comparisons[]` manifest entry with `visualParityGate: false`. The unsafe Ash example gained `--clear-alpha`, and the local runner now uses alpha `1.0` for opaque-black captures and `0.0` for transparent captures. A 32x32 Seed-san smoke under `target/render-parity-ash-review-smoke` passed manifest validation with Ash alpha mismatches `0`, selected `rgb-visible` PSNR `11.0627 dB`, max channel delta `255`, checksum `4972ef1dcaaccc8a`, and `31` draw plans. `--render-ash-visual-gate` is now the opt-in path for applying the normal PSNR/max-delta thresholds once Ash color accumulation is ready.
- Aligned the Ash parity camera/light boundary with the wgpu and Bevy capture inputs. `AshSceneOptions` now drives `AshSceneUniform`, outline screen-coordinate scale, camera position, direct-light scale, directional color, and MToon lighting config, while `AshVrmFramePlanOptions` exposes matching `--camera-y`, `--camera-z`, `--target-y`, `--direct-light-scale`, `--directional-{r,g,b}`, and MToon lighting CLI knobs. The unsafe Ash renderer now derives the scene aspect ratio from the readback extent, and local render parity forwards its camera/light arguments to Ash. Ash also applies the Vulkan clip-space Y flip in its scene projection, fixing the previously upside-down review capture. A 128x128 Seed-san Ash review smoke under `target/render-parity-ash-review-128` passed manifest validation with alpha mismatches `0`, selected `rgb-visible` PSNR `12.7715 dB` after the Y flip, and checksum `03b5e353cef9c0a3`; Ash remains non-gating because the next blocker is texture/material color accumulation rather than projection orientation.
- Added the extension-kit optimizer foundation. `vrm-io::optimize_primitive` now provides a renderer-independent preprocessing pass for glTF primitives: it validates parallel attributes and index buffers, removes degenerate triangles and unused vertices, normalizes skin weights, removes empty morph targets, compacts weighted joint palettes, and returns stable vertex/joint remap reports. `apply_joint_compaction_to_skin` applies the palette remap to `GltfSkinData`, the root facade re-exports the optimizer API, and focused tests cover stale unused joint data, invalid attributes/indices, invalid weighted joints, morph removal, and skin palette application.
- Extended structured diagnostics into the IO loader boundary. `vrm-io` now depends on `vrm-diagnostics`, re-exports diagnostic types, and adds policy-aware `load_vrm_from_slice_with_policy` / `load_vrm_from_path_with_policy` APIs returning `LoadedVrmWithDiagnostics`. The legacy loaders remain strict and return `LoadedVrm`, while the new path merges sans-IO diagnostics, unknown root-extension preservation warnings, and existing VRMA/animation warnings into one structured report. Focused tests cover lenient malformed-expression loading with JSON path diagnostics, unknown extension preservation plus warning, IO warning mirroring, and root facade re-exports.
- Added Ash normal-map diagnostic parity with the wgpu/Bevy capture paths. `AshVrmFramePlanOptions` and `AshRenderOptions` now carry `--normal-map-mode generated-tangents|derivative|view-derivative`, `--normal-map-scale`, and `--disable-normal-maps`; `tools/ci/local-ci.rs --render-ash-readback` forwards those flags into the release-built Vulkan example; and the source-controlled Ash GLSL handoff shader implements derivative/view-derivative fallback normals through `dFdx`/`dFdy` plus the existing material-extra view-derivative flag. Focused Ash tests, clippy, GLSL compilation, and 128px Seed-san derivative/view-derivative render diagnostics passed. The diagnostic artifacts `target/render-parity-ash-review-128-normal-derivative` and `target/render-parity-ash-review-128-normal-view-derivative` both produced Ash selected `rgb-visible` PSNR `15.4683 dB`, slightly worse than the generated-tangent default `15.4876 dB`, so the remaining Ash blocker still points at outline/primitive edge coverage and MToon material accumulation rather than tangent fallback choice.
- Added Ash outline diagnostic parity with the wgpu/Bevy/three-vrm render-parity paths. `AshVrmFramePlanOptions` and `AshRenderOptions` now carry `--disable-outlines` and `--outline-width-scale`; the planner skips outline primitives when requested and scales outline width before calling the shared `GltfPrimitiveData::outline_vertices` helper. The local render-parity runner now forwards outline flags and `--render-mtoon-time` into the release-built Ash readback example. A 128px Seed-san smoke under `target/render-parity-ash-review-128-outline-off-forwarded` passed with exact opaque alpha parity, `21` Ash draw plans instead of the outlined `31`, and Ash selected `rgb-visible` PSNR `16.6624 dB`; this proves the diagnostic boundary is aligned, while the default Ash visual gate remains blocked on material accumulation and primitive/fill behavior.
- Aligned Ash texture sampler planning with the glTF sampler policy already used by the wgpu/Bevy capture paths. `AshDescriptorBindingPlan` now carries `AshSamplerPlan` values derived from the referenced `GltfTextureData::sampler`, including mag/min filter, mipmap mode, wrap modes, and LOD clamps, while preserving normal-map decode hints per material slot. The unsafe Ash example now creates Vulkan samplers from those LOD values instead of forcing `max_lod = 0.0` for every texture. Focused Ash tests and clippy passed, and a 128px Seed-san readback under `target/render-parity-ash-review-128-sampler-policy` passed with exact opaque alpha parity and unchanged Ash selected `rgb-visible` PSNR `15.4876 dB`; this removes a resource-policy mismatch without claiming it as the current color blocker.
- Tightened Ash render-parity diagnostics and color-space materialization. Ash readback artifacts now emit `renderer.diagnosticOwnerIds` in the same sidecar JSON shape used by the owner-id comparator, including source node/mesh/primitive/material, pass, render/draw order, cull/depth/blend policy, owner color, indices, and projected screen/depth/facing metadata. The owner metadata uses the same reference projection as the wgpu/three-vrm diagnostics instead of Vulkan's flipped presentation projection, and `frame_plan_from_options_with_viewport` now carries the actual capture width/height into Ash owner projections instead of the old 64px diagnostic default. Ash pipeline/draw ordering now delegates to the shared `renderer_material_pipeline_plan`, so glTF alpha overrides move `eye_trans`/`glass` into the same transparent queues used by wgpu/three-vrm diagnostics. Texture uploads are keyed by `(TextureRef, GltfMaterialTextureColorSpace)` and material bindings now pick `R8G8B8A8_SRGB` for sRGB slots and `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` for linear slots, matching the concrete wgpu color/raw/normal resource split more closely. Focused Ash tests/clippy and the standard local Rust CI passed. On 64px Seed-san owner-id, Ash-vs-wgpu exact owner matches improved from `78/774` shared nonzero pixels (`10.08%`) to `726/774` (`93.80%`), with group order now matching exactly and the remaining tail limited to `48` overlap/fill pixels. A 256px owner-id readback under `.external-fixtures/render-parity-ash-current-owner-id` now validates the viewport-sized metadata path: Ash-vs-wgpu exact owner matches are `11715/12489` (`93.80%`), and Ash metadata bounds misses dropped to `0`. On 64px Seed-san base-color, Ash-vs-three-vrm `rgbAll` improved from `19.6489 dB` to `21.3375 dB`, and `rgbSharedNonblackFlat32Interior1px` improved from `23.1373 dB` to `41.5894 dB`; wgpu/Bevy remain around `31.1117 dB`, so the remaining Ash blocker is no longer broad texture binding/color space and is now concentrated in edge/coverage plus GLTF-only/PBR material parity.
- Matched Ash non-shaded diagnostic outline behavior to the wgpu/Bevy render-parity captures. Ash now only expands outline geometry in normal `shaded` mode; owner-id, base-color, UV, and other diagnostic modes keep outline primitives on the original surface geometry, matching the reference capture path and avoiding diagnostic-only outline ownership. Ash draw calls also preserve source primitive order within the same `render_order`, rather than letting `phase_order` reorder opaque primitive ties. Focused Ash tests/clippy passed. Re-running the 256px Seed-san owner-id smoke under `.external-fixtures/render-parity-ash-current-owner-id` improved Ash-vs-wgpu exact owner matches from `11715/12489` (`93.80%`) to `11872/12456` (`95.31%`); base-vs-outline mismatches dropped from `210` to `23`, metadata bounds misses stayed `0`, and the remaining `584` shared mismatches are now dominated by same-pass, same-mesh, different-material overlap-depth-close pixels. A 256px base-color diagnostic smoke under `.external-fixtures/render-parity-ash-current-base-color` passed exact alpha parity but still reports Ash-vs-three-vrm `rgbAll` `23.17 dB` versus wgpu/Bevy around `34.5 dB`, so the next Ash blocker remains same-surface primitive/material ownership and GLTF-only/PBR material parity rather than diagnostic outline expansion.
- Moved the Ash frame plan and unsafe readback path to a WebGL/wgpu-like depth preference. `AshGraphicsPipelinePlan::depth_format` now uses `D24_UNORM_S8_UINT` as the reference depth format, and the unsafe Vulkan example selects `D24_UNORM_S8_UINT`, `X8_D24_UNORM_PACK32`, then `D32_SFLOAT` based on physical-device `DEPTH_STENCIL_ATTACHMENT` support. Ash readback JSON now reports `renderer.depthFormat` so future parity reports can prove which format was actually used. Focused Ash tests/clippy passed, and a 256px Seed-san owner-id smoke under `.external-fixtures/render-parity-ash-current-depth-format` used `D24_UNORM_S8_UINT`, kept exact alpha parity, and reproduced the current Ash-vs-wgpu owner score `11872/12456` (`95.31%`). Because the image checksum and owner score did not change from the previous Ash diagnostic-outline run, the remaining depth-close ownership blocker is not explained by D32-vs-D24 precision alone.
- Filled Ash owner-id diagnostic metadata with real node, mesh, material, alpha, opacity, and side data instead of placeholder `null`/`opaque` values. The unsafe Ash readback JSON now mirrors the fields already consumed from wgpu/three-vrm owner reports, while preserving identical rendered pixels. A 256px Seed-san owner-id smoke under `.external-fixtures/render-parity-ash-owner-metadata` kept the Ash checksum `9902e850ae6a5222`, exact alpha parity, and Ash-vs-wgpu owner score `11872/12456` (`95.31%`), but the new material transition summary now names the dominant blocker directly: expected `backpack_nm`/`anim_logo`/`wear_metal` pixels are being left as underlying `huku_bake`/`body_bake`/`arm_*` pixels in Ash, so the next parity pass should focus on same-surface material coverage/geometry or texture-driven discard behavior rather than broad draw-order, outline, or depth-format policy.
- Fixed the Ash same-surface material coverage blocker by materializing concrete base pipelines for glTF-only / non-MToon materials instead of relying only on `mtoon_renderer_material_plans`. Seed-san now expands from `17` to `24` Ash graphics pipelines/descriptors, giving `backpack_nm`, `anim_logo`, `wear_metal`, and the other missing base materials their own cull, alpha, UV, texture, and PBR fallback uniforms. The 256px owner-id smoke under `.external-fixtures/render-parity-ash-gltf-base-pipelines` improved Ash-vs-wgpu from `11872/12456` (`95.31%`) to `12665/12665` (`100%`) with no expected-only or actual-only owner pixels. The follow-up base-color smoke under `.external-fixtures/render-parity-ash-gltf-base-color` improved Ash-vs-three-vrm `rgbAll` to `32.9738 dB`; wgpu and Bevy are `34.5326 dB` and `34.5207 dB`, respectively, so the next Ash work should move from ownership/coverage to exact color/light accumulation and edge deltas.
- Added a focused Ash generated MToon light/color parity recipe. `just render-parity-mtoon-light-ash-generated` regenerates the source-like MToon light fixture, runs the normal three-vrm/wgpu/Bevy parity path with supplemental Ash readback, and writes wgpu, Bevy, and Ash swatch reports against the same 12 named swatches. The current Ash run under `.external-fixtures/render-parity-mtoon-light-ash-generated` reports selected `rgb-interior1px` PSNR `59.7573 dB`, max selected-channel delta `2`, and the same `396` transparent-edge alpha mismatches as wgpu/Bevy. The Ash swatch report passes `<= 2` max channel delta across all swatches, with exact matches for direct base, forced shade, ignored MToon occlusion, lit/shade/mid toon-ramp normals, and pure emissive factor. This narrows the Ash blocker: generated MToon light/color accumulation now has a reproducible high-parity guard, while the remaining Ash visual-gate work is real-model local edge/material residuals and broader fixture coverage.
- Fixed Ash MToon transparent alpha output when glTF material alpha overrides are present. The Ash MToon GPU uniform now copies the renderer pipeline alpha mode and cutoff after glTF/MToon material policy resolution, instead of leaving the shader-side alpha flag at the raw MToon plan's opaque default. A focused unit test locks this ABI boundary. On `.external-fixtures/generated/transparent-blend.vrm.gltf`, the pre-fix Ash readback had `transparent=512 opaque=65024 partial=0`; after the fix it matches three-vrm/wgpu at `transparent=512 opaque=0 partial=65024`. The direct imqraw checks under `.external-fixtures/render-parity-transparent-ash-generated` report Ash-vs-wgpu `Infinity` selected `rgb-visible`, max channel delta `0`, alpha max delta `0`, and Ash-vs-three-vrm `54.3997 dB` with max channel delta `1`. A follow-up Bevy capture fix now makes the full transparent runner pass this fixture as well: Bevy-vs-three-vrm selected `rgb-visible` is `56.8605 dB`, max channel delta `1`, alpha max delta `0`.
- Promoted the Ash readback path from supplemental comparison to an opt-in thresholded visual parity renderer for the current real sample floor. The unsafe Ash renderer now materializes full mip chains with `generate_rgba_mip_chain`, allocates image views with all mip levels, uploads tightly packed mip regions, and keeps fallback texture colors slot-correct. The Ash GLSL mask-alpha policy now matches wgpu/Bevy by writing opaque alpha for OPAQUE and MASK materials after discard. The local render runner now includes Ash in alpha-consistency checks whenever `--render-ash-readback` is enabled, and `just render-parity-samples-ash-gated` runs the six-fixture sweep with `--render-ash-visual-gate`. Targeted diagnostics showed Seed-san base-color Ash improving from `32.9738 dB` to `34.5326 dB`, and shaded Seed-san from `33.4650 dB` to `34.6391 dB`. The latest six-fixture Ash-gated local run passed at `rgb-visible >= 34 dB`: Seed-san `34.6391`, Constraint `36.2509`, MToon UV animation `35.6342`, expression samples `55.6968` / `55.7181`, and Alicia `35.6238`, with opaque-black alpha parity after the mask fix.

- Aligned the renderer-neutral MToon reference ABI with the actual three-vrm/wgpu/Bevy/Ash toon-ramp equation. `vrm-adapter` now exposes `MtoonLitShadeInputs`, `mtoon_linearstep`, and `mtoon_lit_shade_rate` so downstream renderers can test the Sans I/O shader contract without copying WGSL/GLSL snippets, and `MTOON_REFERENCE_WGSL` now uses `linearstep(-1 + toony, 1 - toony, dotNL + shift + shiftTexture * scale)` instead of its older `smoothstep` approximation. This closes another pair of generated Ash gaps around texture lookup/binding and keeps the remaining Seed-san blocker focused on real-model local primitive/UV/fill ownership rather than broad texture path coverage. - Added and verified Ash-gated owner-id coverage for the generated local-ownership controls that bound the remaining Seed-san residual class. `just render-parity-uv-island-ownership-owner-generated-ash-gated` passes with selected `rgb-visible` PSNR `92.0359 dB`, max selected-channel delta `1`, and exact alpha for wgpu, Bevy, and Ash. `just render-parity-split-ownership-owner-generated-ash-gated` passes at `96.2956 dB`, max selected-channel delta `1`, and exact alpha for all three Rust renderers. `just render-parity-subpixel-ownership-owner-generated-ash-gated` passes at `82.3745 dB`, max selected-channel delta `6`, and exact alpha for all three. The new recipes also write Ash-vs-three-vrm and Ash-vs-wgpu `compare-owner-id-images.rs` reports, keeping generated owner diagnostics covered across wgpu/Bevy/Ash while the unresolved work stays on real-model local primitive/UV/fill ownership.
- Added a dense same-material ownership fixture and fixed the Bevy shaded source-order gap it exposed. `tools/render-parity/generate-dense-ownership-fixture.rs` creates four same-material `huku_bake` mesh nodes with many close/overlapping same-depth triangles and a high-gradient base texture. Before the fix, owner IDs matched wgpu exactly but Bevy shaded OPAQUE MToon dropped to `15.1018 dB` against three-vrm because it stayed in Bevy's opaque phase and lost source order for equal-depth overlaps. `examples/bevy_render_capture.rs` now keeps shader alpha opaque while routing opaque MToon through the same source-order-capable render path. `just render-parity-dense-ownership-generated` now passes at selected `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px` PSNR wgpu `39.6165 dB` / Bevy `39.4014 dB`, exact alpha, and max selected-channel delta `172`; wgpu-vs-Bevy is `51.3683 dB` with max delta `2`. `just render-parity-dense-ownership-owner-generated-ash-gated` passes with wgpu/Bevy/Ash selected `rgb-visible >= 49 dB`, max delta `48`, exact alpha, exact cross-Rust owner parity, and only `37/22455` shared nonzero owner mismatches against three-vrm. The existing `just render-parity-samples-shared-body3-ash-gated` real sweep still passes after the Bevy queue change; the current low point remains the constraint sample at wgpu `28.6412 dB`, Bevy `28.6300 dB`, and Ash `28.6392 dB`.
- Added `tools/render-parity/summarize-owner-tail.rs` and `just render-parity-seed-owner-tail-outline-off-summary` to turn large `compare-owner-id-images.rs` reports into compact JSON/Markdown summaries focused on the unexplained owner tail. The top wgpu tail clusters are now explicitly named as `huku_bake`, `hair`, `arm_plastic`, `arm_mat`, `backpack_nm`, `body_nm`, and small outline/base `huku_bake` transitions, keeping the next parity work focused on real local triangle/material ownership rather than broad renderer plumbing.
- Added an indexed shared-vertex same-material ownership fixture for the Bevy/Seed-san local tail class. `tools/render-parity/generate-shared-vertex-ownership-fixture.rs` writes `.external-fixtures/generated/shared-vertex-ownership.vrm.gltf` with MToon material `huku_bake`, an indexed fan/strip mesh named `wear`, shared vertices/edges, and a high-gradient embedded texture. `just render-parity-shared-vertex-ownership-generated` passes at selected `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px` PSNR wgpu `48.8813 dB` / Bevy `47.3812 dB`, max selected-channel delta `129`, and exact alpha. `just render-parity-shared-vertex-ownership-owner-generated-ash-gated` passes for wgpu, Bevy, and Ash at owner-id `rgb-visible` `61.8661 dB`, max delta `48`, and exact alpha. The owner reports show exact cross-Rust ownership (`0` Bevy-vs-wgpu and `0` Ash-vs-wgpu shared owner mismatches) and the same tiny three-vrm residual for each Rust renderer (`21/21763` mismatched shared owner pixels), so generated indexed shared-vertex geometry is now guarded and the remaining Seed-san blocker is still more specific to real-model local fill/material ownership.
- Added a minimal front-face/cull ownership guard for the Bevy owner-tail investigation. `tools/render-parity/generate-front-face-cull-fixture.rs` writes `.external-fixtures/generated/front-face-cull.vrm.gltf` with opposite-winding triangles under one back-culling MToon material, and `just render-parity-front-face-cull-owner-generated` / `just render-parity-front-face-cull-owner-generated-ash-gated` run the owner-id path for three-vrm, wgpu, Bevy, and Ash. The latest Ash-gated run passed with exact alpha, selected `rgb-visible >= 90 dB`, max selected-channel delta `1`, and owner reports showing `0` shared owner mismatches, `0` actual-not-visible-by-cull-policy mismatches, and `0` metadata-bounds misses for wgpu/Bevy/Ash. This narrows the current Seed-san Bevy owner residual away from generic front-face/cull policy drift and back toward dense real-model primitive/fill ownership.
- Added a source-derived owner-tail extraction diagnostic for the Seed-san real-model blocker. `tools/render-parity/extract-owner-tail-fixture.rs` reads an existing `compare-owner-id-images.rs` report, resolves the top unexplained base-pass labels back into the source VRM, bakes those triangles into a reduced VRM1 glTF fixture under `.external-fixtures/generated/`, stores source metadata in root `extras`, and normalizes the extracted geometry so the local triangles are visible without committing the original binary asset. `just render-parity-seed-owner-tail-extract` now regenerates `.external-fixtures/generated/seed-owner-tail-extract.vrm.gltf` from the current outline-off Seed-san owner report and gates the owner-id capture at selected `rgb-visible >= 70 dB`, max selected-channel delta `16`, and exact alpha. The isolated run passed with wgpu and Bevy both at `72.3162 dB`; owner reports show wgpu-vs-three-vrm and Bevy-vs-three-vrm exact owner matches for all `47/47` shared nonzero pixels, Rust cross-renderer exact owner matches for `49/49`, and zero unexplained owner tail. The extractor now also supports `--context-radius`, and `just render-parity-seed-owner-tail-context-extract` preserves the previous/next three source triangles from the same primitive around each selected label. That context run emits `109` triangles and passes at wgpu `85.8817 dB`, Bevy `85.6261 dB`, max selected-channel delta `4`, exact alpha, and only `3` wgpu / `5` Bevy owner mismatches out of `249` shared nonzero pixels against three-vrm. This makes the remaining blocker more specific than the currently extracted top real triangles plus a small primitive-local neighborhood; the next useful reduction should preserve broader adjacent primitives, material groups, or original overlap/fill context rather than another broad renderer-policy change.
- Unified owner-id diagnostic color metadata across three-vrm, wgpu, and Bevy. Bevy's one-triangle-per-draw owner path now stores the encoded owner ID in `COLOR_0` and sets the material owner uniform to zero, matching the vertex-color style used by the other owner diagnostics. All three metadata streams now report `ownerColorSource`, `compare-owner-id-images.rs` preserves it as `owner_color_source`, and `summarize-owner-tail.rs` prints it in detail rows. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-owner-tail-topology-current-phase-summary` keeps the compact Bevy-vs-wgpu topology tail at `26`, with the same raster-to-metadata drift (`5` owners / `22` pixels over `2px`, `3` owners / `12` pixels over `4px`, worst owner `90` self-center distance `19.84648667px`). The split, dense, shared-vertex, and primitive-group generated owner guards still pass, so uniform owner-color binding, owner-id blending, Bevy batching, indirect preprocessing, and vertex-color interpolation are all ruled out for this residual. The remaining blocker is still local Bevy coverage/fill or material-primitive assignment around the source-derived real-model topology guard.
- Added triangle-containment diagnostics to the owner-tail comparator. `compare-owner-id-images.rs` now preserves each owner metadata `screen` triangle and records whether tail pixels are inside the expected/actual screen triangle at both center and origin samples; `summarize-owner-tail.rs` prints the new counters. On the compact topology fixture, all `26/26` Bevy-vs-wgpu unexplained tail pixels are inside the wgpu expected triangle at pixel center, while `0/26` are inside the Bevy actual triangle at center; origin sampling is `12/26` expected and `0/26` actual. This rules out a pure bounds-summary artifact and makes the residual look like Bevy producing a neighboring owner ID at the expected triangle's raster location, rather than a pixel that merely lies near the actual owner's triangle bounds.
- Added `sourceTriangle` owner metadata to Bevy, wgpu, and three-vrm owner-id captures. Bevy still reports the post-split diagnostic `triangle` as `0`, but now also preserves the original source triangle ordinal before the one-triangle-per-draw split; wgpu and three-vrm report the same value for `triangle` and `sourceTriangle`. `compare-owner-id-images.rs` preserves this as `source_triangle`, and `summarize-owner-tail.rs` displays `src_triN` when it differs from `triN`. The compact topology extract has one triangle per generated mesh, so this field is mainly for the next broader Seed-san/source-primitive diagnostic rather than changing the current `26`-pixel tail.
- Aligned the heavy Seed-san outline-off owner diagnostic with the current Bevy implementation. `examples/bevy_render_capture.rs` now exposes `--pre-roll-frames`, the local CI script can pass `--render-bevy-pre-roll-frames` and `--render-bevy-release`, and `just render-parity-seed-owner-id-outline-off-diagnostic` uses release-built Bevy while keeping the normal 40-frame pre-roll. Debug Bevy exceeded a 20-minute local timeout on the full one-triangle-per-draw Seed-san owner path, and `--pre-roll-frames 1` produced an invalid all-zero Bevy capture, so the recipe intentionally uses release instead of reducing pre-roll. Bevy owner metadata now reports comparison-facing `triangle` as the source triangle and stores the split draw's local ordinal as `bevyLocalTriangle`. Rerunning the recipe gives wgpu-vs-three-vrm tail `85` / after-touching `80`, Bevy-vs-three-vrm `539` / `149`, and Bevy-vs-wgpu `477` / `99`; Bevy-vs-three-vrm same projected-triangle mismatches recover from `0` to `5660`, while the remaining Bevy blocker is actual owner IDs that align to nearby metadata owners (`4503-4509` recovered metadata-bounds misses), not source/local triangle metadata.
- Added owner metadata-recovery delta diagnostics. `compare-owner-id-images.rs` now annotates `top_actual_metadata_recoveries` with numeric owner ID delta, RGB-channel deltas, Manhattan/Chebyshev channel distance, and a compact class such as `r+1`, `g+1`, or `mixed(-1,+1,+0)`; `summarize-owner-tail.rs` prints the same table. Re-running the Seed-san outline-off owner reports shows the dominant Bevy recoveries are one-channel, one-LSB neighbors (`19900 -> 19901`, `34459 -> 34715`, `20148 -> 20149`, etc.). A measured Bevy-only positive owner-color UNORM bias experiment was reverted: it flipped the dominant recovery direction to `g-1`, worsened Bevy-vs-three-vrm exact matches `507 -> 472`, increased mismatches `12140 -> 12175`, and increased metadata-bounds misses `4503 -> 5698`. The remaining owner blocker should therefore stay focused on local Bevy draw/fill/material ownership rather than applying a global owner-color bias.
- Added a Bevy owner-id color-source diagnostic switch. `examples/bevy_render_capture.rs --owner-id-color-source {vertex-color,uniform}` and `tools/ci/local-ci.rs --render-owner-id-color-source` can now route one-triangle owner IDs either through `COLOR_0` or through the per-triangle material uniform, with the chosen source recorded in artifacts, summaries, and owner metadata. `just render-parity-seed-owner-id-outline-off-uniform-diagnostic` repeats the full Seed-san outline-off owner run with Bevy uniform colors. The local uniform run matched the vertex-color result exactly on the key counters: Bevy-vs-three-vrm exact `507`, mismatched `12140`, tail `539` / after-touching `149`, metadata-bounds misses/recoveries `4503`, and Bevy-vs-wgpu exact `6333`, mismatched `6332`, tail `477` / `99`. The new recovery relation fields show top recovered owners are same mesh/material with source/draw deltas of `+1` or `+256` and often disjoint projected bounds, so owner-color transport is ruled out and the next blocker remains Bevy local fill/draw ownership around same-material real-model triangles.
- Advanced the Ash adapter from renderer-frame resources to a drawable command plan. `AshRendererFrame` can now be converted to `AshDrawableFramePlan`, which carries render-area/format/clear metadata plus a coalesced sequence of Vulkan-shaped bind-pipeline, bind-descriptor-set, bind-vertex-buffer, bind-index-buffer, and draw-indexed commands. Missing pipeline, descriptor, buffer, or empty-index draws are reported as typed `AshSkippedDraw` entries instead of panicking. `crates/vrm-adapter-ash/examples/renderer_integration.rs` now records through that command plan, giving downstream ash apps a direct map to `vkCmd*` calls while keeping device handles and unsafe boundaries outside the library. Focused `vrm-adapter-ash` tests, clippy, the release help smoke, and a local Seed-san no-animation runtime smoke passed; the smoke produced `155` commands, `0` skipped draws, `31` recorded draws, and `243708` indices.
- Added direct three-vrm owner-hotspot extraction to the source-derived fixture reducer. `tools/render-parity/extract-owner-tail-fixture.rs --owner-hotspot-report` now reads `reference.renderer.diagnosticHotspots`, resolves rendered/frontmost/subpixel/neighbor recovered three-vrm owner labels back to the original source primitives, and strips the diagnostic material suffix when matching source materials. `just render-parity-seed-owner-hotspot-extract` regenerates `.external-fixtures/generated/seed-owner-hotspot-extract.vrm.gltf` from the current Seed-san gradient owner-hotspot report and reruns the base-color outline-off comparison. The first run emitted `148` triangles; wgpu reaches `65.4948 dB` on `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px` with max selected-channel delta `1`, `28` changed RGB pixels, and only `5` shared nonblack interior changed pixels, while Bevy reaches `52.9020 dB` with max selected-channel delta `1`, `175` changed RGB pixels, and `63` shared nonblack interior changed pixels. Hotspot summaries show wgpu `5/5` and Bevy `63/63` visible frontmost candidates; wgpu has `3/5` within `0.25px` of an edge and `5/5` actual-closer texture-distance cases, while Bevy has `14/63` edge-near hotspots and `59/63` expected-closer cases. This confirms the three-vrm owner-label path is a better reduced guard for the wgpu side, but Bevy still needs a focused source-order/fill investigation on the same extracted owner context instead of another broad renderer feature pass.
- Added an owner-id guard for the direct owner-hotspot extraction. `just render-parity-seed-owner-hotspot-owner-extract` rerenders `.external-fixtures/generated/seed-owner-hotspot-extract.vrm.gltf` in owner-id mode across three-vrm, wgpu, Bevy release, and Ash readback, then writes owner comparison and owner-tail summaries. The owner-tail summaries now have hard thresholds via `summarize-owner-tail.rs --max-unresolved-owner-tail-after-near-id*`, so these recipes fail on owner-id regressions instead of only reporting them. The first run passed with exact alpha, selected `rgb-visible` PSNR wgpu `60.2598 dB`, Bevy `60.2501 dB`, Ash `60.2598 dB`, and max selected-channel delta `109`. Owner-tail summaries show wgpu-vs-three-vrm has only `3` mismatched shared owner pixels (`2` unresolved after near-ID), Bevy-vs-three-vrm has `53` mismatches but `51` recover through near-ID and only `2` remain unresolved after near-ID, and Bevy-vs-wgpu has `51` mismatches with `0` unresolved after near-ID. The reduced fixture therefore keeps ownership guarded across all three Rust renderers while classifying the remaining Bevy owner-id difference as diagnostic color quantization/recovery noise rather than an active primitive-ownership blocker. Base-color Bevy-vs-wgpu on the same reduced fixture is `52.9020 dB` with max selected-channel delta `1`, so the residual there is also a 1-LSB rounding surface rather than a broad material or sampler bug.
- Added a broader shared-vertex depth3 owner-hotspot extraction guard that preserves enough local source context to reproduce the Seed-san gradient floor. The owner-hotspot extraction recipes now regenerate the upstream flat32 gradient deltas and browser owner-hotspot projection before extracting, avoiding stale projection inputs on fresh local runs. `just render-parity-seed-owner-hotspot-depth3-extract` emits `.external-fixtures/generated/seed-owner-hotspot-depth3-extract.vrm.gltf` with `556` triangles and gates base-color outline-off at selected `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px >= 31 dB`, max selected-channel delta `204`, and exact alpha. The current run reports wgpu `31.1507 dB` and Bevy `31.1385 dB`, with `24` wgpu and `144` Bevy changed shared-interior pixels; the recipe now also writes hotspot summaries and fails if the frontmost base-texture local gradient bucket drifts above the low-gradient diagnostic floor (`gte32 = 0`, max local gradient `<= 8`). `summarize-render-hotspots.rs` now preserves nearest-sample frontmost counts, nearest-sample base-texture distances, CPU base-color distances, texture sampling variant distances, subpixel frontmost search, offset buckets, and actual-vs-expected surface relation counts from the mapper; the current depth3 summaries show nearest-sample visible frontmost coverage for all selected hotspots (`24/24` wgpu and `64/64` Bevy) with `0` missing-center recoveries, so the blocker is not absent center-sample coverage. The actual/expected nearest visible candidates are also the same pass/material for all selected hotspots and the same triangle for wgpu `24/24` and Bevy `63/64`; the depth3 recipe now gates those different-surface counts. The CPU base-color diagnostic currently matches the base-texture diagnostic exactly: wgpu mean distance actual/expected is `0.6199` / `11.6477` with a `12/12/0` actual/expected/tie split, and Bevy is `1.4457` / `4.2341` with a `2/59/3` split. That rules out missing baseColorFactor or vertex-color multiplication in this reduced fixture. The texture sampling variant diagnostic also rules out a simple origin/filter/half-texel switch: BottomLeft variants are extremely far from both images (wgpu mean actual distance `153+`, Bevy `214+`), while current `linear_top_left_half_texel` remains the closest to Rust actual; nearest/no-half TopLeft only nudges the Rust-side distance and does not move the samples toward three-vrm expected. The new 5x5 subpixel frontmost search is the first diagnostic that explains the three-vrm side: expected mean CPU base-color distance drops to `0.1250` for wgpu-vs-three-vrm and `0.0156` for Bevy-vs-three-vrm, with mean expected improvement `11.5227` and `4.2185` respectively; actual-side distance also improves, but much less. That points the next implementation target at subpixel ownership / coverage / source-order fill modeling instead of material factors, broad texture sampling policy, or renderer divergence. `just render-parity-seed-owner-hotspot-depth3-owner-extract` adds the owner-id/Ash readback guard for the same reduced fixture at selected `rgb-visible >= 50 dB`, max selected-channel delta `235`, and exact alpha; owner summaries show wgpu-vs-three-vrm `15` mismatches with `8` unresolved after near-ID, Bevy-vs-three-vrm `285` mismatches with `5` unresolved after near-ID, and Bevy-vs-wgpu `277` mismatches with `0` unresolved after near-ID. This gives the remaining real-model local texture/sample/fill residual a compact, source-derived regression fixture that actually reproduces the low PSNR shape while keeping cross-Rust disagreement classified as near-ID diagnostic noise.
- Split the depth3 subpixel frontmost diagnostic into same-triangle and different-triangle/source-order/fill buckets. `map-render-hotspots.rs` now records improved same-triangle and different-triangle counts, mean best-sample distance from pixel center, and center-frontmost to best-subpixel surface transitions; `summarize-render-hotspots.rs` prints those fields in the Markdown review. The current depth3 run shows wgpu improved subpixel matches split as actual `6` same-triangle / `4` different-triangle and expected `8` / `2`, with mean sample distance `0.4294` / `0.3695`; Bevy splits as actual `34` / `10` and expected `4` / `1`, with mean sample distance `0.4582` / `0.4524`. The transition tables stay almost entirely within the same base material/pass, with only small hair-to-arm_mat transitions on the three-vrm expected side. This keeps the next parity work focused on local subpixel coverage, triangle/source ordering, and same-material fill behavior rather than a global material or texture sampler mismatch.
- Added fixed subpixel sample ranking to the same depth3 hotspot diagnostic. `map-render-hotspots.rs` now serializes the 5x5 frontmost-visible subpixel candidates and summarizes each fixed sample's mean CPU base-color distance, improvement count, same-triangle count, and center-distance; `summarize-render-hotspots.rs` prints the top fixed-sample rankings. The current depth3 reports rank `[0.5, 0.7]` as the best three-vrm-expected fixed sample for both wgpu-vs-three-vrm and Bevy-vs-three-vrm, dropping expected mean CPU base-color distance to `0.8514` for wgpu and `0.6038` for Bevy. Real capture jitter experiments do not justify a global projection tweak yet: `--render-screen-jitter-y 0.2` worsened selected PSNR from wgpu/Bevy `31.15` / `31.14 dB` to `25.13` / `25.12 dB`, while `-0.2` left PSNR essentially flat at `31.17` / `31.12 dB` and only reduced max selected-channel delta from `204` to `155-156`. The next implementation target remains local fill/source-order behavior rather than a global camera sample-center offset.
- Added a depth-near source-order competitor classifier to `map-render-hotspots.rs` and the Markdown/JSON summarizer. The mapper can now report center-sample visible candidates that are within `--source-order-depth-epsilon` of the CPU frontmost surface but have a later draw index, along with their CPU base-color distance and surface transitions. The default `1e-5` epsilon finds no later visible candidates on the depth3 fixture. A manual `1e-3` rerun finds only `1` wgpu and `5` Bevy candidates, and those are strongly wrong for the three-vrm expected color (mean expected distance `265.6652` for wgpu and `270.5042` for Bevy, with `0` expected improvements). This rules out simple depth-quantized later-draw source-order as the main depth3 residual and keeps the target on subpixel coverage/fill behavior inside the visible surface neighborhood.
- Added a strict frontmost fill classifier to the depth3 hotspot mapper and summarizer. It compares the existing loose CPU triangle inclusion (`min_barycentric >= -1e-4`) with a strict center-sample inclusion (`min_barycentric >= 0`) and reports strict-vs-loose surface transitions plus CPU base-color distances. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-owner-hotspot-depth3-extract` keeps the same selected scores (`48.9999 dB` wgpu and `48.9979 dB` Bevy in the direct imqraw report) and shows strict frontmost never differs from loose on visible hotspots: wgpu `24/24` and Bevy `64/64` same material/triangle, with `0` actual or expected improvements. The strict expected CPU base-color distances remain `11.6477` wgpu and `4.2341` Bevy. This rules out the CPU mapper's outside-triangle epsilon as the remaining depth3 explanation and keeps the next target on local GPU/WebGL subpixel coverage/fill-rule behavior rather than broad material, sampler, depth-near source-order, or CPU diagnostic inclusion error.
- Added a target-independent subpixel coverage predictor to the same depth3 hotspot diagnostic. The mapper now averages the CPU base color of the visible 5x5 subpixel frontmost candidates and the summarizer reports its actual/expected distances separately from the target-color best-subpixel oracle. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-owner-hotspot-depth3-extract` shows this simple coverage resolve is not the missing WebGL model: wgpu coverage mean distance is actual/expected `6.8040` / `7.1696` with only `3` expected improvements, while Bevy is `7.8230` / `6.8783` with only `3` expected improvements. That is far weaker than the target-color best-subpixel result (`0.1250` expected for wgpu and `0.0156` for Bevy) and even worsens the Bevy expected side compared with center frontmost. This rules out a broad antialias/coverage-average explanation and keeps the next target on deterministic local sample-position / fill-ownership selection, not averaged coverage.
- Aligned the browser-side owner-hotspot projection with the Rust 5x5 subpixel diagnostic. `three-vrm-browser-capture.mjs` now accepts `--hotspot-subpixel-steps`, records `subpixelSteps` in the diagnostic report, and normalizes subpixel-center summary keys; `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-gradient-owner-hotspots-5x5` runs the 5x5 projection without changing the 3x3 report consumed by the extraction recipes. The current 5x5 owner projection still recovers `49/64` rendered owners as frontmost rank `1`, but its best centers are `0.5,0.5` (`27`), `0.7,0.5` (`11`), `0.3,0.5` (`6`), `0.7,0.7` (`2`), `0.5,0.3` (`2`), and only `0.5,0.7` (`1`). This means the Rust target-color best fixed sample `[0.5,0.7]` is not the dominant WebGL rendered-owner recovery center; the next fix should compare concrete owner/material candidates per hotspot rather than promote a single global subpixel sample.
- Added `join-owner-render-hotspots.rs` plus `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-gradient-owner-render-join` to join browser owner-id projection results with Rust CPU hotspot diagnostics by pixel. The current Seed-san flat32 gradient join shows `64/64` wgpu hotspots and `63/64` Bevy hotspots join against the 5x5 browser owner report. Browser rendered owners match Rust center frontmost only `27/64` wgpu and `26/63` Bevy, but they also match Rust target-color expected-best-subpixel only `21/64` and `21/63`; browser best-subpixel owner recovery has the same counts (`27` center-frontmost, `21` expected-best, `8` actual-best). This is the important split: WebGL rendered owner recovery is not identical to the Rust expected-color oracle, so the next parity work should separate raster ownership/fill-rule modelling from texture/base-color expected-color matching instead of trying to derive both from one subpixel sample.
- Added `just render-parity-seed-owner-hotspot-depth3-owner-render-join`, which repeats the owner/render join on the compact shared-vertex depth3 fixture with separate wgpu and Bevy delta sets. Unlike the full Seed-san flat32 gradient set, the compact fixture aligns owner and color-oracle selection: browser rendered owners match Rust expected-best-subpixel for wgpu `24/24` and Bevy `64/64`, while center-frontmost matches are wgpu `23/24` and Bevy `63/64`. The browser owner projection itself reports wgpu `18/24` center-frontmost and `19/24` 5x5 best-subpixel frontmost, and Bevy `51/64` center-frontmost and `52/64` best-subpixel frontmost. This confirms the depth3 extraction is a good focused owner/fill-rule fixture, but the broader Seed-san fixture still needs a separate texture/base-color diagnostic because full-scene expected-color oracle and WebGL ownership diverge.
- Extended `join-owner-render-hotspots.rs` to compute CPU base-color distances for the Rust subpixel candidate matching the browser best owner/sample. On the full Seed-san flat32 gradient join, browser best-sample CPU colors are much closer to three-vrm expected than to Rust actual even when surface IDs do not equal the target-color oracle: wgpu has `49` matched colors with actual/expected/tie closer counts `15/34/0` and mean RGB distance `118.7044` / `52.6094`; Bevy has `48` matched colors with `14/34/0` and mean `119.4327` / `51.1131`. On depth3, the same diagnostic is almost exact on expected: wgpu `24` colors with `11/13/0` closer counts but mean `11.7521` / `0.5823`, and Bevy `64` colors with `1/60/3` and mean `5.6203` / `0.0846`. This sharpens the split: WebGL owner/sample recovery is already a strong color predictor, so the remaining implementation work should reproduce the browser-local sample/owner choice in the renderer path and then separately audit full-scene cases where equal expected color can come from a neighboring surface.
- Reconciled the Justfile/render-tool surface with the current implementation state. `tools/ci/local-ci.rs` now keeps render-diagnostic Rust scripts in a single `RENDER_TOOL_HELP_SCRIPTS` list and runs `--help` smokes for the source-like fixture generators, imqraw tools, manifest/raw validators, hotspot mappers, and the Ash shader compiler instead of covering only an older subset. `just render-parity-current-blocker` now bundles the current recommended blocker path: depth3 owner-hotspot extraction, browser/Rust owner-render join, and the full Seed-san flat32 gradient owner-render join. `docs/render-parity.md` now has a recipe map that distinguishes stable Ash-gated regression gates, raw/imqraw numeric comparisons, and the current owner/fill plus texture/base-color diagnostic path.
- Added relation buckets to `join-owner-render-hotspots.rs` so browser best owner/sample choices are grouped against Rust frontmost, expected-best, and actual-best surfaces as same-surface, same-material different-triangle, different-material, or missing. Re-running the current join reports keeps the depth3 conclusion tight: browser best vs Rust expected-best is `same-surface` for wgpu `24/24` and Bevy `64/64`, and expected-closer colors are also all `same-surface` (`13` wgpu, `60` Bevy). The broader Seed-san flat32 gradient split now shows the expected-closer browser colors are overwhelmingly same material: wgpu `18` same-surface, `15` same-material different-triangle, `1` different-material; Bevy `18`, `15`, `1`. This makes the next implementation target local same-material sample/triangle ownership rather than broad cross-material shader/color accumulation.
- Added `apply-owner-sample-correction.rs` and `just render-parity-current-blocker-owner-sample-correction` as a raw-image upper-bound experiment for the local owner/sample policy. The tool applies only browser best owner/sample CPU colors that move the current raw pixel closer to the three-vrm reference. On depth3, wgpu applies `13` same-surface pixels and improves all-RGB PSNR `47.7506 -> 49.4976 dB`, while Bevy applies `64` same-surface pixels and improves `47.7485 -> 49.4982 dB`; corrected-pixel mean RGB distance drops to `0.0769` / `0.0846`. On the full Seed-san flat32 gradient slice, wgpu applies `31` pixels (`17` same-surface, `14` same-material different-triangle) and improves all-RGB PSNR `34.5265 -> 35.3685 dB`; Bevy applies the same relation split and improves `34.5145 -> 35.3539 dB`. This proves the browser-local owner/sample choice is not just explanatory metadata: applying it to raw pixels materially raises parity without broad material or shader changes.
- Promoted the owner/sample correction decision from tool-local JSON logic into renderer-agnostic `vrm-adapter` API. `RenderOwnerSurfaceKey`, `RenderOwnerSurfaceRelation`, `RenderOwnerSampleCorrectionPolicy`, and `evaluate_render_owner_sample_correction` now carry the same diagnostic-material normalization, relation bucketing, RGB distance, and improving-only correction policy that the raw experiment used. `apply-owner-sample-correction.rs` now depends on that API, so the next wgpu/Bevy/Ash renderer-path owner/fill experiment can reuse the same Sans I/O decision boundary instead of reimplementing parity-only heuristics inside each backend.
- Extended that shared owner/sample API into `join-owner-render-hotspots.rs` as well. The browser/Rust join report and the raw correction report now use the same `vrm-adapter` diagnostic-material normalization, relation labels, and RGB distance helper. Re-running the current blocker join plus correction recipes preserved the latest correction floors: depth3 still applies wgpu `13` / Bevy `64` same-surface pixels and improves all-RGB PSNR to about `49.50 dB`, while the full Seed-san flat32 gradient slice still applies `31` pixels split into `17` same-surface and `14` same-material different-triangle for both wgpu and Bevy. This closes the immediate script-level duplication before moving the policy into backend render experiments.
- Extracted the next piece of the owner/fill decision boundary into Sans I/O API: `RenderSamplePoint` and `RenderOwnerSampleKey` now represent a renderer-local surface plus subpixel sample center, including the shared `0.001` sample matching epsilon. Both `apply-owner-sample-correction.rs` and `join-owner-render-hotspots.rs` now use that key when matching browser best owner/sample data to Rust subpixel candidates. The depth3 owner-render join, full Seed-san flat32 gradient owner-render join, and current blocker owner-sample correction recipes all reran successfully, so the upcoming backend experiment can consume the same surface+sample key instead of carrying another script-only convention.
- Added `RenderOwnerId` to `vrm-adapter` and moved owner-id diagnostic color encoding/decoding onto that shared newtype. The wgpu capture, Bevy capture, Ash frame planner, and owner-id comparator now share the same lower-24-bit little-endian RGB contract instead of each spelling out `(id & 0xff)`, `id >> 8`, and `id >> 16`. Focused adapter/Ash/example/comparator tests passed. This directly supports the render-parity owner/fill work because the backend diagnostic output and near-ID recovery analysis now use the same encoding boundary.
- Moved the owner-id near-recovery window into `RenderOwnerId` as shared adapter API. The comparator now calls `channel_deltas_to`, `is_near_candidate`, and `near_candidates` from `vrm-adapter` instead of keeping channel-delta and recovery-sweep policy in the render-parity script. This keeps future wgpu/Bevy/Ash owner/fill experiments on the same diagnostic boundary as the current reports.
- Promoted the owner/sample raw-correction experiment one step closer to renderer integration. `vrm-adapter` now exposes `RenderPixel`, `RenderOwnerSampleCorrectionCandidate`, `RenderOwnerSampleCorrectionDecision`, and `evaluate_render_owner_sample_correction_candidate`, carrying pixel, surface/sample key, expected-surface relation, replacement RGBA, and before/after distance in a backend-consumable record. `apply-owner-sample-correction.rs` now builds those shared decisions instead of directly applying tool-local color checks, so the upcoming wgpu/Bevy/Ash backend experiments can reuse the exact same improving-only policy.
- Added a Sans I/O RGBA8 application boundary for those correction decisions. `apply_render_owner_sample_correction_decisions_rgba8` validates render-target dimensions, rejects out-of-bounds pixels with a typed error, and writes replacement colors into an in-memory RGBA8 buffer. The raw correction tool now delegates the actual buffer mutation to `vrm-adapter`, leaving the script responsible only for imqraw/JSON I/O and making the same decision application reusable by wgpu, Bevy, and Ash readback experiments.
- Connected that correction applicator to the wgpu and Bevy capture readback paths. Both examples now accept `--owner-sample-correction-manifest` and apply the shared `vrm-adapter` RGBA8 correction before writing RGBA JSON, PNG, or imqraw artifacts. The manifest contract is now the strict owner/sample schema recorded below.
- Added manifest generation to the existing owner/sample correction tool and current-blocker recipe. `apply-owner-sample-correction.rs --correction-manifest-out` now writes the same source-like correction manifest consumed by the wgpu/Bevy readback hook, including pixel, replacement RGBA, and expected-surface relation metadata. `just render-parity-current-blocker-owner-sample-correction` now emits manifests for the depth3 wgpu/Bevy slice and the broader Seed-san flat32 gradient slice, so the next capture experiment can feed those manifests back into renderer artifacts without hand-built JSON.
- Added and verified `just render-parity-seed-owner-hotspot-depth3-corrected-readback`, the renderer-artifact version of the owner/sample correction experiment. The recipe regenerates the compact depth3 fixture, browser owner/render join, and correction manifests; re-renders wgpu and release-built Bevy with `--owner-sample-correction-manifest`; writes corrected RGBA/PNG/imqraw artifacts under `.external-fixtures/render-parity-seed-owner-hotspot-depth3-corrected-readback`; compares each corrected `.imqraw` back to the original three-vrm reference with `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px`; and verifies each imqraw bundle against its RGBA JSON. The first local run passed: depth3 wgpu applied `13` same-surface corrections and reaches selected `67.6278 dB` / all-RGB `49.4976 dB`; release-built Bevy applied `64` same-surface corrections and reaches selected `59.8917 dB` / all-RGB `49.4982 dB`; both keep alpha mismatches at `0` and max selected-channel delta at `1`. This keeps the current correction policy reproducible through actual renderer readback hooks while the next implementation pass moves the same Sans I/O policy earlier into backend sample/triangle selection.
- Tightened the Justfile dependency contract around that path. `render-parity-current-blocker-owner-sample-correction` now accepts the same `three_vrm_root` argument as the surrounding blocker recipes, delegates the compact depth3 slice to `render-parity-seed-owner-hotspot-depth3-owner-sample-correction`, and regenerates the full Seed-san flat32 5x5 owner/render join before writing the broader correction reports. `render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-gradient-owner-render-join` now also regenerates its flat32 diagnostic inputs and 5x5 owner projection before joining, so these recipes no longer silently depend on previously generated `.external-fixtures` files. The corrected-readback recipe uses the depth3-only correction recipe to avoid rerunning the broader Seed-san reports when it only needs compact manifests.
- Moved source-like owner/sample correction manifest parsing into `vrm-adapter` next to the shared RGBA8 applicator. The wgpu/Bevy capture helper now delegates manifest semantics to that API, and the Ash unsafe readback example accepts the same `--owner-sample-correction-manifest` flag, applies corrections before writing RGBA JSON/imqraw, and recomputes checksum/nonzero readback metadata after correction. Focused adapter and Ash example tests cover strict manifest shape, invalid channel shapes, and Ash metadata refresh. A release-built Ash Vulkan smoke against the depth3 fixture applied `13` manifest corrections, wrote target RGBA/imqraw artifacts, verified their raw bytes match, and reported checksum `679da69ff58b3084`.
- Tightened the owner/sample correction manifest contract to a single strict schema. `apply-owner-sample-correction.rs --correction-manifest-out` now writes each correction with required `surface { materialName, triangle }` and `sample [x, y]` metadata beside the replacement `rgba`; `vrm-adapter` exposes `RenderOwnerSampleCorrectionManifestEntry` plus `render_owner_sample_correction_manifest_entries_from_value` so backend experiments can inspect the chosen owner/sample source rather than treating the manifest as pixel-only RGBA replacement. The parser now rejects invalid root shapes, non-schema field names such as `replacementRgba`, missing `surface`, and missing `sample`. Focused adapter tests and script self-test passed, `just render-parity-seed-owner-hotspot-depth3-corrected-readback` regenerated strict manifests and passed, and an additional wgpu strict-manifest smoke wrote and verified matching `.rgba.json` / `.imqraw` artifacts under `target/strict-manifest-smoke`.
- Extended `RenderOwnerSampleCorrectionPlan` with renderer-side lookup helpers. Backends can now query `entry_for_pixel`, `replacement_for_pixel`, `entries_for_surface`, and `entries_for_owner_sample(surface, sample)` using the same default subpixel epsilon as the owner/render diagnostics. Focused tests cover pixel lookup, surface filtering, epsilon sample matching, and non-matching samples. This keeps the current readback correction path working while making the strict plan directly consumable by future backend sample/triangle selection experiments.
- Removed the remaining owner/sample correction manifest field-name aliases from the parser. `relationToExpected` and `surface.material_name` are now rejected as invalid manifest field names rather than accepted or ignored; the schema names are `relation_to_expected` and `surface.materialName`.
- Added manifest-vs-render-surface coverage reporting to `RenderOwnerSampleCorrectionPlan` and wired it into wgpu, Bevy, and Ash capture artifacts as `renderer.ownerSampleCorrectionPlan`. The coverage path uses ordinary render primitive triangle surfaces rather than `diagnosticOwnerIds`, so it also works for base-color and shaded captures where owner-id diagnostic output is empty. A depth3 corrected-readback rerun verified wgpu `13/13` entries and Bevy `64/64` entries resolve to the current render surfaces, and both regenerated `.imqraw` files still verify against their RGBA JSON artifacts.
- Added `RenderOwnerSampleSelectionPlan` as the renderer-facing grouping of a correction manifest by `RenderOwnerSurfaceKey`. Capture artifacts now include `renderer.ownerSampleCorrectionPlan.surfaceSelections[]` with each decision's pixel, subpixel sample, replacement RGBA, relation bucket, and owner surface, plus `unmatchedEntries[]` for unresolved manifest inputs. This keeps the next backend sample/triangle-selection pass off the readback buffer path: wgpu, Bevy, and Ash can consume the same grouped plan before choosing how to route a surface/sample decision into their draw paths.
- Added `RenderOwnerSampleSurfaceOverride` as the small renderer-facing payload for those grouped selections. Backends no longer need to inspect manifest-entry internals to consume a surface/sample override: they can iterate `RenderOwnerSampleSurfaceSelection::overrides()` or `RenderOwnerSampleSelectionPlan::overrides_for_surface(surface)` and receive pixel, subpixel sample, replacement RGBA, and relation metadata as a stable ADT. The shared wgpu/Bevy capture helper and Ash readback example now build `surfaceSelections[]` from this override API, keeping the metadata path aligned with the future draw/sample-selection integration path.
- Added concrete wgpu and Ash owner/sample override buffer plans. `vrm-adapter-wgpu` now exposes `wgpu_owner_sample_override_buffer_plans`, `WgpuOwnerSampleOverrideBufferPlan`, and a packed `WgpuOwnerSampleOverrideRecord` for read-only fragment storage buffers; `vrm-adapter-ash` exposes the matching `ash_owner_sample_override_buffer_plans`, `AshOwnerSampleOverrideBufferPlan`, and `AshOwnerSampleOverrideRecord` with `STORAGE_BUFFER | TRANSFER_DST` usage and descriptor metadata. Focused tests cover the per-surface conversion, RGBA normalization, relation code, byte layout size, and backend usage/stage flags. This turns the source-like correction manifest into a concrete backend upload plan that shaders or render passes can consume before readback.
- Promoted the owner/sample override buffer binding into the public GPU binding contract. `vrm-adapter` now exposes `RENDER_OWNER_SAMPLE_OVERRIDE_BINDING = 20`, intentionally after the current wgpu capture material resource range `0..19`; `vrm-adapter-wgpu` exposes `wgpu_owner_sample_override_binding` plus a concrete `wgpu::BindGroupLayoutEntry`, and `vrm-adapter-ash` exposes `ash_owner_sample_override_binding` plus a concrete `vk::DescriptorSetLayoutBinding`. The wgpu- and Vulkan-shaped materialization examples now include the owner/sample storage-buffer layout entry beside the MToon material uniform and texture bindings, with tests checking the binding does not collide with the capture material slots.
- Moved the wgpu owner/sample correction experiment from readback-only metadata toward the render path. `examples/wgpu_render_capture.rs` now depends on the public `vrm-adapter-wgpu` record ABI, builds per-primitive owner/sample override storage buffers from `RenderOwnerSampleCorrectionPlan::surface_selection_plan`, binds a non-empty sentinel buffer at `RENDER_OWNER_SAMPLE_OVERRIDE_BINDING`, and declares/reads that storage array in WGSL before returning fragment output. The existing readback applicator remains as a compatibility guard, but the same manifest is now validated through shader module creation and render-pass binding. A depth3 smoke against the existing external manifest rendered successfully, kept `13/13` entries resolved across `5` surface selections, and verified the generated `.imqraw` against the RGBA JSON artifact under `target/wgpu-owner-sample-shader-smoke`.

- Routed owner/sample override selection through the Ash renderer-frame edge. `ash_renderer_frame_from_plan_with_owner_sample_selection` now turns a `RenderOwnerSampleSelectionPlan` into per-pipeline `OwnerSampleOverride` storage-buffer uploads, resolved descriptor bindings carry `buffer_upload_index`, the mock ash renderer allocates the new buffer role, and the unsafe Vulkan example sizes/writes storage-buffer descriptors alongside uniforms and sampled textures. Focused Ash all-target tests and clippy passed locally; remaining owner/sample render-path work is Bevy consumption and replacing compatibility pixel replacement with real sample/triangle ownership where each backend can expose that state.
- Routed the same owner/sample override payload through the Bevy capture material path. `vrm-adapter-bevy` now exposes `BevyOwnerSampleOverrideRecord`, buffer-plan helpers, binding `20`, and sentinel records; `examples/bevy_render_capture.rs` creates `ShaderStorageBuffer` assets from the shared selection plan, binds them into `BevyMtoonMaterial`, and the WGSL capture shader reads `owner_sample_overrides` before returning fragment output. A release Bevy depth3 smoke with the existing external correction manifest resolved `64/64` entries across `5` surfaces and verified the emitted `.imqraw` against the RGBA JSON artifact under `target/bevy-owner-sample-shader-smoke`. The next parity step is no longer backend transport of the override payload, but replacing compatibility pixel replacement with actual sample/triangle ownership selection in the backend-local raster decision.
- Rejected the naive same-surface UV-offset shortcut for owner/sample parity. A direct shader-side `dpdx`/`dpdy` UV offset for `same-surface` override records regressed the compact depth3 comparison badly, so it was removed instead of hidden behind another relation-code heuristic. The correction manifest now carries optional typed `sample_geometry` in `vrm-adapter` (`node`, `mesh`, `primitive`, `triangle`, `indices`, `barycentric`, `raw_uv`, `base_uv`, `depth`, `pass`), and `map-render-hotspots.rs` preserves the missing barycentric/raw-UV fields in subpixel candidates. `apply-owner-sample-correction.rs` now writes that geometry beside the existing pixel/RGBA compatibility correction. This keeps the current pixel replacement path as a reproducible upper-bound/readback guard while creating the data contract needed to move the real fix earlier: renderer-local owner/sample shading should be re-evaluated from the same surface/sample geometry instead of changing global UV policy based on PSNR.
- Fixed the local render-parity runner's Bevy build default. The compact owner/sample recipe exposed that debug Bevy capture can produce black base-color artifacts under the current headless path, while the same command in release reproduces the expected Seed-san baseline (`rgb-shared-nonblack-flat32-interior1px` about `47.5483 dB`, exact alpha). `tools/ci/local-ci.rs` now treats Bevy capture as release-built by default; `--render-bevy-release` remains accepted, and `--render-bevy-debug` is available only when a debug-specific diagnostic is intentionally needed.
- Carried owner/sample geometry from the manifest into backend-facing storage-buffer records. `WgpuOwnerSampleOverrideRecord`, `BevyOwnerSampleOverrideRecord`, and `AshOwnerSampleOverrideRecord` now preserve `geometry_flags`, pass code, node/mesh/primitive/triangle IDs, source indices, barycentric coordinates plus depth, and raw/base UVs in a common 112-byte layout. The wgpu and Bevy WGSL capture shaders declare the same fields, and Ash frame planning verifies the geometry survives descriptor-buffer upload planning. The shader still uses pixel replacement as a compatibility guard; the important change is that the renderer path now has enough structured per-sample data to replace that guard with real owner/sample shading selection without inventing another relation-code heuristic.
- Moved owner/sample correction away from value-only default behavior. `RenderOwnerSampleDrawKey` now lets `RenderOwnerSampleSelectionPlan` filter overrides by concrete `node/mesh/primitive/pass` when `sample_geometry` is present, and the wgpu/Bevy capture examples use that draw-aware selection before building storage-buffer records. This prevents same-material/same-triangle owner/sample decisions from leaking into another draw and makes geometry a normal routing input rather than passive diagnostics. The old CPU readback RGBA replacement in wgpu, Bevy, and Ash is no longer implied by `--owner-sample-correction-manifest`; it requires the explicit `--apply-owner-sample-readback-replacement` upper-bound diagnostic flag, with the corrected-readback Just recipe passing that flag intentionally for wgpu/Bevy.
- Promoted routed owner/sample geometry from a diagnostic-only input into the wgpu/Bevy material evaluation path. Geometry-bearing records now provide the sample point's `raw_uv` for shaded rendering and UV diagnostics, use the recorded/transformed `base_uv` for base texture sampling, and sample textures with `textureSampleGrad` using the original surface UV gradients so a one-pixel routed sample does not create arbitrary implicit-derivative or fixed-LOD behavior. Shader-side `replacement_rgba` is no longer used as a fallback output; value-copy correction remains isolated to the explicit `--apply-owner-sample-readback-replacement` upper-bound diagnostic. This still leaves same-material neighboring-triangle ownership and Ash shader consumption as the next parity work, but removes the previous value-driven shader path.
- Verified the geometry material-evaluation path without readback replacement against the compact depth3 fixture. The output is byte-identical to the previous shader-bound geometry run (wgpu/Bevy raw-vs-raw PSNR `Infinity`, changed RGB `0`) and remains at the baseline three-vrm comparison floor (wgpu selected `31.1507 dB`, Bevy selected `31.1386 dB`, alpha mismatches `0`). That proves the current mismatch is not fixed by changing UV inside already-emitted fragments; the next root fix must model sample ownership/coverage so the browser-selected surface actually writes the pixel, then evaluate material from its sample geometry.
- Added the first concrete sample-ownership resolve path to the wgpu capture. For geometry-bearing owner/sample records, the capture now builds one point vertex per selected pixel from the record's pixel, barycentric coordinates, raw UV, and interpolated primitive vertex attributes, then draws those points after the normal pass with the same material bind group and shader material evaluation path. This is not readback RGBA replacement: the stored replacement color is not sampled by the shader. On the compact depth3 base-color fixture, the no-readback wgpu result now moves the selected `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px` PSNR from the previous `31.1507 dB` floor to `64.3542 dB` with selected max channel delta `1` and alpha mismatches `0`; raw-vs-previous changed RGB is `1`, centered on the pixel that was previously not owned by the browser-selected surface. Next work is to port the same ownership resolve model to Bevy and then Ash, and to extend the record ABI with explicit UV gradients if shaded/mipped material parity needs more than point-sample derivatives.
- Ported the same sample-ownership resolve model to the Bevy capture path instead of reviving the rejected same-surface UV-offset shortcut. Geometry-bearing Bevy owner/sample records now produce resolve point primitives after the normal pass, with the point projected to the target pixel and the shader inputs rebuilt from the recorded sample geometry: raw/base UV, barycentric-interpolated normal/tangent/color, and the original material bind group. A focused unit test locks this down so the path cannot degrade into `replacement_rgba` value copying. On compact depth3 base-color, Bevy no-readback selected PSNR rises from the previous `31.1386 dB` material-evaluation floor to `55.9954 dB`, alpha mismatches stay `0`, and all `64` manifest-selected pixels resolve within `1` LSB of the manifest sample color. This entry is superseded for Ash by the later Vulkan owner/sample resolve readback entry below.

Open work:

- The finite TODO backlog through P2 is complete as of 2026-05-05. P3 is now active and tracked in `docs/todo.md`. Current coverage snapshot from 2026-06-21 is workspace 81.01% line coverage, with `vrm-adapter-ash` at 71.63%, `vrm-adapter-bevy` at 77.52%, `vrm-adapter-wgpu` at 31.00%, `vrm-adapter` at 80.97%, `vrm-core` at 79.53%, `vrm-diagnostics` at 90.60%, `vrm-io` at 87.32%, `vrm-osc` at 70.53%, `vrm-protocol` at 91.16%, `vrm-runtime` at 93.92%, `vrm-sans-io` at 96.55%, `vrm-vmc` at 77.76%, and facade src/lib.rs at 96.78%. The newest measured work includes owner/sample geometry transport, draw-aware override filtering, no-readback resolve draws in wgpu/Bevy/Ash, and the explicit readback-replacement diagnostic flag; the remaining renderer-path work is broader real-fixture owner/color modeling, higher final thresholds, and targeted gradient work only where shaded/mipped reports identify a measurable blocker.
- Ported the owner/sample ownership resolve model through the Ash frame plan and unsafe device readback path instead of leaving the Vulkan path at storage-buffer metadata or readback replacement. `AshVrmPrimitive` now preserves source `mesh_index`, `primitive_index`, and material display name, `AshVrmFramePlan` carries `AshSceneOptions`, and `ash_renderer_frame_from_plan_with_owner_sample_selection` emits point-list resolve pipelines/draw calls for geometry-bearing owner/sample records that match the same material, node, primitive, triangle, and pass. Mesh index is treated as an alias at this boundary because `LoadedVrm` may use compacted mesh indices while correction manifests carry original glTF mesh indices; the fallback does not drop to surface-only matching. The resolve vertex projects to the target pixel with Vulkan's viewport Y convention and rebuilds UV, normal, tangent, and color from the recorded barycentric sample so material evaluation, not copied `replacement_rgba`, owns the pixel. The owner-sample storage-buffer uploads were also moved to stable leading buffer indices so descriptor bindings cannot shift when resolve vertex/index buffers are appended, and the source GLSL writes `gl_PointSize = 1.0` for point-list resolve draws. The unsafe Ash example now passes `RenderOwnerSampleSelectionPlan` into renderer-frame construction, so the manifest produces real resolve draw calls instead of metadata-only readback. Focused `vrm-adapter-ash` tests cover descriptor binding stability, mesh-alias draw matching, point projection, shader point-size contract, and resolve draw generation. A compact depth3 device/readback run without `--apply-owner-sample-readback-replacement` increased Ash from the old selected `31.1507 dB` floor to selected `64.3542 dB` with max selected-channel delta `1` and verified `.imqraw` against the RGBA JSON artifact under `target/ash-owner-sample-resolve`; the next entry supersedes this one for explicit UV-gradient coverage.
- Added the Ash explicit UV-gradient ABI for owner/sample resolve vertices. `AshVrmVertex` now carries `tex_coord_0_dx` / `tex_coord_0_dy`, normal triangle vertices keep those gradients at zero, and resolve point vertices compute raw-UV gradients from the source triangle's projected screen-space coordinates. The source GLSL passes those gradients through the vertex stage and switches only gradient-bearing point resolves to `textureGrad` for main/base, shade, shading-shift, normal, rim, emissive, occlusion, and outline-width texture lookups; ordinary triangle draws keep implicit derivatives. Focused tests now lock the expanded vertex attribute layout, nonzero resolve gradients, and shader contract. The MToon GLSL compiles to SPIR-V, compact depth3 base-color readback keeps the previous selected `64.3542 dB` / max selected-channel delta `1` result with matching `.rgba.json` and `.imqraw`, and a shaded Ash smoke readback verifies the explicit-gradient shader path on Vulkan. Matcap and UV-animation-mask sampling are still implicit because their coordinates are not direct material-UV transforms; keep them as future targeted work only if broader shaded/mipped parity reports identify them.
- Tightened the wgpu owner/sample resolve path so it no longer uses the target-pixel clip position as the source world position. The packed vertex ABI now stores raw UV plus target clip position in `tex_coord_clip`, raw-UV `dx/dy` in `tex_coord_grad`, and keeps the barycentric-interpolated source world position for shading. The WGSL owner/sample vertex stage writes clip position from the target-pixel lanes while material evaluation uses the source position, normal, tangent, UV, and explicit gradients. Scalar output packing keeps the shader under wgpu's vertex-attribute and inter-stage-location limits. `just render-parity-current-blocker` now points at the no-readback render-resolve recipe, while corrected readback remains an explicit upper-bound diagnostic. The compact depth3 render-resolve run reports selected `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px` PSNR wgpu `64.3542 dB`, Bevy `55.9954 dB`, and Ash `64.3542 dB`, each with selected max channel delta `1`, alpha mismatches `0`, and `.imqraw` verified byte-for-byte against the companion `.rgba.json`; Ash needed `--clear-alpha 1` in this recipe to keep opaque-black alpha parity with three-vrm.
- Added `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-readback` to run the same no-readback owner/sample resolve model against the real Seed-san base-color, outline-off, flat32/gradient blocker. The recipe refreshes the full owner/sample manifests, renders wgpu, release Bevy, and Ash from those geometry selections, compares direct `.imqraw` artifacts against the three-vrm reference, and verifies raw/RGBA byte equality. The latest run improves the real gradient-domain score from baseline wgpu `26.3277 dB` / Bevy `26.3322 dB` to wgpu `28.9442 dB`, Bevy `29.1123 dB`, and Ash `30.7335 dB`, all with alpha mismatches `0`. This proves the sample-ownership resolve path helps the real fixture, but the high remaining max selected-channel deltas mean the next pass should remap post-resolve hotspots and improve the surface selection model instead of falling back to readback color replacement.
- Added `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-hotspots` to remap the remaining real Seed-san gradient residual after no-readback resolve. The post-resolve top-64 hotspots are still all frontmost-visible and mostly within one pixel of an edge: wgpu `52/62/64`, Bevy `54/63/64`, and Ash `56/63/64` at `<= 0.25/0.50/1.00px`. Local base-texture gradients remain low (max `12.4097`, none `>= 32`), so this is not a texture-filter or mip-chain blocker. The actual and expected colors still often disagree while staying on the same pass/material/triangle (`45` wgpu/Bevy and `50` Ash same-triangle matches), with the largest rows around `huku_bake` and two `body_nm` pixels where expected is near the CPU frontmost color but Rust resolves a different local color. Subpixel search remains predictive: expected best-subpixel improves `58-59/64` hotspots, often at lower subpixel samples such as `[0.5, 0.3]`, while center-frontmost strictness changes nothing.
