Treat this as evidence for a fill/owner modelling gap, not as an oracle for selecting new default samples by RGB distance.
- Added `build-owner-sample-selection.rs` and switched the standard render-resolve recipes to its owner-id based manifests. The new tool reads only three-vrm owner-hotspot projection JSON and Rust CPU hotspot geometry, never expected/actual color images, and emits the existing strict manifest shape so wgpu, Bevy, and Ash can consume the same renderer path. `render-parity-current-blocker-owner-sample-selection` and `render-parity-seed-owner-hotspot-depth3-owner-sample-selection` are now the default manifest builders; the older `apply-owner-sample-correction.rs` recipes remain explicit upper-bound diagnostics. This addresses the earlier value-driven behavior: PSNR can still report whether a change helped, but sample ownership is now derived from rendered owner identity first.
- Verified the owner-id based selection path on the compact current blocker. `just render-parity-seed-owner-hotspot-depth3-owner-sample-selection` resolved wgpu `24/24` and Bevy `64/64` rendered-owner hotspot samples without reading target colors. `just render-parity-current-blocker` then passed through wgpu, release Bevy, and Ash resolve draws with those manifests. The selected `rgb-shared-nonblack-interior1px` results remain wgpu `64.3542 dB`, Bevy `55.9954 dB`, and Ash `64.3542 dB`, with selected max channel delta `1`, alpha mismatches `0`, and verified `.imqraw`/RGBA byte parity. This confirms the previous compact score was not dependent on value-copy or expected-color filtering.
- Re-ran the real Seed-san flat32/gradient render-resolve path with owner-id based selection manifests rather than color-distance correction manifests. The owner selection builder resolved wgpu `49/64` and Bevy `48/63` rendered-owner samples without reading target colors; missing recovery remains `15` for both. The no-readback real-model results are wgpu `28.5090 dB`, Bevy `27.2176 dB`, and Ash `30.7519 dB` on `rgb-shared-nonblack-gradient-interior1px`, with exact opaque alpha and verified `.imqraw`/RGBA byte parity. These wgpu/Bevy numbers are lower than the previous correction-derived run, which is the correct signal: the default path is no longer selecting samples because their CPU colors look closer to the target. The remaining task is to match three-vrm's rendered owner/fill-rule first, then audit texture/base-color color modelling for the recovered-owner cases.
- Added and ran `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-owner-render-join` as the post-resolve root-cause diagnostic. It reuses the current render-resolve residual deltas, asks three-vrm to render owner IDs for those exact pixels, summarizes owner recovery for wgpu/Bevy/Ash, and joins the rendered owner IDs with Rust hotspot candidates. The first run reports rendered owners for `64/64` residual pixels across all three backends; Rust center-frontmost owner matches are wgpu `47/64`, Bevy `46/64`, and Ash `45/64`, while 5x5 owner recovery is `49/64` for all three. The recovered browser-best sample colors are mixed rather than a universal target-color oracle: wgpu favors expected `34/49`, Bevy is almost split `25/49`, and Ash favors actual `33/49`. That gives the next root split: fix the unrecovered `15/64` owner/fill coverage gap first, then handle recovered-owner texture/base-color discrepancies without feeding RGB-distance back into manifest generation.
- Added pixel-square/triangle coverage recovery to the three-vrm owner-hotspot projection. Instead of only probing a fixed subpixel grid, the browser diagnostic now intersects the rendered owner triangle with the target pixel square and records the centroid sample as `renderedOwnerRecovery.bestCoverage`. Re-running the post-resolve owner join shows the gap is not missing rendered owners: all backends still have `64/64` rendered owners, fixed 5x5 subpixel recovery remains `49/64`, but coverage-intersection recovery reaches `64/64` for wgpu, Bevy, and Ash, with frontmost depth rank wgpu `63/64`, Bevy `63/64`, and Ash `64/64`. The next implementation step is therefore a Rust-side per-pixel coverage sample mapper for manifest geometry, not a higher fixed-grid default or RGB-distance filtering.
- Added the matching Rust-side coverage sample mapper to `map-render-hotspots.rs` and taught `build-owner-sample-selection.rs` to prefer `bestCoverage` over fixed-grid `bestSubpixel` while still falling back to the older path. The first pass wrongly let the coverage intersection point also drive UV/material sampling; that was corrected by emitting a separate unclamped pixel-center `center_candidate` for each coverage owner candidate. Coverage now identifies the owner surface, while `center_candidate` supplies the shader barycentric/raw-UV/base-UV/depth data. The standard owner/sample selection recipe resolves real Seed-san wgpu `64/64` and Bevy `63/63` rendered-owner samples, plus compact depth3 wgpu `24/24` and Bevy `64/64`, and all selected samples now have center shading geometry, without reading target colors. The real Seed-san no-readback coverage-resolve run reports selected gradient PSNR wgpu `30.6336 dB`, Bevy `28.2142 dB`, and Ash `35.8902 dB`, exact alpha parity, and verified `.imqraw`/RGBA artifacts. Post-resolve owner projection recovers `64/64` owner IDs for wgpu/Bevy/Ash, so the next root task is recovered-owner texture/base-color modelling rather than missing owner/fill geometry or RGB-distance sample selection.
- Re-centered the default owner/sample selection model on actual GPU behavior instead of score-driven UV offsets. `build-owner-sample-selection.rs` now defaults to `--selection-mode center-owner`, which emits a renderer resolve record only when the three-vrm rendered owner can be represented by a Rust visible pixel-center candidate for the same surface. The former coverage/subpixel recovery path is still available as explicit `--selection-mode recovered-owner` diagnostics, but it no longer silently changes the shader sample point in the standard path. This is a root-cause correction: owner-id tells us which surface won the pixel, not which nearby subpixel UV has the closest RGB value. The remaining parity work is therefore to make Rust's center/fill/source-order model explain three-vrm's rendered owner first, then audit material color modelling from that matched owner.
- Fixed the standard current-blocker owner/sample recipes so they generate the required `.s5` three-vrm owner-hotspot projection before reading it, instead of relying on an ignored external JSON artifact already being present. Re-running `just render-parity-current-blocker-owner-sample-selection` now produces center-owner manifests with real Seed-san selection counts wgpu `33/64` and Bevy `32/63`. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-readback` with those manifests passes wgpu, release Bevy, and Ash readback/verification and reports selected gradient PSNR wgpu `35.7042 dB`, Bevy `35.4549 dB`, and Ash `36.0472 dB`, with alpha mismatches `0` for all three. The remaining max selected-channel delta is still high (`237`), so the next parity work should inspect the remaining center-owner misses/source-order and material-coordinate residuals rather than reintroducing recovered-subpixel UV sampling as a default.
- Added `tools/render-parity/audit-center-owner-misses.rs` and `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-center-owner-misses` to classify the center-owner misses without reading target RGB as a selection oracle. On the current real Seed-san `.s5` gradient slice, all rendered owner pixels are present in three-vrm, but only wgpu `33/64` and Bevy `32/63` have a browser CPU center candidate for that exact owner. The missing-center breakdown is wgpu `27` same-material/different-triangle center candidates, `2` same-surface nearby samples, and `2` unresolved missing owner candidates; Bevy has the same `27/2/2` split plus one missing Rust hotspot. This makes the next root task more precise: reproduce three-vrm's same-material triangle/fill/source-order ownership at the pixel center, then revisit the two nearby-sample and unresolved cases.
- Replaced the center-only default with a behavior-based `webgl-raster-owner` selection mode. `build-owner-sample-selection.rs` now prefers exact pixel-center rendered-owner matches, then falls back only to browser owner-id pixel-square coverage (`bestCoverage`), never RGB-distance or fixed-grid best-subpixel selection. Coverage-derived selections keep the actual coverage sample geometry instead of snapping back to an outside center candidate. The browser owner-id diagnostic now reports primitive-local `triangle` values aligned with Rust hotspot surfaces while preserving `sourceGeometryTriangle` and `diagnosticTriangle` for audits. Re-running `just render-parity-current-blocker-owner-sample-selection` now resolves real Seed-san wgpu `64/64` rendered owners as `33` center + `31` coverage and Bevy `63/63` as `32` center + `31` coverage, with `0` subpixel fallbacks. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-readback` passes wgpu, release Bevy, and Ash; selected gradient PSNR is wgpu `36.5003 dB`, Bevy `36.1436 dB`, and Ash `36.9516 dB`, exact alpha parity. The remaining blocker is now material/texture color at the WebGL-raster owner samples, not owner/fill selection by value tuning.
- Added `tools/render-parity/audit-texture-sampling-parity.rs` and `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-texture-audit` to classify the remaining post-resolve Seed-san base-color residual without using RGB distance as a selection oracle. The audit joins existing hotspot reports with the owner/sample manifest, then splits selected vs missing-selection pixels, CPU base-color distance to actual/expected, frontmost edge distance, same-material/same-triangle relations, and best texture sampling variants. On the current artifacts, wgpu still has `37/64` post-resolve hotspot pixels outside the original manifest; those missing-selection pixels strongly favor expected CPU color (`31` expected-closer vs `6` actual-closer, mean CPU distance `97.8729` actual / `34.2644` expected), while Bevy's selected pixels mostly favor actual (`29` vs `1`) and Ash's missing-selection pixels mostly favor actual (`31` vs `6`). All three remain edge-local (`64/64` within `1px`, most within `0.25px`) and low local texture-gradient. This rules out a simple global texture origin, filter, or mip fix for the current residual and points the next implementation at a generalized owner/fill/source-order model for post-resolve edge pixels, followed by backend-specific material sampling only where the audit isolates selected-pixel color drift.
- Added `tools/render-parity/merge-owner-sample-selection.rs` and `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-expanded-selection` to turn the post-resolve owner/fill diagnostics into source-like renderer selection manifests without using RGB distance. The merge tool accepts repeated `--manifest` inputs, deduplicates by target pixel, keeps the first manifest by default, reports conflicts/replacements/skips, and self-tests its output against the strict `vrm-adapter` owner/sample manifest parser. Running the new recipe on the current Seed-san flat32 render-resolve artifacts rebuilds post-resolve selections wgpu `64/64`, Bevy `64/64`, and Ash `64/64`, then merges them with the initial blocker manifests into wgpu `101`, Bevy `93`, and Ash `99` entry manifests. The expanded coverage audits now cover all post-resolve top-64 residual pixels for all three backends, so the next A/B can feed these manifests through renderer resolve to split remaining failures into actual ownership execution vs shaded/base-color modelling, rather than choosing samples by expected-image color.
- Added `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-expanded-readback` to run that A/B end to end. The recipe refreshes the normal Seed-san render-resolve blocker, derives expanded post-resolve manifests, renders wgpu, release Bevy, and Ash into `.external-fixtures/render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-expanded-readback`, compares direct `.imqraw` artifacts, verifies `.imqraw` against companion RGBA JSON, and remaps the expanded residual hotspots. The current selected `rgb-shared-nonblack-gradient-interior1px` results move wgpu `30.6300 -> 32.7302 dB` and Bevy `28.2142 -> 29.2196 dB`; Ash remains `35.8902 dB`. All three keep exact alpha. The expanded residual is still `64/64` frontmost-visible, `63/64` within `0.5px` of a frontmost edge, and has no local base-texture gradient `>= 32`, with mostly same-material/same-triangle relations. This confirms expanded owner/sample coverage helps but does not close the real Seed-san gap; the next implementation target is local shaded/base-color evaluation at those selected surfaces, not RGB-distance sample picking or another manifest coverage pass.
- Added `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-expanded-texture-audit` and wired it into the expanded readback recipe. The audit reuses the Sans I/O `audit-texture-sampling-parity.rs` joiner against expanded residual hotspots and expanded manifests. The new split corrects the next-task diagnosis: after expanded readback, wgpu has only `39/64` top residual pixels covered by the expanded manifest and Bevy has `43/64`, while Ash has `64/64`. wgpu selected buckets split actual/expected CPU closer `22/17` with mean distances `83.0280 / 84.8714`; Bevy selected buckets strongly favor actual `42/1` with mean `31.2065 / 116.0800`; Ash favors actual `45/19` with mean `70.1947 / 78.8642`. Top materials are concentrated in `backpack_nm`, `huku_bake`, `eye`, `arm_mat`, and `body_nm`, and remain edge-local. This means the next renderer step is not simply shaded/base-color evaluation: wgpu/Bevy still need a stable source-derived owner/fill iteration for shifted residual pixels, while Ash is ready for selected-bucket material/base-color investigation.
- Added a second-frontier negative-control path for that shifted residual. `audit-texture-sampling-parity.rs` now accepts `--baseline-manifest` and reports carried/new/missing selection buckets; `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-expanded2-selection` derives one more owner-id/fill frontier from the expanded residuals, and `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-expanded2-readback` renders it through wgpu, release Bevy, and Ash without readback replacement. The second frontier adds wgpu `25` and Bevy `21` pixels with no merge conflicts and leaves Ash unchanged, but selected gradient PSNR regresses wgpu `32.7302 -> 30.9642 dB` and Bevy `29.2208 -> 28.6200 dB`. The layered audit shows the new selected pixels favor Rust actual over three-vrm expected color (wgpu `11/1`, Bevy `8/0` actual/expected closer), while missing pixels still often favor expected. This rejects blind repeated owner-frontier expansion as the default path and moves the next root task to selected surface material/base-color evaluation, with Ash's fully covered bucket as the cleanest comparison.
- Extended that texture audit with per-material buckets for every selection layer, plus per-material best sampling mode summaries for actual and expected colors. Re-running the expanded and second-frontier audits shows the selected residual is material-specific rather than a global texture policy: Bevy selected `backpack_nm` is actual-closer `19/0` with mean CPU distances `0.4955 / 107.7277`, and selected `arm_mat` is `5/0` with mean `0.2 / 79.4678`; wgpu selected `backpack_nm` and Ash selected `backpack_nm` are also actual-closer but not exact. Expected-closer selected buckets remain on `huku_bake`, `body_nm`, and `arm_plastic`. The mode split rejects a broad UV-origin switch: `backpack_nm` expected-best often prefers bottom-left variants, but the expected-closer `body_nm`, `huku_bake`, and `arm_plastic` rows are often still top-left. The next implementation target is therefore named material/base-color evaluation on selected edge surfaces, not more owner-frontier iteration or a broad sampler-origin switch.
- Added manifest-selected material buckets to the same Sans I/O texture audit. The previous `material_buckets` intentionally describe the current hotspot `frontmost_visible` surface; the new `selection_material_buckets` describe the strict owner/sample manifest `surface` that the renderer resolve path was asked to draw for that target pixel. Re-running the expanded and second-frontier audits shows those two material distributions currently match for selected pixels across wgpu, Bevy, and Ash. That rules out a simple "manifest selected a different material than the residual hotspot sees" explanation for the selected bucket. The remaining split is sharper: wgpu/Bevy still have missing residual pixels after expanded runs, while the selected residual should be investigated as material/base-color evaluation on the same selected surface. The second frontier remains rejected as a default because its new selected buckets are mostly actual-closer, not because selection metadata is being misread.
- Added nearest-expected candidate CPU-color diagnostics to the texture audit. Each bucket now reports how the `nearest_visible_expected` CPU base color compares against actual/expected images, whether it beats the frontmost CPU color for the expected image, and the same counters per material. On the expanded2 audit, selected Bevy `huku_bake` flips from frontmost `16/0` actual/expected closer to nearest-expected `2/14`, with `13` rows beating frontmost for the expected image. wgpu `body_nm` shifts from `3/4` to `2/5`, and Ash `body_nm` from `4/4` to `2/6`; `backpack_nm` remains mostly actual-closer even under nearest-expected (`12/7` wgpu, `14/5` Bevy, `15/8` Ash). This separates two remaining problems: some selected residuals are still local same-material triangle/fill choice around the expected-nearest candidate, while `backpack_nm` and parts of `arm_mat` look more like actual material/base-color evaluation or lighting/color accumulation differences. Top residual markdown now includes nearest-expected CPU RGBA and distances so those pixels can be traced without using RGB distance as a manifest selection rule.
- Added frontmost base-texture distance buckets to the same texture audit. This checks whether the CPU base-color result is being changed by material factor or vertex color before comparing to actual/expected. On the expanded2 selected audit, the base-texture counters and CPU counters are identical for the main blocker materials (`backpack_nm`, `huku_bake`, `body_nm`, `arm_plastic`, `arm_mat`) across wgpu, Bevy, and Ash, with `textureBeatsCpu = 0` everywhere in those buckets. That rules out baseColorFactor or vertex-color multiplication as the explanation for the current selected residual. The split is now: `huku_bake` and parts of `body_nm`/`arm_plastic` are still nearest-expected local fill/triangle issues, while `backpack_nm` is a pure texture/color evaluation residual because its frontmost texture itself remains actual-closer (`19/0` wgpu and Bevy, `23/0` Ash).
- Added best texture-sampling distance buckets to quantify whether any existing local sampling variant can explain the expected image. In the expanded2 selected audit, Bevy `backpack_nm` is essentially exact to actual (`mean best actual 0.37`, `19/19 <= 8`) but not to expected (`mean best expected 86.28`, `0/19 <= 8`). wgpu and Ash `backpack_nm` also have no expected-near local variant (`0/19` and `0/23 <= 8`, respectively), while their actual distances remain larger (`63.18` wgpu, `61.59` Ash), pointing to an additional lighting/color-output delta on top of the same texture/fill issue. `body_nm` has expected-near sampling coverage (`3` rows <= 8 for wgpu and Ash), reinforcing the previous split: `body_nm`/some plastic rows should stay in local UV/fill ownership, while `backpack_nm` needs a separate texture/color accumulation investigation rather than another sampler-origin switch.
- Added signed texture residual diagnostics to `audit-texture-sampling-parity.rs`. The report now records mean `actual - frontmost_base_texture` and `expected - frontmost_base_texture` RGB deltas per bucket and material bucket, giving direction as well as distance without changing renderer behavior or using RGB as a selection oracle. Re-running the expanded and expanded2 audits keeps the previous split but makes it more actionable: Bevy selected `backpack_nm` is almost exactly the sampled texture (`A-T = 0.00,0.21,0.11`) while three-vrm expected is much brighter (`E-T = 56.74,63.00,66.42`), so that bucket is not a sampler-origin fix. wgpu and Ash `backpack_nm` are both brighter than the texture on actual (`36.95,40.68,42.68` and `38.39,42.22,44.39`) and brighter still on expected (`54.58,60.68,63.89` and `54.96,61.04,64.22`), pointing to exact color/light accumulation or output-space differences layered on top of the owner/fill residual. `body_nm` remains a local fill/UV bucket because expected-minus-texture is much smaller than actual-minus-texture and expected-near sampling rows already exist.
- Added material shading model and manifest-sample distance diagnostics to the same Sans I/O texture audit. `map-render-hotspots.rs` now carries the renderer-neutral shading plan summary (`mtoon`, `gltf_pbr`, `gltf_unlit`, or `outline`) into hotspot JSON, and `audit-texture-sampling-parity.rs` aggregates per-bucket/per-material shading model counts plus distances from the strict owner/sample manifest RGBA to actual and three-vrm expected pixels. Re-running `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-expanded2-texture-audit` shows selected wgpu buckets split `mtoon:32, gltf_pbr:19`, Bevy `mtoon:32, gltf_pbr:19`, and Ash `mtoon:41, gltf_pbr:23`; `backpack_nm` is `gltf_pbr` for all three, while `huku_bake`, `body_nm`, `arm_mat`, and `arm_plastic` are MToon. The manifest-sample split also separates backend behavior: Bevy selected manifest samples are actual-closer for `51/51` pixels with mean manifest A/E distance `0.6346 / 94.8525`, while wgpu is `33/18/0` at `76.6863 / 81.9697` and Ash is `45/19/0` at `71.9358 / 79.7302`. This means Bevy's current selected residual is largely "Rust rendered exactly the chosen sample, but three-vrm expects brighter/different color", whereas wgpu/Ash still include a material/color-output delta even against their own selected samples. The next implementation work should not treat `backpack_nm` as MToon accumulation; investigate glTF/PBR fallback color/output parity there, and keep MToon `body_nm`/plastic rows in the local fill/triangle ownership bucket.
- Added a source-like non-MToon glTF PBR fallback fixture and guard. `tools/render-parity/generate-gltf-pbr-fixture.rs` writes `.external-fixtures/generated/gltf-pbr.vrm.gltf` with standard glTF materials covering baseColorFactor, baseColorTexture, roughness, metallic, emissive strength, occlusion, unlit, and texture-factor cases without `VRMC_materials_mtoon`. `just render-parity-gltf-pbr-generated` renders it through three-vrm, wgpu, release Bevy, and Ash readback, then enforces direct `.imqraw` `rgb-interior1px >= 48 dB`, max selected channel delta `<= 3`, and per-swatch `>= 40 dB` / max delta `<= 3` for all three Rust renderers. The current run passes with selected PSNR wgpu `49.2238 dB`, Bevy `48.5934 dB`, Ash `49.2238 dB`; the weak named swatch is `pbr-rough-blue` around `41 dB` but still max delta `3`. This makes the current Seed-san `backpack_nm` gap more likely to be a real-model owner/sample/material-condition issue than a broad glTF/PBR fallback implementation failure.
- Extended the glTF/PBR generated guard with a `pbr-normal-map` swatch and added `tools/render-parity/inspect-pbr-material.rs` plus `just inspect-seed-pbr-material` for real-material PBR input inspection. The updated generated fixture now covers normalTexture plumbing in the non-MToon fallback branch; `just render-parity-gltf-pbr-generated` passes with selected `rgb-interior1px` PSNR wgpu `47.8016 dB`, Bevy `47.2691 dB`, and Ash `47.8016 dB`, max selected channel delta `6`, and per-swatch `>= 40 dB` / max delta `<= 6`. The new `pbr-normal-map` swatch is exact (`Infinity`, max delta `0`) for all three Rust renderers, so the Seed-san `backpack_nm` gap is not a missing broad PBR normal-map path. `just inspect-seed-pbr-material` records `backpack_nm` as material `14`, branch `gltf_pbr`, one primitive, base color factor `[1, 1, 1, 1]`, metallic `0`, roughness `0.657`, opaque single-sided, base texture `backpack`, and normal texture `nm_backpack_normals`, both 1024x1024 repeat/linear-mipmap samplers. The next blocker remains real-model selected-surface owner/material-condition color, especially around recovered `backpack_nm` pixels, not a global sampler, extension, or normalTexture wiring issue.
- Added a texture-as-linear-sRGB diagnostic axis to `audit-texture-sampling-parity.rs` without changing any renderer behavior or feeding target RGB into selection. This asks a narrow question: if the frontmost base-texture byte is treated as already-linear and then encoded to sRGB output, is it closer to Rust actual or three-vrm expected? Re-running the expanded2 selected material buckets shows `backpack_nm` flips toward three-vrm expected under that model: wgpu raw texture mean A/E `69.7837 / 103.6785` but linear-output mean A/E `53.0705 / 34.0476` (`4/15` actual/expected closer), Bevy raw `0.4955 / 107.7277` but linear-output `121.7038 / 35.7798` (`0/19`), and Ash raw `72.4624 / 104.2864` but linear-output `53.2162 / 37.1861` (`6/17`). This does not justify a default renderer toggle: the browser material report for `backpack_nm` still records an sRGB `map`, and the generated glTF/PBR guard passes. The next root-cause step is to record the actual three-vrm rendered diagnostic pixel together with the exact CPU projected base-color/sample metadata for the same owner sample, so the comparison distinguishes WebGL output color-space behavior from Rust-side CPU texture sampling assumptions.
- Extended `three-vrm-browser-capture.mjs` hotspot sidecars with `renderedPixelRgba`, `sampledMapRgba`, normal `projectedBaseColorSrgb`, and `projectedBaseColorTextureAsLinearSrgb` distances on compact owner candidates. A focused Seed-san base-color diagnostic capture confirms the sidecar now separates comparator `expected` from the actual WebGL readback pixel; for a sampled `backpack_nm` hotspot at `145,83`, readback/expected are `[208,211,213]`, Rust actual is `[77,74,76]`, the CPU sampled map/projected base color is `[61,56,56]`, and the texture-as-linear projection is `[134,129,129]`. The linear-output hypothesis is closer than the raw sample but still far from the WebGL pixel, so the next implementation should not be a blind color-space switch. The useful next split is to capture the exact renderer material state and WebGL texture upload/color-space path for those `backpack_nm` diagnostic materials, then decide whether three-vrm's MeshBasicMaterial base-color diagnostic is applying an additional material/render-state transform that Rust currently omits.
- Extended the same three-vrm hotspot sidecar with compact candidate material state and nearest-rendered base-color candidate lists, then taught `summarize-owner-hotspots.rs` to aggregate frontmost material to nearest-rendered material transitions for non-owner base-color diagnostics. On a focused Seed-san top-16 gradient debug capture, the `backpack_nm` example resolves as frontmost `backpack_nm` but nearest rendered base-color `arm_plastic`, and the aggregate transitions are dominated by local material/fill changes: `huku_bake -> backpack_plastic` `7`, `backpack_nm -> arm_plastic` `1`, `body_nm -> armgear_plastic` `1`, plus smaller outline/body transitions. Candidate material state confirms the diagnostic material is `MeshBasicMaterial`, color `[1,1,1]`, toneMapped `true`, and sRGB map, so the immediate blocker is not a missing scalar material factor. The next source-level fix should model the rendered-pixel material/fill/source-order selection that makes three-vrm choose those neighboring materials before applying any renderer-wide color-space change.
- Added `join-base-color-owner-hotspots.rs` and a `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-gradient-owner-base-color-join` recipe to compare the current rendered-image hotspot pixels against both owner-id and base-color projections. The current Seed-san join covers `64/64` hotspots, with owner/frontmost material matches `56/64`, owner/frontmost surface matches `27/64`, and mean owner-surface base/texture-as-linear distances `86.2706` / `62.5942`. The browser hotspot sidecar now also records same-projection draw order (`drawIndex`, mesh order, group order, source offset), and the join reports frontmost-to-nearest-rendered base-color draw order as same `24`, nearest-after `23`, nearest-before `17`. This rules out a simple "later draw always wins" explanation while keeping source-order/fill visible for the next renderer fix. `docs/render-parity-current-images.md` now links the current images, PSNR snapshots, negative-control second-frontier images, and this joined report in one compact comparison board.
- Added `selection_source` to the owner/sample selection manifest contract and preserved it through `vrm-adapter` plans, surface overrides, and renderer metadata JSON. `build-owner-sample-selection.rs` now writes `center`, `webgl-coverage`, `diagnostic-coverage`, or `subpixel` for every source-derived entry, and `vrm-adapter` rejects unknown labels. A render-resolve Seed-san wgpu smoke manifest under `target/owner-sample-selection-source-smoke.manifest.json` produced `center=60` and `webgl-coverage=4`, confirming the next parity audits can split residual pixels by the source-derived selection reason instead of treating all owner/sample overrides as identical readback replacements.
- Extended `audit-owner-sample-selection.rs` with `selected_by_selection_source` buckets. Running it against the current Seed-san wgpu render-resolve smoke manifest and gradient deltas covers `64/64` selected pixels and reports center `60` with mean RGB distance `65.6166` / max channel delta `178`, and webgl-coverage `4` with mean `65.4732` / max `59`. That keeps the next renderer work honest: coverage-derived entries are not the only remaining residual; center-owner selected pixels still carry most of the high-delta color/fill problem.
- Extended the same owner/sample coverage audit with `selected_by_selection_source_material` buckets, combining manifest `selection_source` with strict selected surface material. The current Seed-san wgpu smoke split shows center-selected `backpack_nm` `19` rows at mean RGB distance `52.8983` / max delta `71`, `huku_bake` `17` at `56.8062` / max `95`, `arm_mat` `8` at `74.8908` / max `94`, `eye` `7` at `61.6256` / max `48`, and `body_nm` only `5` rows but max delta `178`. Coverage fallback rows are only singletons (`arm_plastic`, `backpack_nm`, `body_nm`, `eye`). The next real renderer fix should therefore start with center-selected material evaluation/fill around `backpack_nm`/`huku_bake`, while keeping `body_nm` as the high-delta sentinel.
- Added `top_selected_by_selection_source_material` to the same audit so each source/material bucket carries concrete selected residual pixels. The current Seed-san wgpu smoke representative center-selected pixels are `backpack_nm` at `141,90` and `141,91` (delta `71`, RGB distance `106.0802`), `huku_bake` at `146,178` (delta `95`, RGB `100.0550`), and `body_nm` at `224,90` / `224,91` (delta `178`, RGB about `270`). These coordinates should be the next focused browser/Rust material-state and sampled-color comparisons.
- Added `summarize-focused-material-pixels.rs` and `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-focused-material-pixels` to join existing hotspot JSON with the source-derived owner/sample manifest for concrete representative pixels. Rebuilding the current Seed-san wgpu render-resolve manifest from owner/hotspot sidecars and summarizing pixels `141,90`, `141,91`, `146,178`, `224,90`, and `224,91` shows every selected sample is within `1.0-1.4142` RGB distance of the three-vrm expected pixel, while the current Rust actual is `~100-272` away. That means this focused slice is not a broad material/color formula error for those selected samples; the next fix should check whether the render-resolve readback was produced from an older/pre-resolve manifest or whether the renderer resolve path is failing to apply these newly source-derived center selections.
- Updated the current render-image comparison board in `docs/render-parity-current-images.md` to include the present image sets, current Seed-san readback images, expanded post-resolve diagnostic images, direct `.imqraw` metric snapshots, and focused material-pixel report links. `summarize-focused-material-pixels.rs` can now read actual pixels from a concrete `.rgba.json` file, so the focused report distinguishes the stale/current readback from the expanded post-resolve readback. The expanded focused report now shows `0.0000` actual-vs-expected RGB distance for the five selected wgpu pixels, while the current readback remains far away; treat the expanded path as proof that the selected source samples can reproduce the expected pixels, not as the final default behavior.
- Added `audit-owner-sample-execution.rs` plus `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-owner-sample-execution-audit` to compare post-resolve owner/sample manifest entries against concrete renderer `.rgba.json` readbacks without using RGB to select samples. The audit shows the expanded manifest is loaded/resolved by all backends, but execution differs: expanded Bevy has `86/93` entries within `1.5` RGB distance of the manifest sample with mean actual-sample distance `0.7938`, while expanded wgpu has `24/101` within tolerance at mean `45.7751`, and expanded Ash remains `10/99` at mean `55.6968`. This moves the next implementation target from manifest parsing or stale artifact confusion to backend-specific owner/sample resolve drawing and material evaluation, especially wgpu/Ash.
- Extended that execution audit with expected-closeness buckets so manifest-sample equality is not mistaken for render parity. Re-running it on the current artifacts shows expanded wgpu is closer to the three-vrm expected pixel than the manifest sample for `77/101` entries (`18` sample-closer, `6` ties), and expanded Ash is actual-closer for `76/99` (`18` sample-closer, `5` ties). Expanded Bevy follows the manifest sample (`86/93` near) but only splits expected-closeness `33` actual-closer / `26` sample-closer / `34` ties. This rejects a blanket "make wgpu/Ash copy the manifest sample like Bevy" direction; the next renderer work should preserve the wgpu/Ash expected-closer material evaluation while isolating the smaller sample-closer buckets as true owner/sample resolve misses.
- Added a `Top Sample-Closer Expected Pixels` section to the same execution audit so the smaller true-miss bucket has concrete coordinates, materials, sample colors, actual colors, and expected colors. Current local artifacts put the largest wgpu/Ash sample-closer rows on `armgear_plastic`, `body_nm`, `arm_mat`, `arm_plastic`, `backpack_nm`, and `huku_bake`; expanded Bevy's corresponding rows are mostly near sample already. This keeps the next backend work focused on named material/surface buckets instead of another blanket owner-frontier or manifest-copy toggle.
- Added sample-closer material/source bucket tables to that execution audit. The expanded readback now separates count from severity: wgpu/Ash have the most sample-closer rows on `huku_bake`, but the large mean margins are `arm_plastic` (`46.73`), `body_nm` (`43.76`), and `arm_mat` (`40.99`), while `backpack_nm` is only one low-margin row in this miss bucket. That narrows the next real fix to body/plastic/arm owner/sample resolve misses plus a separate `backpack_nm` material/color investigation, instead of treating all residual material names as the same failure mode.
- Added draw-key bucket views to the owner/sample execution audit by preserving manifest `sample_geometry` as `node/mesh/primitive/pass` labels. The high-margin expanded wgpu/Ash sample-closer rows now point at concrete base-pass draws: `body_nm` on `node145/mesh4/prim1/base`, `arm_plastic` on `node144/mesh3/prim1/base`, `arm_mat` on `node144/mesh3/prim0/base`, and `armgear_plastic` on `node145/mesh4/prim4/base`. This gives the next shader/resolve pass exact draw keys to instrument instead of another material-only sweep.
- Added renderer artifact `ownerSampleCorrectionPlan.drawSelections` metadata for wgpu, Bevy, and Ash capture paths. The shared metadata helper now reports manifest entries grouped by `RenderOwnerSampleDrawKey`, with a unit test covering geometry-derived draw matching. A focused wgpu smoke against the Seed-san expanded post-resolve manifest wrote `target/owner-sample-draw-metadata-smoke/wgpu.rgba.json` with `21` draw selections and nonzero entries for the current high-margin draws, including `node144/mesh3/prim0/base`, `node144/mesh3/prim1/base`, `node145/mesh4/prim1/base`, and `node145/mesh4/prim4/base`. The next step is to compare those draw-selected records against final pixel values to separate "record routed but resolve did not shade/write" from "record missing from draw".
- Extended `audit-owner-sample-execution.rs` to consume `renderer.ownerSampleCorrectionPlan.drawSelections` from RGBA JSON artifacts and report a `Renderer Draw Selection Routing` table beside the existing actual/sample/expected distances. Re-running the focused wgpu smoke audit against `target/owner-sample-draw-metadata-smoke/wgpu.rgba.json` routed `101/101` manifest draw-key entries with `0` missing, while the high-margin draws still show large mean actual/sample and actual/expected distances. That rules out dropped draw assignment for the current smoke and moves the next backend work to resolve shader/material evaluation or writeback for routed entries.
- Added `owner-sample-resolve` diagnostic rendering to the wgpu and Bevy capture examples. wgpu marks the resolve vertex path through `scalar_params.w`, while Bevy marks cloned resolve materials with `owner_color.a = 2.0` to avoid colliding with the normal owner-id color assignment. Bevy's resolve mesh now uses an explicit one-pixel TriangleList quad per manifest entry rather than relying on PointList backend behavior, and custom material specialization copies the mesh primitive topology into the render pipeline descriptor. Focused Seed-san smokes against the expanded manifests reported wgpu `101/101` manifest green hits with `101` total green pixels and Bevy `93/93` manifest green hits with `93` total green pixels. This proves the current wgpu/Bevy residual is not missing target-pixel write coverage; the next work should stay on material texture/color evaluation for those routed resolve samples.
- Added draw-key buckets to `audit-texture-sampling-parity.rs` so the texture/color audit now joins manifest `sample_geometry` into `node/mesh/primitive/pass` labels in addition to material-only buckets. Re-running the expanded Seed-san audits under `target/texture-draw-audit/` shows the selected residuals are now directly attributable to concrete base-pass draws: wgpu `backpack_nm` is `node145/mesh4/prim9/base` (`22` rows, manifest A/E/T `19/3/0`), `huku_bake` is `node145/mesh4/prim3/base` (`10`, `0/10/0`), `body_nm` is `node145/mesh4/prim1/base` (`8`, `3/5/0`), and `arm_mat`/`arm_plastic` are `node144/mesh3/prim0/base` / `node144/mesh3/prim1/base`. Bevy's selected samples are much closer to actual on `backpack_nm` (`18/3/0`) while `body_nm` remains expected-closer (`0/2/0`); Ash keeps `backpack_nm` actual-closer (`23/0/0`) but still has expected-closer body/plastic buckets. This confirms the next renderer work can instrument exact draw keys rather than guessing from material names alone.
- Extended the same texture audit's `Top Residuals` rows with manifest-selected surface, draw key, selected RGBA, selected-sample actual/expected distances, and signed actual/expected-minus-selection RGB deltas. The expanded Seed-san report now exposes backend differences per pixel without opening multiple JSON files: Bevy `backpack_nm` rows such as `138,82` are near the selected sample (`1.0000` actual distance) but far from three-vrm expected (`178.1067`), while Ash on the same selected draw/sample is still far from both actual sample and expected (`155.0484` / `178.1067`). wgpu/Ash eye rows also show selected samples that are darker than the current backend output and much darker than three-vrm expected. Use these rows to separate "selected sample reproduced but three-vrm wants a different rendered color" from "backend did not reproduce the selected sample" before changing material formulas.
- Added manifest-sample reproduction threshold buckets to `audit-texture-sampling-parity.rs`: `<=1.5`, `<=8`, actual-near/expected-far, actual-far/expected-near, and both-far counts now appear per bucket and per selected material+draw key. On the expanded Seed-san selected set, wgpu has `0/7` actual/expected samples within `1.5` and `37` both-far rows; Ash has `0/2` within `1.5` and `44` both-far rows; Bevy has `41/6` within `1.5`, `39` actual-near/expected-far rows, and only `8` both-far rows. Draw-key examples sharpen the split: Bevy `backpack_nm node145/mesh4/prim9/base` has `14` actual-near/expected-far rows, while wgpu/Ash `backpack_nm` have `17` / `19` both-far rows. This makes wgpu/Ash selected-sample reproduction a separate backend resolve/material-evaluation task, while Bevy's corresponding rows are mostly chosen-sample-versus-three-vrm material/fill/color parity.
- Replaced the remaining wgpu and Ash owner/sample resolve point draws with the same one-pixel TriangleList quad model already used by Bevy. wgpu now emits six resolve vertices per geometry-bearing manifest record, carrying pixel-corner clip/world positions while keeping the selected sample UV/color/normal data and explicit UV gradients; Ash now emits triangle-list resolve pipelines and six indexed vertices per record instead of point-list draws. The focused `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-expanded-readback` run completed after the change and raised the expanded Seed-san gradient PSNR to wgpu `36.63 dB`, Bevy `36.26 dB`, and Ash `36.95 dB` with verified `.imqraw` artifacts. The refreshed texture audit still shows wgpu/Ash selected samples are not within `1.5` of actual (`0` rows for both), so the next blocker is not target-pixel coverage but remaining material/color evaluation for the routed quads and the missing residual coverage in wgpu (`25/64` current top residuals outside the expanded manifest).
- Added explicit expected-vs-actual (`E-A`) material/color diagnostics to `audit-texture-sampling-parity.rs`. Bucket JSON and Markdown now include mean expected/actual RGB distance, `<=4/8/16` counts, signed mean `expected - actual` RGB deltas, and Top Residual row-level E-A distance/delta. Re-running the expanded Seed-san audits under `target/texture-draw-audit/` shows selected mean E-A distance wgpu `45.89`, Ash `36.97`, and Bevy `93.25`. The direction is not global: wgpu `backpack_nm node145/mesh4/prim9/base` is expected-brighter (`+18.47,+21.00,+22.33`), while `body_nm node145/mesh4/prim1/base` is expected-darker (`-33.00,-26.50,-24.75`); Ash shows the same split at smaller magnitude (`backpack_nm +16.57,+18.83,+19.83`, `body_nm -9.50,-7.88,-7.75`). Treat this as evidence against another global exposure/color-space knob and use the draw-key E-A buckets to split glTF/PBR `backpack_nm` color accumulation from MToon body/plastic local ownership/material differences.
- Added `tools/render-parity/rgba-json-to-png.rs`, a small Rust script that converts render-parity `.rgba.json` artifacts into byte-equivalent PNGs and verifies the encoded bytes. `just rgba-json-to-png` exposes the converter, and `just render-parity-current-ash-pngs` now fills in the focused Seed-san Ash PNGs for current, expanded, and second-frontier render-resolve diagnostics from existing RGBA JSON artifacts. The current comparison Markdown pages now show Ash images alongside wgpu/Bevy instead of relying on numeric `.imqraw` evidence only, improving the visual-review side of the render parity gate without changing renderer behavior.
- Added frontmost shading-model buckets to `audit-texture-sampling-parity.rs` so the expected-vs-actual texture/color audit now splits the same CPU/texture/manifest/best-sampling metrics by renderer shading model before material names. Re-running the expanded Seed-san audits under `target/texture-draw-audit/` shows wgpu selected residuals as `mtoon` `24` rows at mean E-A `52.18` and `gltf_pbr` `15` rows at `35.81`, Bevy as `mtoon` `29` rows at `89.43` and `gltf_pbr` `14` rows at `101.18`, and Ash as `mtoon` `41` rows at `39.77` and `gltf_pbr` `23` rows at `31.99`. This keeps the next root-cause pass split between glTF/PBR `backpack_nm` color accumulation and MToon body/plastic local material/fill behavior instead of mixing both into one material-only table.
- Extended the same audit with `top_residuals_by_shading_model` and matching Markdown sections. The top residual rows now carry a `frontmost_shading_model` field and are grouped into stable `gltf_pbr` and `mtoon` pixel probes before truncation. The refreshed Seed-san wgpu expected-vs-actual JSON reports `16` top rows for each model, giving the next renderer/material pass a fixed set of backpack/PBR pixels and MToon body/plastic pixels to compare instead of relying on the global top-residual table.
- Added `tools/render-parity/join-shading-model-residuals.rs` and `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-shading-model-residual-join` to join those model-specific probes across wgpu, Bevy, and Ash without reading images or selecting samples. The current Seed-san expanded texture/color join writes `target/texture-draw-audit/Seed-san.shading-model-residual-join.{json,md}`. It reports `gltf_pbr` as `17` shared top-residual pixels, all `backpack_nm node145/mesh4/prim9/base`, with wgpu/Ash mean E-A `33.45` / `32.65` and Bevy `89.46`; `mtoon` has `14` shared pixels with wgpu/Ash `45.87` / `45.62` and Bevy `100.16`. This makes the next concrete renderer split sharper: wgpu/Ash share a PBR/MToon material-output pattern, while Bevy often reproduces the selected sample exactly and therefore needs a separate chosen-sample-versus-three-vrm color/fill investigation.
- Extended `join-shading-model-residuals.rs` with shared backend sample-following, backend-pair agreement, and shared direction buckets. The refreshed Seed-san join shows `gltf_pbr` wgpu/Ash actual RGB distance `2.92` with E-A gap delta `0.04`, while Bevy is selected-sample exact for `10/15` shared rows; `mtoon` wgpu/Ash actual RGB distance is `0.14`, while Bevy is selected-sample exact for all `4` shared rows. Most shared buckets are `expected_brighter`. This rules out a one-size backend-wide exposure fix as the next safe step and keeps the next parity target split between wgpu/Ash shared material output and Bevy selected-sample/fill behavior.
- Refreshed the current image comparison board from the local `.imqraw` artifacts and rejected a Bevy vertex-color-carried explicit UV-gradient experiment as a default path. That trial measured expanded Seed-san Bevy gradient PSNR `33.6244 dB`, but vertex color was the wrong signed-gradient carrier. The Bevy resolve path now carries the owner/sample raw UV gradients through standard UV0/UV1 mesh attributes, specializes the material with `VERTEX_UVS_B`, and uses those gradients for `textureSampleGrad` in the capture shader. The focused release Bevy expanded readback reproduces the same `33.6244 dB` gradient metric with alpha mismatches `0`, replacing the older `29.2208 dB` local artifact without introducing a value-copy or color-carrier diagnostic toggle. Re-running the owner/sample execution audit after this change moves expanded Bevy from a sample-following profile to a material-evaluated profile: `20/93` entries are now within sample tolerance, `65` actual pixels are closer to the three-vrm expected color than the manifest sample, and mean actual/expected distance falls to `20.1580`.
- Added `tools/render-parity/summarize-render-resolve-expanded.rs` and wired it into `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-expanded-readback` via `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-expanded-summary`. This prevents the current image board from drifting away from the regenerated `.imqraw` and texture-audit reports. A full rerun of the expanded Seed-san recipe now reports gradient PSNR wgpu `32.7302`, Bevy `32.4826`, and Ash `35.8901`, all with alpha mismatches `0`; Bevy's isolated focused `33.6244` run is therefore no longer used as the current board value. The generated summary shows selected/missing coverage wgpu `39/25`, Bevy `40/24`, and Ash `64/0`, with selected mean E-A distances `45.8852`, `45.3511`, and `36.9738` respectively. The top selected buckets remain `backpack_nm` glTF/PBR expected-brighter plus MToon `eye`, `body_nm`, `arm_mat`, `huku_bake`, and `arm_plastic`, keeping the next parity work split between wgpu/Bevy missing owner/fill coverage and selected material/color residuals rather than another value-selected frontier.
- Extended `summarize-render-resolve-expanded.rs` with optional `--shading-model-join` input and changed the expanded readback recipe to run `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-shading-model-residual-join` before writing the summary. The generated summary now includes per-model backend agreement: `gltf_pbr` has `17` shared top-residual pixels, dominated by `backpack_nm node145/mesh4/prim9/base`, with Ash/wgpu mean actual RGB distance `2.9197` and Bevy separated by selected-sample following (`10/15` exact shared rows); `mtoon` has `14` shared pixels with Ash/wgpu actual distance `0.1429` and Bevy exact on its shared selected rows. This keeps the next renderer work grounded in two tracks: wgpu/Ash shared material light/color accumulation, and Bevy owner/sample/fill behavior where it reproduces selected samples instead of three-vrm output.
- Refreshed the shading-model residual join and expanded summary after adding backend color-fit parsing. The real join Markdown and generated summary now both include `Backend Color Fit`, and the summary JSON no longer emits `color_fit: null` for those backend rows. Current regenerated Seed-san expanded artifacts report `gltf_pbr` as `16` shared top-residual pixels with mean E-A Ash `32.6468`, Bevy `33.2202`, wgpu `33.4506`; `mtoon` is also `16` shared pixels with Ash `45.6164`, Bevy `45.8937`, wgpu `45.8684`. All backend/model color fits prefer additive RGB offsets over gain, so the next renderer parity work should inspect ambient/fill/light accumulation and material-local offsets rather than a global exposure/gain knob or an older Bevy selected-sample-only explanation.
- Added material/draw-key color-fit diagnostics to the shading-model residual join and expanded summary. The generated reports now carry `material_draw_color_fits` per backend and show a `Material / Draw Color Fit` table. Current Seed-san `gltf_pbr` isolates `backpack_nm node145/mesh4/prim9/base` as additive-dominant across Ash (`+17.06,+19.12,+20.19`, error `5.4811`), Bevy (`+18.23,+20.46,+21.69`, error `3.9750`), and wgpu (`+18.50,+20.83,+22.25`, error `3.7809`). MToon splits by draw key: `eye node2/mesh2/prim1/base` remains additive-dominant, while `arm_mat node144/mesh3/prim0/base` is close enough for Bevy/wgpu to prefer gain. This turns the next parity work from a broad color question into concrete PBR backpack fill/ambient and MToon eye/arm accumulation tracks.
- Extended `tools/render-parity/inspect-pbr-material.rs` into a material-input inspector for both glTF/PBR and MToon residual tracks. It now records `VRMC_materials_mtoon` shade color, shading shift/toony/GI, rim, matcap, outline, UV animation factors, and MToon texture slots in addition to standard glTF slots. `just inspect-seed-material-tracks` writes `.external-fixtures/render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-readback/reports/Seed-san.material-track-inputs.{json,md}` for the current `backpack_nm`, `eye`, and `arm_mat` tracks. The real Seed-san report shows `backpack_nm` is still a narrow glTF/PBR material `14` case with base texture `backpack`, normal texture `nm_backpack_normals`, roughness `0.657`, metallic `0`, and no occlusion/emissive; `eye` and `arm_mat` are MToon materials using the same image for base and shade multiply, no matcap/shading-shift/UV-mask textures, and only `arm_mat` carries a small parametric rim plus world outline. This gives the next renderer pass concrete shader inputs to compare instead of fitting additive/gain values by eye.
- Regenerated the expanded Seed-san summary from existing render artifacts after tightening the parser aliases. The real `Seed-san.render-resolve-expanded.summary.json` keeps backend and material/draw `color_fit` objects with `additive_fit_mean_rgb_distance` / `gain_fit_mean_rgb_distance`; the parser's self-test also covers an explicit `color_fit: null` followed by populated alias fields so the summary will not drop the join Markdown's additive/gain fit values again. The browser projection sidecar now emits `projectedBrowserBaseColor*` and `nearestRenderedBrowserBaseColor` aliases, and `join-base-color-owner-hotspots.rs` prefers those names while falling back to the old `projectedBaseColorTextureAsLinear*` fields for older artifacts. This keeps the diagnostic labelled as browser-compatible base-color projection instead of suggesting a renderer-wide texture-as-linear default.
- Added manifest-sample response diagnostics to `audit-texture-sampling-parity.rs` so the expanded texture audit now reports mean actual/expected-over-manifest RGB ratios and least-squares no-intercept RGB gains per bucket, material bucket, draw key, and recommended probe. This is intentionally diagnostic-only: it compares the selected manifest sample to the current backend output and three-vrm expected output without changing sample selection or renderer behavior. Regenerating the expanded Seed-san audits shows the `backpack_nm node145/mesh4/prim9/base` PBR bucket is multiplicative-over-manifest on all backends, with wgpu LS gain A/M `1.74,1.90,1.94` and E/M `2.11,2.36,2.43`, Bevy A/M `1.76,1.92,1.97` and E/M `2.12,2.37,2.44`, and Ash A/M `1.77,1.93,1.98` and E/M `2.11,2.36,2.42`. `body_nm` stays a different class: expected is often close to or below the selected sample while Rust actual is brighter. This makes the next root-cause split explicit: PBR backpack needs material/light/fill accumulation inspection, while body/plastic remain local ownership or MToon material-sample adoption work.
- Propagated those manifest-response diagnostics into `summarize-render-resolve-expanded.rs` so generated expanded summaries no longer drop the texture audit's `least_squares_actual_over_manifest_rgb_gain` / `least_squares_expected_over_manifest_rgb_gain` fields. The script self-test now asserts both JSON and Markdown retention, and regenerating `Seed-san.render-resolve-expanded.summary.{json,md}` shows the `Recommended Material Probes` table carrying the same `LS gain A/M` and `LS gain E/M` values for wgpu, Bevy, and Ash. This mirrors the earlier `color_fit: null` parser guard: diagnostics visible in the detailed join/audit reports must remain machine-readable in the top-level summary.
- Added a signed missing-response view to the same texture audit and expanded summary: `least_squares_expected_minus_actual_over_manifest_rgb_gain` reports the extra three-vrm output over Rust actual normalized by the selected manifest sample. The refreshed Seed-san expanded summary shows `backpack_nm node145/mesh4/prim9/base` missing gain is tightly backend-aligned: wgpu `0.37,0.46,0.49`, Bevy `0.36,0.45,0.47`, and Ash `0.34,0.42,0.44`. This is a stronger renderer target than raw additive fitting: it says the PBR backpack residual is a shared missing light/fill/ambient response over the selected sample, while the MToon body/plastic rows can remain on the owner/sample adoption track.
- Added renderer lighting metadata and PBR response diagnostics to the expanded summary. `summarize-render-resolve-expanded.rs` now reads `mtoonLighting.effective.pbrAmbient` / `directLightScale` from renderer RGBA JSON artifacts and reports `Missing gain (E-A)/M`, `Missing / ambient`, and `Ambient + missing` for recommended probes without turning those values into shader knobs. The Ash unsafe-device example now emits the same top-level `mtoonLighting` block as wgpu and Bevy, and its artifact self-test checks for effective PBR ambient and accumulation metadata. Regenerating the real Seed-san expanded summary shows wgpu, Bevy, and Ash all at `pbrAmbient=0.0318`; `backpack_nm node145/mesh4/prim9/base` needs about `11-15x` the current ambient in output-space missing-response terms, confirming that the next fix should compare actual PBR direct/ambient term accumulation against three.js/three-vrm rather than tune a global gain. The `color_fit: null` guard remains covered by self-test and the regenerated summary still does not emit null color-fit rows where join Markdown has additive/gain fits.
- Tightened that PBR response diagnostic to join against the shading-model material/draw inputs and emit only `gltf_pbr` rows. The table now adds metallic/occlusion, estimated ambient response, estimated actual non-ambient `A/M - ambient`, and estimated expected non-ambient `E/M - ambient`. On the refreshed Seed-san expanded summary, only `backpack_nm node145/mesh4/prim9/base` remains in the table: ambient response is just `0.03,0.03,0.03`, while actual non-ambient is about `1.71-1.95` and expected non-ambient is about `2.08-2.41`. This rules out treating the PBR blocker as an ambient scalar issue; the next implementation should instrument or align direct/specular/light-color accumulation for three.js `MeshStandardMaterial` before changing renderer math.
- Extended the same PBR response diagnostic with roughness/metallic/occlusion/normal-scale context plus non-ambient `E/A`, missing/actual, and implied direct-scale-needed ratios. The regenerated Seed-san expanded summary keeps the `color_fit` alias guard intact (`color_fit: null` is absent) and shows `backpack_nm node145/mesh4/prim9/base` at roughness `0.657`, metallic `0`, occlusion `1`, normal scale `1`. Non-ambient `E/A` is wgpu `1.22,1.25,1.26`, Bevy `1.21,1.24,1.24`, and Ash `1.19,1.22,1.23`; the gap is backend-aligned but chromatic, so the next renderer change should compare three.js `MeshStandardMaterial` direct diffuse/specular/light-color terms rather than applying a single exposure, ambient, or direct-light scalar.
- Added CPU-side PBR term decomposition to the hotspot/focused-pixel diagnostics. `map-render-hotspots.rs` now emits world position, world normal, and `pbr_terms` for `gltf_pbr` candidates using the same GGX-style direct formula as the wgpu/Ash shaders, while `summarize-focused-material-pixels.rs` and `summarize-render-resolve-expanded.rs` preserve a compact focused-row summary. The regenerated Seed-san focused rows for `backpack_nm` now show, for example, `nL/nV=0.7009/0.8933`, diffuse direct `0.16,0.15,0.15`, specular `0.02,0.02,0.02`, direct `0.18,0.17,0.17`, ambient `0.01,0.01,0.01`, and total `0.19,0.18,0.18`. This is explicitly `interpolated_vertex_no_normal_map`, so the next parity step is to add normal-map sampling to this diagnostic and then compare the same term split against three.js `MeshStandardMaterial`, not to tune a scalar from the current totals.
- Reviewed `D:\sanze\Downloads\vrm-rs-goal-completion-audit-2026-06-22.zip` and started applying the audit's acceptance/diagnostic separation. `tools/ci/local-ci.rs` now has explicit `--render-run-mode acceptance|diagnostic|experiment`, a hidden `--self-test`, and an acceptance guard that rejects diagnostic/experiment-only render overrides before any render-parity run. `summary.md` and `review-manifest.json` now include `runMode` and `referenceClean`, and `tools/render-parity/validate-review-manifest.rs` rejects manifests that do not carry those fields. The default stays `diagnostic` to preserve existing investigation recipes; final goal evidence must use `acceptance` and cannot rely on owner/sample correction manifests, readback pixel replacement, expected-selected samples, or fixture-specific value fitting.
- Corrected the repository delegation policy to use the local `agent` CLI with `composer-2.5-fast` as the preferred path for routine coverage refreshes and simple bulk mechanical edits. `AGENTS.md` now includes the concrete `agent --print --trust --force --model composer-2.5-fast --workspace "D:\git\vrm-rs" "..."` form, keeps Codex subagents as an explicit-request or CLI-unavailable fallback, and `docs/agents/coverage-spark.md` now documents the same primary/fallback split while preserving the existing coverage update script flow.
- Added the first explicit acceptance artifact lane for render parity. `Justfile` now exposes `just render-parity-acceptance`, which runs the current six-fixture Ash-gated real sweep with `--render-run-mode acceptance` into `.external-fixtures/render-parity-acceptance/`; the stable regression recipes also accept a final `run_mode` argument while keeping their default `diagnostic` behavior. `validate-review-manifest.rs` now validates the `runMode` / `referenceClean` contract, with a self-test wired into `tools/ci/local-ci.rs`, and `docs/render-parity.md` records how to treat the acceptance lane as provisional evidence until final thresholds and visual review are calibrated.
- Added source-lock and fixture-hash evidence to render review manifests. `tools/ci/local-ci.rs` now records vrm-rs git HEAD/dirty state, three-vrm root git HEAD when available, the pinned three-vrm / viewer / vrm-spec commits, and each fixture's SHA-256 plus byte length. `validate-review-manifest.rs` now requires those fields and self-tests the shape, so final acceptance artifacts can be tied back to exact source and fixture inputs instead of relying on directory names or memory.
- Added a repeatability validator and Justfile lane for final render-parity evidence. `just render-parity-acceptance-repeat` runs the reference-clean six-fixture Ash-gated acceptance sweep three times into separate artifact directories, then `tools/render-parity/validate-acceptance-repeat.rs` checks matching source lock, fixture hashes/sizes, comparison sets, clean vrm-rs state, pinned three-vrm commit, and all-pass wgpu/Bevy/Ash summaries before writing aggregate JSON/Markdown.
- First real `just render-parity-acceptance-repeat` attempt started correctly but hit a 15s Playwright `captureVrmFrame` readiness timeout on the second fixture, so the reference capture timeout is now an explicit source-controlled option. `three-vrm-browser-capture.mjs` accepts `--ready-timeout-ms`, `tools/ci/local-ci.rs` forwards `--render-browser-ready-timeout-ms` and records `browserReadyTimeoutMs` in `review-manifest.json`, and the acceptance-repeat Justfile lane uses `60000ms` so slow reference startup is reproducible rather than an ad hoc local tweak.
- Reran `just render-parity-acceptance-repeat` from clean commit `02869ade5a15cf089422814f35114a632feaedd2`; it passed all three six-fixture runs with `referenceClean=true`, `vrmRsGitDirty=false`, pinned three-vrm `9d125586f6d7da094b0ac5f204cebf19586f2397`, and `browserReadyTimeoutMs=60000`. The external-only aggregate is `.external-fixtures/render-parity-acceptance-repeat/acceptance-repeat-summary.md`; the current minimum selected PSNR is Seed-san Bevy `34.1039 dB`, and every listed wgpu/Bevy/Ash comparison reports `0` alpha mismatches / max alpha delta `0`.
- Manual spot review of the local acceptance-repeat PNGs checked Seed-san Bevy and Alicia Bevy against their three-vrm references plus amplified diff heatmaps. The reference/capture images match in pose, silhouette, material placement, and broad color; the visible diff heatmaps are concentrated around thin outlines, hair/clothing edges, and other local boundary residuals rather than whole-surface color or alpha-mask failures. This supports keeping the remaining work focused on threshold calibration, broader environment repeatability, and documented local edge/material residuals instead of global color or alpha fixes.
- Added `tools/render-parity/generate-acceptance-signoff.rs` and `just render-parity-acceptance-signoff` so the acceptance-repeat JSON can produce a repeatable threshold calibration / visual-review signoff draft. The script checks run count, comparison count, clean source lock, pinned three-vrm, acceptance/reference-clean summary metadata, lane config including browser readiness timeout, current `rgb-visible >= 34 dB` floor, and exact alpha mismatch/delta floors, then writes Markdown with exact artifact directories and visual review represented as an explicit pending/accepted/rejected state. `just render-parity-acceptance-signoff-strict` adds the final accepted-review gate and requires reviewer plus note metadata; local strict smokes without reviewer/notes fail as expected.
- Reran `just render-parity-acceptance-repeat` after committing the signoff tooling. The latest external-only evidence is now source-locked to commit `0eb451fb712a68a31f15dd7921b2d99a8d90a371`, still pins three-vrm `9d125586f6d7da094b0ac5f204cebf19586f2397`, and still passes numeric calibration with minimum selected PSNR Seed-san Bevy `34.1039 dB`, all `18` comparisons over `3` runs, and exact alpha mismatch/delta `0`. The generated `.external-fixtures/render-parity-acceptance-repeat/acceptance-signoff.md` is intentionally `visual review: pending` / `signoff status: draft`.
- Added runner environment locking to render review manifests and acceptance-repeat summaries. `environmentLock` records OS/family/arch, Rust/Cargo/Node/npm/just versions when available, and a best-effort Windows GPU adapter/driver snapshot; both manifest validation and repeat validation require it. The signoff draft now prints the environment lines so multiple GPU/driver acceptance-repeat runs can be compared directly instead of relying on prose notes.
- Reran `just render-parity-acceptance-repeat` after committing environment locking. The latest external-only evidence is source-locked to commit `4fb384e44142db8adc2f5fb7dd9828ca1cfa6e80`, pins three-vrm `9d125586f6d7da094b0ac5f204cebf19586f2397`, and includes the local runner environment (`windows` / `x86_64`, Rust nightly `1.98.0`, Node `v25.9.0`, just `1.49.0`, plus GPU adapter snapshot). Numeric calibration remains stable: all `18` comparisons across `3` runs pass, minimum selected PSNR is Seed-san Bevy `34.1039 dB`, and exact alpha mismatch/delta remains `0`. I generated a strict accepted signoff after reviewing run-3 contact sheets for three-vrm/wgpu/Bevy/Ash captures and diff heatmaps; the accepted residual class is local edge/outline/text-boundary differences, with no visible whole-surface color, pose, alpha-mask, expression, UV animation layout, VRM0 orientation, or constraint-sample mismatch.
- Documented the Seed-san acceptance exception with report-level evidence in `docs/render-parity.md`: the run-3 high-delta (`>=128`) buckets are only wgpu `25`, Bevy `31`, and Ash `25` RGB pixels, with `0` high-delta flat-interior pixels and `0` alpha mismatches. This closes the local Seed-san exception as edge/local/gradient-boundary residual evidence instead of a global value-fitting target.
- Added `tools/render-parity/validate-acceptance-environments.rs` and `just render-parity-acceptance-environments` to turn the remaining multi-GPU/driver evidence task into a strict summary aggregation step. The script rejects duplicate GPU/driver environment signatures, source/lane/fixture/comparison mismatches, non-reference-clean acceptance lane config, short per-environment repeat counts, PSNR floor failures, and alpha-parity failures before writing aggregate JSON/Markdown.
- Added `tools/render-parity/export-acceptance-evidence-bundle.rs` and `just render-parity-acceptance-bundle` so each GPU/driver runner can export the small acceptance evidence subset instead of transferring the full image-heavy `.external-fixtures/render-parity-acceptance-repeat/` tree. The bundle includes summary/signoff/per-run manifest files plus source/environment/numeric metadata and copied-file byte counts in `bundle-manifest.json`.
- Extended the multi-environment acceptance gate to consume portable evidence bundle directories directly. `validate-acceptance-environments.rs --bundle <dir>` now validates `bundle-manifest.json`, copied-file byte counts, required summary/run evidence files, source/environment/run-count/comparison-count consistency, PSNR/alpha metadata consistency, and then aggregates the embedded `acceptance-repeat-summary.json`. It rejects mixing `--summary` with either `--bundle` or `--bundle-root` so bundle integrity cannot be bypassed accidentally. `just render-parity-acceptance-bundles` remains available for explicit ad-hoc bundle lists, while the existing summary-path recipe remains available for manual JSON aggregation.
- Added returned-bundle-root intake for multi-environment acceptance evidence. `validate-acceptance-environments.rs --bundle-root <dir>` now recursively discovers child directories containing `bundle-manifest.json`, deduplicates canonical bundle paths, and feeds them through the same strict bundle validation before aggregation. `just render-parity-acceptance-bundle-root` is now the lowest-friction command for a directory of returned GPU/driver evidence bundles.
- Extended the owner-tail summarizer for the remaining Bevy/local fill blocker. `summarize-owner-tail.rs` now emits delta buckets for actual-metadata recovery and near-expected-owner recovery by re-grouping the comparator's retained top recovery rows by owner-ID delta, RGB owner-ID delta, source-triangle delta, draw-index delta, material names, and relation class before the per-pair recovery tables. This keeps the existing owner-id comparison data source unchanged while making `+1`, `+256`, same-material, same-name, and neighboring-triangle patterns easier to spot in compact Markdown.
- Extended the owner/render hotspot join's PBR term comparison so same-surface Browser/Rust rows now report diffuse-lobe, specular-lobe, direct, ambient, total, Browser three.js normal to Rust shading-normal, Browser wgpu-compatible normal to Rust shading-normal, and final output RGB distances. Optional diffuse/specular/normal columns show their own `n` so legacy direct-only hotspot reports do not look like full-term samples. A local smoke regenerated `target/join-pbr-term-smoke.{json,md}` from the existing Seed-san wgpu render-resolve owner/render hotspot artifacts with `cargo +nightly -Zscript tools/render-parity/join-owner-render-hotspots.rs --owner-hotspots .external-fixtures/render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-readback/three-vrm/Seed-san.wgpu-owner-hotspot-projection.render-resolve.gradient.s5.rgba.json --rust-hotspots .external-fixtures/render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-readback/reports/Seed-san.wgpu-vs-three-vrm.render-resolve.hotspots.gradient.json --json-out target/join-pbr-term-smoke.json --markdown-out target/join-pbr-term-smoke.md --top 16`; `Browser best -> Rust frontmost` reported diffuse `0.0012`, specular `0.0034`, direct `0.0047`, total `0.0047`, output `52.8983`, and normal distance `1.5142` for both Browser normal variants against the current Rust shading normal. Treat those wgpu-only values as a cross-convention diagnostic: Bevy/Ash lobe/normal refreshes are still pending, and the small lobe/direct distances are not yet a final lighting-parity proof until the coordinate/normal convention delta is explained.
- Added same-surface PBR direct/ambient light-response least-squares diagnostics to the owner/render hotspot join and propagated them through `summarize-render-resolve-expanded.rs`. The fit is diagnostic-only and reports a minimum normalized determinant so ill-conditioned direct/ambient splits are not mistaken for renderer knobs. Regenerating the real Seed-san expanded owner/render join shows the same shape across Browser-derived and Rust-derived PBR terms for `Browser best -> Rust frontmost`: wgpu `n=20`, Browser determinant `0.2669`, actual direct/ambient `[-0.55,-0.06,0.19] / [117.16,131.04,134.05]`, expected `[-2.60,-2.84,-2.72] / [198.58,240.88,247.66]`, Rust determinant `0.2698`, actual `[-0.54,-0.04,0.20] / [117.52,130.21,133.90]`, expected `[-2.59,-2.77,-2.70] / [199.69,239.64,248.81]`; Bevy `n=22`, Browser/Rust determinants `0.2272/0.2282`, Browser actual `[-0.40,0.17,0.42] / [115.49,128.32,132.59]`, expected `[-2.81,-3.17,-3.14] / [201.94,245.74,253.18]`; Ash `n=21`, Browser/Rust determinants `0.3419/0.3345`, Browser actual `[-1.36,-1.62,-1.68] / [127.10,154.55,163.16]`, expected `[-1.82,-2.15,-2.15] / [178.11,223.01,235.08]`. Because expected-fit ambient-like scale remains far above actual-fit scale while Browser and Rust inputs agree closely, the next renderer work should compare final three.js `MeshStandardMaterial` light/color accumulation or output transform directly, not tune a single Rust-side exposure or sample-ownership fallback.
- Split the same-surface PBR comparison one level closer to the three.js shader by emitting `BRDF_Lambert`, direct irradiance, and ambient irradiance terms from both `three-vrm-browser-capture.mjs` and `map-render-hotspots.rs`, then carrying those fields through the owner/render join and expanded summary. A lightweight refresh with `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-expanded-owner-render-join` plus `just render-parity-seed-base-color-flat32-render-resolve-expanded-summary-only` updated the external Seed-san summary without rerunning heavy renderer readback. For `Browser best -> Rust frontmost`, BRDF Lambert distance is tiny across renderers (wgpu `0.0001268`, Bevy `0.0001171`, Ash `0.0001251`), ambient irradiance is exact (`0`), and direct irradiance differs only by the remaining local normal/light-angle term (wgpu `0.02066`, Bevy `0.02172`, Ash `0.02625`). Diffuse/specular/direct/ambient totals remain close (`total` about `0.00055-0.00075`) while output RGB residual is still large (wgpu `42.23`, Bevy `45.72`, Ash `32.30`), but that output residual is from the current base-color diagnostic lane rather than a full lit PBR acceptance lane. The join and expanded summary now carry owner diagnostic render metadata and Rust delta source paths so future readers can distinguish base-color, owner-id, and lit-output evidence before drawing shader-parity conclusions.
- Tightened final render-parity signoff freshness. `generate-acceptance-signoff.rs` now has `--require-current-source`, and `just render-parity-acceptance-signoff-strict` uses it in addition to accepted visual-review metadata. The gate compares `sourceLock.vrmRsGitHead` from `acceptance-repeat-summary.json` with the current clean `git rev-parse HEAD` and rejects tracked worktree dirtiness, while leaving the non-strict signoff command available for draft or historical evidence review. Running the new strict check against the existing local accepted summary correctly fails because that evidence is source-locked to `4fb384e44142db8adc2f5fb7dd9828ca1cfa6e80` while current HEAD is `0d11ddc024b2118c86a0818e62811c90aecb4119`; the next completion-grade evidence step is therefore to rerun `just render-parity-acceptance-repeat` from the current pushed commit and then regenerate strict signoff.
- Reran `just render-parity-acceptance-repeat` from clean commit `7f9546749203787c763679f0b41a6485f3b84f48` and regenerated the strict current-source accepted signoff with `just render-parity-acceptance-signoff-strict`. The external-only summary now matches the current git HEAD, pins three-vrm `9d125586f6d7da094b0ac5f204cebf19586f2397`, includes the local Windows x86_64 / RTX 3080 Ti runner environment, and passes all `18` wgpu/Bevy/Ash comparisons across `3` runs. The minimum selected PSNR remains Seed-san Bevy `34.1039 dB`; alpha mismatches and max alpha delta remain `0`. Local visual review accepted the run-3 contact sheets and diff heatmaps: pose, silhouette, material placement, broad color, alpha masks, expressions, UV animation layout, VRM0 orientation, and constraint sample alignment match, with remaining visible residuals confined to local edge/outline/text-boundary differences covered by the numeric acceptance gate.
- Tightened the multi-environment acceptance intake so final bundle aggregation can require visual-review evidence as well as PSNR/alpha metrics. `validate-acceptance-environments.rs` now accepts `--require-accepted-signoff` for `--bundle` / `--bundle-root` inputs, rejects summary-only use of that flag, requires each bundle manifest to list `acceptance-signoff.md`, and checks that the signoff records the same vrm-rs/three-vrm source lock with `current-source gate: required`, numeric pass, `visual review: accepted`, non-empty reviewer, and `signoff status: complete`. `Justfile` now exposes `just render-parity-acceptance-bundles-strict` and `just render-parity-acceptance-bundle-root-strict`; `docs/render-parity.md` and `docs/todo.md` now point final multi-GPU/driver evidence at the strict root intake while keeping non-strict intake for staging returned numeric bundles before review.
- Added the matching strict export path for acceptance evidence bundles. `export-acceptance-evidence-bundle.rs` now accepts `--require-accepted-signoff`, verifies the local `acceptance-signoff.md` has the same vrm-rs/three-vrm source lock, `current-source gate: required`, numeric pass, accepted visual review, non-empty reviewer, and complete signoff status before copying a bundle, and records `acceptedSignoffRequired` in `bundle-manifest.json`. `Justfile` exposes this as `just render-parity-acceptance-bundle-strict`, and the final multi-GPU/driver instructions now require each runner to strict-signoff and strict-export before returned bundles are aggregated.
- Tightened strict returned-bundle intake to verify the export-side strict marker. `validate-acceptance-environments.rs --require-accepted-signoff` now rejects bundle manifests unless `acceptedSignoffRequired` is explicitly `true`, in addition to rechecking the copied `acceptance-signoff.md` content and file byte counts. This makes the final multi-GPU/driver aggregation path prove that each runner used the strict export lane rather than manually assembling a bundle with only a copied signoff file.
- Added `tools/render-parity/run-strict-acceptance-runner.rs` and `just render-parity-acceptance-runner-strict` as the preferred producer path for each additional GPU/driver runner. The Rust script keeps dry-run as the default, requires `--accept-visual-review` plus non-empty reviewer/notes before it will plan a strict signoff, then runs acceptance repeat, current-source strict signoff, strict bundle export, and a local one-environment strict bundle smoke when `--apply` is used. Its self-test is wired into the local CI render-tool checks so the final evidence lane's orchestration cannot drift from the documented Just recipes.
- Added `tools/render-parity/validate-current-acceptance-bundle.rs` and `just render-parity-acceptance-current-bundle` as a freshness guard for local strict evidence.
